And the trapped defenders followed.

## 9. Bring Them Home

The Coalition charged.

Joel moved first.

Not forward.

Sideways.

He stepped into the narrow gap between two support pillars and fired once.

The long-barrelled weapon cracked through the corridor.

The lead Coalition soldier dropped.

Joel fired again.

A second soldier spun into the wall.

“Rook,” Joel said.

Commander Halden Rook stared at him.

“What?”

“Your wounded go in the centre.”

Another shot.

Another Coalition rifle went silent.

“Dax takes rear security. Your best five shooters stay with me.”

Rook looked at the crimson-eyed stranger giving orders inside his station.

Joel turned toward him.

“Commander.”

Something in his voice made Rook react before thinking.

“You heard him!” Rook shouted. “Wounded centre! Five forward!”

The old soldiers moved.

Anton laughed.

“There he is.”

Joel glanced at him.

“Anton.”

“Yes?”

“Left corridor.”

Anton looked toward the junction.

Coalition troops were moving behind the smoke.

“How many?”

“Enough to entertain you.”

Anton grinned.

“Excellent.”

He ran.

Sera’s defenders watched him disappear around the corner.

Then the firing started.

Deep cracks.

Metal impacts.

Shouting.

A scream.

Then Anton’s laughter.

Dax looked toward Joel.

“Your brother is insane.”

Joel fired twice.

“Possibly.”

“You say that very calmly.”

“I have had years to adjust.”

---

Anton reached the left corridor before the Coalition squad had finished forming.

Seven soldiers.

Heavy armour.

One shield carrier.

Two breachers.

The shield carrier saw him first.

“CONTACT!”

Anton fired.

The heavy pistol struck the shield.

The impact threw the soldier backward.

Anton laughed.

“Oh, that is good armour.”

The breachers opened fire.

Anton moved behind a support beam.

Rounds tore chunks from the wall.

He looked down at his pistol.

“One shot?”

Another burst hit the beam.

Anton smiled.

“Two.”

He stepped out.

The first shot broke the shield carrier’s knee.

The second struck the breacher behind him.

Anton crossed the distance before the others could adjust.

The nearest soldier swung his rifle.

Anton caught it with one hand.

The soldier tried to pull away.

Anton did not move.

The man looked at him.

Anton’s crimson eyes burned brightly now.

“Mine.”

He pulled.

The Coalition soldier came with the rifle.

Anton drove his shoulder into the man’s chest.

Armour plates cracked.

The soldier hit the floor.

Another attacker came from the side with a combat blade.

Anton turned.

Caught the wrist.

Twisted.

The blade fell.

Then Anton threw the soldier into the wall.

Hard.

Too hard.

The armour left a dent.

The remaining troops hesitated.

Anton looked at them.

“You were doing better when you were shooting.”

They fired.

Anton charged directly into the gunfire.

---

Joel heard the weapons behind him.

He did not look back.

“Advance.”

Rook’s soldiers pushed through the junction.

Joel walked at the front.

Not running.

Walking.

His pistol moved from target to target.

He did not shoot centre mass when he could avoid it.

Shoulder.

Weapon arm.

Knee.

Helmet sensor.

Rifle power pack.

He was breaking the Coalition formation rather than killing everyone in front of him.

Rook noticed.

“You could kill them.”

“Yes.”

“Why not?”

Joel fired.

A Coalition rifle shattered.

The soldier dropped behind cover.

“Because they may surrender.”

Rook stared at him.

Coalition rounds struck the wall.

Joel moved behind cover.

Rook followed.

“You think they will surrender?”

“Some.”

“After seven years?”

Joel looked at him.

“Not everyone fighting for a bad commander is a bad person.”

Rook said nothing.

That sounded less like a mercenary opinion.

It sounded like doctrine.

Joel checked the corridor.

“Ready?”

Rook raised his rifle.

“Yes.”

Joel stepped out.

“Move.”

The defenders surged forward.

---

Dax held the rear.

Three wounded soldiers moved ahead of him while two old garrison troops supported them.

Dax checked his ammunition.

Low.

Very low.

A warning flashed across his weapon.

“Rear contact!”

Coalition soldiers appeared behind them.

Dax turned.

Five.

Then eight.

“Keep moving!” he shouted.

He opened fire.

One enemy dropped.

Another took cover.

The rest continued.

Dax emptied the rifle.

“Damn.”

He threw it aside and drew his pistol.

The Coalition troops advanced.

Then someone dropped from an overhead maintenance opening.

Anton landed between them.

Dax stared.

Anton looked over his shoulder.

“You seem busy.”

“How did you get there?”

“Shortcut.”

The Coalition soldiers fired.

Anton raised the heavy pistol.

Three shots.

Three impacts.

Dax looked toward the open maintenance hatch.

“You climbed through that?”

“Yes.”

“In the middle of a firefight?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

Anton glanced at him.

“Because walking around would take longer.”

Dax stared.

Anton fired again.

The nearest Coalition soldier fell.

“Move your wounded.”

Dax grabbed one injured man.

“You giving me orders now?”

Anton laughed.

“Apparently it runs in the family.”

That word stayed with Dax.

Family.

Not unit.

Not company.

Family.

He remembered the crimson eyes.

The strange weapons.

The way Joel had taken control without raising his voice.

Dax knew enough mercenary stories.

Some names travelled farther than fleets.

He looked at Anton.

“Hjertstedt.”

Anton fired down the corridor.

“You already knew that.”

“No.”

Dax’s metal hand tightened around the wounded soldier’s armour.

“I am beginning to understand it.”

Anton smiled.

“Good.”

---

On Kestrel’s command deck, Sera watched the rescue unfold through damaged security cameras.

Half the feeds were dead.

The others were filled with smoke and broken images.

But she could see enough.

Joel stood at the front of the formation.

Her people were following him.

Not because he had demanded it.

Because every order worked.

Anton appeared and disappeared across separate camera feeds.

Corridor Twelve.

Maintenance Four.

Rear junction.

Then suddenly beside Dax.

Her engineer stared.

“How is he moving that quickly?”

“I do not know.”

“They are pushing through eighty boarders.”

Sera watched Joel direct Rook’s soldiers around another enemy position.

“He is not pushing through them.”

“What?”

“He is taking them apart.”

The engineer looked closer.

Sera was right.

Joel was not fighting the enemy line.

He was breaking its structure.

Destroying weapons.

Separating squads.

Forcing some to retreat.

Leaving surrender routes.

Anton was different.

Anton was the hammer.

Where resistance became hard, Anton appeared.

Then it stopped being hard.

Sera opened the channel.

“Joel.”

His voice returned immediately.

“Captain.”

“How far?”

“Three corridors.”

“Coalition reinforcements are moving toward your position.”

“How many?”

“Forty. Perhaps fifty.”

Anton’s voice came over the same channel.

“Good.”

Sera closed her eyes.

“Anton.”

“Yes, Captain?”

“Do not encourage them.”

“I did not transmit to them.”

Joel laughed quietly.

Even under fire.

Sera looked at the outer tactical display.

The sixty-one-ship Coalition fleet was continuing to move into position around Kestrel.

The station’s broken guns followed them badly.

Targeting solutions formed.

Then disappeared.

The enemy knew Kestrel was weak.

They were getting closer.

“Sera,” Joel said.

It was the first time he had used her name without title.

She noticed immediately.

“What?”

“Once Rook is secure, start pulling your people away from the outer docking rings.”

“Why?”

“The fleet will attack.”

“I know.”

“No.”

His voice changed.

Serious.

“You know they might attack.”

Joel fired in the background.

“We know they will.”

Sera looked at the sixty-one ships.

“How?”

“Because their admiral has already committed too much force to withdraw without taking the station.”

Anton came over the channel.

“And because he insulted her.”

Joel sighed.

“That is not strategically relevant.”

“It is to me.”

Sera nearly smiled.

“Bring Rook back.”

Joel answered:

“We will.”

---

Maintenance Junction Nine disappeared behind them.

Rook’s defenders crossed the final corridor toward the command-lift sector.

Coalition troops followed.

Then stopped.

Joel stopped too.

The defenders looked back.

Twenty-eight Coalition soldiers stood beyond the smoke.

Weapons ready.

Joel raised one hand.

Rook’s soldiers held fire.

The Coalition lieutenant stared at him.

“Move aside.”

Joel looked at the officer’s battered armour.

“You know the station is lost to your commander.”

“It belongs to the Coalition.”

“The Coalition abandoned it.”

“We have orders.”

Joel nodded.

“I understand.”

The lieutenant hesitated.

Joel lowered his pistol slightly.

“You have wounded.”

The Coalition soldiers looked at one another.

Joel continued.

“So do we.”

Anton reached the group.

He looked disappointed.

“No fighting?”

Joel ignored him.

“To the Coalition troops in this corridor,” Joel said. “Drop your weapons. Your wounded receive treatment. You will not be executed. You will be held until Captain Venn decides your status.”

Rook looked sharply at him.

Anton stopped smiling.

This was no longer a traveller speaking.

This was something older.

Something formal.

The Coalition lieutenant looked at Joel’s crimson eyes.

“On whose authority?”

Joel stared at him.

For a moment, the corridor was completely silent.

Then Joel smiled.

“Today?”

He looked toward Rook.

“Captain Sera Venn’s.”

The lieutenant slowly placed his rifle on the floor.

One soldier followed.

Then another.

Weapons began falling.

Rook stared.

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“You enjoyed that.”

“A little.”

“You should have used the full title.”

“We are still undercover.”

Anton looked around at the surrendered soldiers.

“Poorly.”

Joel laughed.

Rook shook his head.

“Move.”

They reached the command lift with every surviving Kestrel defender.

Nineteen walking.

Four wounded.

None left behind.

Dax entered last.

The lift doors closed.

For the first time in nearly twenty minutes, there was no gunfire.

Rook looked at Anton.

Then Joel.

“You said you would bring us back.”

Joel nodded.

“We did.”

Anton reached inside his coat and touched the old bottle of liquor.

Then he smiled.

“And now we have sixty-one ships to deal with.”

The lift began rising.

Above them waited Sera.

Outside waited a fleet.

Kestrel’s guns were broken.

Its missiles were almost gone.

Its defenders were exhausted.

But Rook and his people were alive.

And Joel von Hjertstedt was no longer smiling like a passenger.

He was smiling like a commander who had finally decided the situation deserved his attention.

## 10. The Voice of Command

The command lift opened.

Commander Halden Rook stepped out first.

His armour was black with smoke. Blood covered one sleeve. Behind him came Dax, the surviving defenders, and the wounded.

Captain Sera Venn left the command chair immediately.

“Casualties?”

“Four wounded badly,” Rook said. “Everyone else made it.”

Sera looked past him.

Joel and Anton entered last.

Anton was still smiling.

His coat was torn across one shoulder. His heavy pistol remained in his hand. Crimson burned faintly in his eyes.

Joel looked almost untouched.

That somehow bothered Sera more.

“You actually brought all of them back,” she said.

Anton looked toward Joel.

“Technically, some walked themselves.”

Joel ignored him.

Then Kestrel shook.

Hard.

Everyone grabbed something.

A deep impact travelled through the asteroid.

The main tactical display flashed.

**OUTER ARMOUR HIT.**

Another impact followed.

Then another.

Sera spun toward the sensor station.

“Report!”

The officer stared at his display.

“Coalition fleet has opened fire!”

The main screen changed.

Sixty-one hostile ships surrounded Kestrel.

Missile trails crossed the dark.

Old Coalition destroyers were firing from Hammer Range. Cruisers remained farther back, feeding targeting information forward. Frigates moved toward the station’s blind zones.

Kestrel’s ancient point-defence guns fired.

Three bursts.

Then one gun stopped.

A second began tracking the wrong target.

The third fired into empty space.

The engineer swore.

“Defence network is losing synchronization!”

Another impact struck.

Lights flickered.

Rook stepped toward the tactical display.

“They know our guns are broken.”

“They knew before they arrived,” Sera said.

The sensor officer shouted.

“More boarding craft launching!”

Red contacts separated from the enemy transports.

Dozens.

Then more.

Sera stared.

“How many?”

“Forty-seven pods and assault cutters.”

Anton walked beside the display.

“That is excessive.”

Joel looked at him.

“We killed many of their first wave.”

“That seems like their problem.”

“It has become ours.”

The internal station map flashed.

New alarms appeared across Dock Ring Four.

Then Ring Six.

Then one of the industrial docking collars.

Rook cursed.

“They are spreading us out.”

Sera activated the station channel.

“All defenders, abandon exposed outer corridors. Fall back toward prepared junctions. Do not get trapped.”

Reports came back immediately.

“Dock Four under pressure!”

“Cutters on the maintenance hull!”

“Breach detected on Industrial Level Three!”

“Enemy boarding force entering Ring Six!”

Another impact shook the station.

This time the main lights went out completely.

Emergency lights came on.

Red.

The command room looked like the inside of a dying warship.

Anton’s eyes brightened.

Sera saw it.

“You are enjoying this again.”

“A little.”

Dax looked at him.

“We are being boarded by hundreds of soldiers.”

“Yes.”

“Our station is being bombarded.”

“Yes.”

“Our defences barely work.”

“Yes.”

Anton smiled.

“You see why it is interesting.”

Dax stared at him.

“I am going to hit you eventually.”

“Take a number.”

Joel was no longer listening.

He stood at the tactical display.

Watching.

Calculating.

The Coalition formation had changed.

Frigates moved closer.

Boarding transports were advancing behind them.

The cruisers remained protected near the gate.

Joel’s expression hardened.

“They have stopped testing.”

Sera came beside him.

“I noticed.”

“No.”

Joel pointed toward the fleet.

“Before, they wanted the station intact.”

“And now?”

“They still want it intact.”

Another salvo struck Kestrel.

Joel watched damage reports appear.

“But they have decided they can rebuild what they break.”

Sera understood.

“That means they will keep pushing.”

“Yes.”

“How long?”

Rook answered.

“With this many boarders?”

He looked at the internal map.

“Hours if we are lucky.”

The engineer shook her head.

“Reactor cooling will fail before that if bombardment continues.”

Sera looked at Joel.

“You said you had solutions.”

Joel looked back.

“I said several.”

Anton leaned toward him.

“I vote for the entertaining one.”

“No.”

“You do not even know which one I mean.”

“I do.”

Anton looked disappointed.

Sera crossed her arms.

“What is the other solution?”

Joel looked toward the communication station.

Then he made a decision.

Sera saw it happen.

His posture changed.

The last part of Joel the passenger disappeared.

He walked toward the communications console.

The young communications officer instinctively moved aside.

Joel sat.

Sera frowned.

“What are you doing?”

“Calling for help.”

Rook almost laughed.

“Through what?”

He pointed toward the communications display.

“The Coalition is jamming every normal relay channel.”

Joel began entering commands.

“Normal channels.”

The officer stared over his shoulder.

“These frequencies are not in the civilian spectrum.”

“No.”

“Military?”

“No.”

Anton walked over.

“Move encryption layer three first.”

Joel looked at him.

“I know.”

“You were entering four.”

“I was checking whether you were paying attention.”

“You were making a mistake.”

Joel continued typing.

Sera stepped closer.

“What channel is that?”

Neither brother answered.

The screen changed.

A black field appeared.

Then a symbol formed in the centre.

A dark crown.

A blade beneath it.

Thin crimson lines around both.

Dax went still.

Rook slowly removed his helmet.

Sera recognized the crest now.

House von Hjertstedt.

But this was not the public mercenary communications network.

Joel placed his hand against the console.

The system scanned him.

**BIOMETRIC AUTHORITY ACCEPTED.**

A second line appeared.

**BLOOD AUTHORITY VERIFIED.**

The communications officer whispered:

“Blood authority?”

Anton smiled.

“This is the interesting part.”

Joel’s crimson eyes brightened.

The console changed again.

**COMMAND PRIORITY REQUESTED.**

Joel entered a code.

Sera watched the system.

Then another line appeared.

**PRIORITY: CRIMSON.**

**HOUSE COMMAND NETWORK ACCESS GRANTED.**

Rook looked at Joel.

“What rank are you?”

Joel ignored the question.

He opened the channel.

Static filled the command room.

Then silence.

Joel spoke.

Not loudly.

He did not need to.

“This is Joel von Hjertstedt.”

The entire command room seemed to become still.

“Crimson priority. Emergency reinforcement request.”

Anton leaned against the console beside him.

Joel continued.

“Coordinates attached. Sector Command Hub Kestrel. Vardos Sector.”

He glanced at the tactical display.

“Hostile Coalition force currently sixty-one warships. Multiple boarding formations already inside the station.”

Sera watched him.

This was not a mercenary asking another mercenary company for assistance.

Joel sounded like someone activating an organisation.

“Civilian population four thousand eight hundred. Protected under Captain Sera Venn’s station contract.”

Sera looked sharply at him.

Joel continued without pause.

“Station defensive grid non-functional. Local garrison exhausted. One allied frigate combat damaged.”

Another impact shook the command room.

Joel barely moved.

“Request nearest available House elements divert immediately.”

Anton tapped Joel’s shoulder.

“Tell them we have liquor.”

Joel slowly turned toward him.

“No.”

“It may improve response time.”

“The House does not dispatch fleets for alcohol.”

“The right alcohol?”

Joel ignored him.

A voice finally answered.

Distorted.

Professional.

“Crimson authority verified.”

Sera’s communications officer looked pale.

The voice continued.

“State reinforcement requirement.”

Joel glanced at the tactical situation.

“Immediate protection force.”

A pause.

“How heavy?”

Anton leaned toward the microphone.

“Very.”

Joel pushed him away.

“Enough to break a fleet action and secure a station.”

The answer came quickly.

“Confirmed.”

Then:

“Nearest banner elements are diverting.”

Sera stepped closer.

“How many?”

Joel looked at the console.

The response came before he could answer.

“Three Vigil groups available.”

Joel shook his head.

“Insufficient.”

Sera stared at him.

Three groups.

Insufficient?

The House officer on the channel did not argue.

“Additional assets?”

“Yes.”

Joel’s voice hardened.

“Send an Oath command group.”

Rook looked at Anton.

Anton smiled.

The voice answered.

“Command group available but outside immediate lane.”

“Divert it.”

“Authority?”

Joel looked almost offended.

“You verified my authority.”

A pause.

“Yes, my lord.”

Silence.

Sera stopped breathing for a moment.

My lord.

Anton looked toward her.

His grin widened.

Sera slowly turned toward Joel.

Joel closed his eyes.

The communications officer stared at him.

Dax whispered:

“Oh.”

Anton laughed.

Joel pointed at him without looking.

“Not one word.”

Anton laughed harder.

Sera stepped closer to Joel.

“Lord?”

Joel kept his attention on the console.

“Complicated.”

“You said you were evaluating captains.”

“Yes.”

“You did not say you could order House battle groups around.”

“It did not seem relevant at the time.”

Anton nearly doubled over laughing.

Sera pointed toward him.

“And you?”

Anton straightened.

“Also complicated.”

Rook stared at both brothers.

“How complicated?”

Anton smiled.

“Very.”

The station shook again.

A violent impact threw one crew member from their chair.

The tactical officer shouted.

“Multiple penetrations! Outer Dock Four!”

Another alarm.

“Boarders entering agricultural storage!”

Another.

“Ring Six defenders falling back!”

Sera turned immediately toward command.

“How long until reinforcements?”

The communication console displayed routing estimates.

The answer was not comforting.

Joel’s expression darkened.

“Not soon enough.”

Anton stopped laughing.

Sera looked at him.

“What does that mean?”

“It means,” Joel said, rising from the communications seat, “we have to hold the station ourselves until they arrive.”

The Coalition fleet fired again.

Six missiles struck the asteroid almost together.

The command room shook violently.

One console exploded.

Smoke filled the air.

The engineer shouted.

“Primary sensor array damaged!”

“Switch to secondary!”

“Secondary is older than Rook!”

Rook looked offended.

“Not by much.”

Sera moved back to the command chair.

“Internal defence status.”

Red filled the screen.

Coalition troops had entered five separate station sections.

The defenders were being compressed toward the central habitation areas.

Sera’s jaw tightened.

“They are going after the refugees.”

Joel stepped beside her.

“Yes.”

Something changed in his face.

The humour vanished.

The crimson in his eyes became brighter.

Anton saw it.

His own expression changed.

No laughter now.

No betting.

The House Charter was simple about this kind of thing.

Shield the helpless.

Punish predation.

Keep war bounded.

And if the enemy refused?

Then the House became considerably less polite.

Anton drew his pistol.

“What do you want defended first?”

Sera looked at him.

For the first time, Anton had asked rather than decided.

She selected three zones.

“Medical.”

Anton nodded.

“Habitation.”

Joel studied the routes.

“And the reactor levels,” Sera finished.

Joel looked at Rook.

“You take habitation.”

Rook nodded immediately.

“Dax?”

Sera looked toward her gunner.

“Medical.”

Dax checked his weapon.

“Finally.”

Anton looked at him.

“I am coming with you.”

Dax stared.

“Why?”

“You still want to hit me.”

“I do.”

“You should survive long enough to try.”

Dax gave him a hard smile.

“Deal.”

Sera looked toward Joel.

“And you?”

Joel studied the internal tactical map.

Red markers were moving toward the central transit spine.

Hundreds of them.

“I will organize the counterattack.”

Sera raised one eyebrow.

“My counterattack.”

Joel looked at her.

Then smiled.

“Of course, Captain.”

Anton leaned toward Dax.

“That means he has already planned it.”

Joel ignored him.

The communication console sounded again.

A House message appeared.

**REINFORCEMENT GROUPS COMMITTED.**

**SANCTUARY SUPPORT ATTACHED.**

**CRIMSON PRIORITY ACTIVE.**

Joel looked at the message.

Then toward Sera.

“They are coming.”

“How many?”

Joel smiled.

“Enough.”

Outside, the Coalition fleet continued its bombardment.

Inside, hundreds of boarders pushed toward the refugees.

Kestrel’s ancient weapons were failing one by one.

But somewhere beyond the Vardos lanes, House warships were changing course.

Vigil frigates left patrol routes.

An Oath command group broke from another contract corridor.

Sanctuary tenders sealed their medical decks and accelerated toward the gate.

The House had heard Joel von Hjertstedt call.

And the House was moving.

Kestrel only had to survive long enough for the answer to arrive.

## 11. The House Answers

Kestrel shook again.

The impact came from somewhere deep beneath the command tower.

Lights failed across half the tactical pit.

For three seconds, the main screen went black.

Then emergency power restored it.

Captain Sera Venn gripped the arms of the old sector command chair.

“Damage.”

Her engineer did not look up.

“Outer armour breach near Dock Four. Pressure holding behind emergency bulkheads.”

“Defence batteries?”

“One still firing.”

“Missiles?”

The engineer hesitated.

“Eight.”

Sera closed her eyes for half a second.

Eight missiles.

Sixty-one Coalition warships.

Hundreds of enemy troops inside her station.

The numbers were becoming ridiculous.

Joel stood beside the tactical display.

He no longer looked like a passenger.

His coat was still torn. His weapon remained holstered. His crimson eyes had faded slightly, but everything else about him had changed.

The way he stood.

The way people looked toward him when reports came in.

The way even Commander Rook had begun waiting for Joel to speak.

Anton had already left with Dax for the medical levels.

Gunfire echoed through the station communications network.

The Coalition admiral appeared on the main screen again.

“Captain Venn.”

Sera stared at him.

“What?”

“Your position is finished.”

Another explosion shook Kestrel.

The admiral did not react.

“You have lost Dock Ring Four. Your outer batteries are silent. We have forces approaching your civilian sections.”

Sera leaned back in the command chair.

“You seem very proud of defeating refugees and sixty-year-old artillery.”

The admiral’s expression hardened.

“Your surrender window has expired.”

Joel quietly said:

“Good.”

Sera glanced toward him.

The admiral continued.

“We are beginning final suppression.”

Joel stepped closer to the tactical console.

“Captain.”

Sera muted the transmission.

“What?”

“Tell your people to brace.”

“For what?”

Joel looked toward the gate.

Sera followed his eyes.

Nothing was there.

Only darkness.

The Vardos gate hung beyond the Coalition fleet, ancient and silent.

Then the sensor officer moved sharply.

“Captain.”

Everyone turned.

He leaned toward his display.

“I have gate activation.”

Sera stood.

The Coalition admiral stopped speaking.

His eyes moved somewhere off-screen.

His bridge had detected it too.

The gate began to glow.

Not slowly.

Violently.

White energy raced around the ancient ring.

Coalition ships nearest the gate immediately changed course.

Their formation became confused.

“Multiple vessels preparing transit,” the sensor officer said.

“How many?”

“I cannot tell.”

Joel smiled.

The gate flashed.

A ship emerged.

Fast.

Too fast.

The first frigate came through the throat already burning hard on manoeuvring thrusters.

Dark armour.

Clean lines.

Weapons recessed beneath disciplined hull plating.

For one second, its transponder remained silent.

Then its identity appeared across every sensor screen in the system.

**HJERTSTEDT VIGIL FRIGATE**

A second ship emerged behind it.

Then a third.

The first immediately rolled away from the arrival envelope.

Point defence fired into debris automatically.

The second burned high.

The third dropped beneath the gate plane.

They did not form a parade line.

They scattered into combat separation the instant they emerged.

Sera stared.

“They came through ready to fight.”

Joel looked at her.

“That is generally recommended when someone is shooting at your destination.”

Another frigate emerged.

Then another.

The Coalition formation reacted.

Destroyers rotated.

Missile doors opened.

The Coalition admiral shouted something toward his officers.

The gate flashed again.

Three more Hjertstedt frigates arrived almost together.

One came through slightly off-vector.

Its thrusters fired immediately.

The frigate missed a Coalition escort by less than a kilometre.

The Coalition ship opened fire.

The Vigil frigate answered.

Not at the reactor.

Not at the bridge.

Its guns struck the Coalition vessel’s forward weapon mounts.

One turret disappeared.

The second jammed halfway through rotation.

The Coalition escort lost firepower in seconds.

Joel watched.

“Good.”

Sera stared at him.

“That was your idea of insufficient reinforcement?”

“They are only the first group.”

The sensor officer looked back.

“Captain, more contacts.”

The gate flashed again.

Another three Vigil frigates emerged.

Nine House warships now moved through the Coalition rear.

The enemy formation began to break apart.

Not because nine frigates could defeat sixty-one ships by themselves.

Because the Coalition suddenly had a new problem.

Every captain near the gate had to choose.

Keep firing at Kestrel.

Or turn around.

Joel pointed toward the tactical display.

“There.”

Sera saw it.

The Coalition bombardment was weakening.

Cruisers were ordering escorts back toward the gate.

Frigates abandoned firing positions.

Boarding transports began changing formation to protect themselves.

“They are turning,” Sera said.

“They have to.”

The communication console sounded.

A new channel forced itself through the Coalition jamming.

The signal was clean.

Strong.

Military.

A woman’s voice filled the command room.

“Sector Command Kestrel, this is Vigil Lead.”

Joel stepped toward the console.

“Go.”

“Crimson authority confirmed. First reinforcement element has emerged. Three Vigil groups operational.”

The officer’s voice remained calm.

“Enemy formation: sixty-one hulls. Confirmation quality improving.”

Joel looked toward the tactical display.

“Priority?”

“Civilian station under active assault.”

Joel nodded.

“Correct.”

The House officer continued.

“White Warrant conditions appear satisfied. Request authority to engage hostile vessels threatening protected population.”

Sera looked at Joel.

She expected him to answer.

He did not.

Instead, Joel turned toward her.

“Captain.”

“What?”

“Your station.”

Sera understood.

Her station.

Her contract.

Her protected civilians.

Her war.

“You called them.”

“Yes.”

“You can command them.”

“Yes.”

“And you are asking me?”

Joel looked toward the burning tactical display.

“You accepted responsibility for Kestrel.”

His voice was quiet.

“That means something.”

Sera looked at the House communications link.

Nine warships waited.

Not firing freely.

Waiting for lawful authority.

For her.

Sera pressed the communication control.

“This is Sector Commander Sera Venn.”

A short silence followed.

Then:

“Listening, Commander.”

Sera looked at the Coalition formation.

“They are bombarding civilian sections. Boarding troops have entered medical and habitation zones.”

The House officer answered:

“Confirmed.”

“Disable any ship actively supporting the assault.”

Joel smiled slightly.

Sera continued.

“Do not destroy vessels that can be captured. Preserve life support where possible.”

Joel looked at her.

The smile grew.

Anton would have been laughing.

The House officer answered:

“Understood.”

Then her voice changed.

Formal.

Hard.

“White Warrant acknowledged.”

Every Hjertstedt frigate transmitted simultaneously.

The same message crossed the system.

**VARDOS COALITION VESSELS.**

**YOU ARE ENGAGED IN ACTIVE ASSAULT AGAINST A PROTECTED CIVILIAN POPULATION.**

**CEASE FIRE.**

**WITHDRAW BOARDING FORCES.**

**CUT ENGINES AND ACCEPT INSPECTION.**

**CONTINUED RESISTANCE WILL AUTHORIZE SEIZURE.**

The Coalition admiral appeared on Sera’s screen.

His controlled expression was gone.

“This station has no authority to issue warrants against Coalition vessels!”

Joel leaned toward Sera.

“May I?”

She looked at him.

“Please.”

Joel opened the channel.

“This is Joel von Hjertstedt.”

The admiral became silent.

Joel’s eyes slowly turned crimson.

“The warrant is not yours to approve.”

The channel closed.

Sera looked at him.

“That was dramatic.”

“Anton is a bad influence.”

Outside, the battle changed.

The Vigil frigates accelerated.

Their formation spread.

Not toward the Coalition cruisers.

Toward the vulnerable structure around them.

Sensor ships.

Missile escorts.

Boarding transports.

The House frigates attacked the fleet’s ability to fight rather than its biggest hulls.

One Vigil group crossed through Hammer Range behind a Coalition transport.

Missiles launched.

Only four.

All four struck around the transport’s engines.

The drive bloom disappeared.

The transport began drifting.

Another Vigil frigate fired on a Coalition destroyer.

Its first salvo damaged the communication mast.

The second destroyed a missile launcher.

Then the House ship broke away before the destroyer could bring its spinal weapon around.

“They are cutting pieces off the fleet,” Sera’s sensor officer said.

Joel nodded.

“Frigates are not supposed to duel cruisers.”

A Coalition frigate attempted to intercept one of the House ships.

Two Vigils immediately turned on it.

One attacked its sensors.

The second fired into the manoeuvring thrusters.

The Coalition ship began rotating helplessly.

Neither House vessel finished it.

Sera watched the crippled frigate drift away.

“They could have destroyed it.”

“Yes.”

“Why did they stop?”

Joel looked at her.

“It is no longer a threat.”

The station shook again.

But this impact was weaker.

The bombardment had dropped by nearly half.

Sera looked at the internal battle map.

Coalition troops remained everywhere.

“Boarders still advancing on medical.”

Joel activated the channel.

“Anton.”

Gunfire answered.

Then Anton’s voice.

“This is an inconvenient time.”

“House ships arrived.”

A pause.

Then:

“They started without me?”

Sera heard Dax shouting behind him.

“ANTON! LEFT!”

A massive gunshot cracked over the channel.

Anton returned.

“Sorry.”

Joel continued.

“Three Vigil groups.”

“Only three?”

Sera looked at Joel.

Joel looked tired.

“Anton.”

“What?”

“Stop insulting the reinforcements.”

“They know I respect them.”

Another explosion.

Anton laughed.

“Medical remains ours.”

Sera released a breath she had not realized she was holding.

“How bad?”

Dax answered this time.

“Bad.”

Anton interrupted.

“Manageable.”

Dax shouted:

“THERE ARE SIXTY OF THEM!”

“Fifty-seven now.”

Another gunshot.

“Fifty-six.”

Sera closed the channel.

The sensor officer suddenly spoke.

“New gate activity.”

Everyone looked up.

Sera stared at Joel.

“More frigates?”

Joel watched the gate.

“No.”

The gate began building energy again.

But this transit looked different.

The arrival field expanded.

Wider.

Heavier.

The Coalition fleet saw it too.

Ships nearest the gate started moving away.

Fast.

The gate flashed.

A bow appeared through the white distortion.

Far larger than a frigate.

Armoured.

Clean.

Heraldic lines cut across the hull.

Its running lights formed a narrow crown shape across the command spine.

Sera slowly stood.

The cruiser emerged fully.

Weapons arrays unfolded along its armour.

Behind it came two Vigil frigates.

Then a Sanctuary tender.

Then another cruiser.

The sensor officer whispered:

“Captain…”

Joel folded his arms.

“The Oath group arrived early.”

Sera stared.

“You said they were outside immediate lane range.”

“They must have been closer than the network estimate.”

The first Oath-cruiser transmitted its identity.

Then its escort ships.

Then the tender.

More House symbols filled the tactical display.

The Coalition fleet stopped advancing.

For the first time since arriving at Kestrel, the enemy fleet began moving backward.

Anton’s voice suddenly came over the command channel again.

“Joel.”

“Yes?”

“Tell me that is an Oath group.”

“It is.”

A burst of laughter came through the speakers.

Dax shouted in the background:

“What is wrong with your family?”

Anton answered immediately.

“Many things!”

Joel closed his eyes.

Sera laughed.

Just once.

Then she stopped herself.

The Oath-cruiser opened a system-wide transmission.

Its commander spoke with cold precision.

“Vardos Coalition fleet.”

“House von Hjertstedt has acknowledged the protection contract of Sector Command Kestrel.”

Sera looked sharply at Joel.

He had not told her that part.

The voice continued.

“Civilian protection is now under witnessed House guarantee.”

The Coalition admiral appeared again.

“You have no jurisdiction here.”

The Oath commander answered without hesitation.

“Then demonstrate yours without firing on civilians.”

Silence.

The House commander continued.

“You have one opportunity.”

“Recall your boarders.”

“Cease bombardment.”

“Accept separation inspection.”

“Your wounded will receive treatment.”

“Your crews will be protected under surrender terms.”

A pause.

“If you refuse…”

The Oath-cruiser’s spinal weapon began charging.

Across the tactical display, Vigil frigates took attack positions.

The Sanctuary tender remained behind them.

Medical beacons already active.

Ready for wounded from both sides.

That detail struck Sera harder than the weapons.

The House had arrived prepared not only to fight.

But to deal with what came after.

The Oath commander finished.

“…then we will remove your ability to continue.”

The Coalition fleet went silent.

Sera watched the tactical display.

Sixty-one enemy ships.

Twelve House combat hulls now present.

More likely coming.

Kestrel still burning.

Boarders still inside.

The war had not ended.

But it had changed.

Joel rested one hand on the command table.

Sera looked at him.

“How many more are coming?”

Joel considered the question.

Then smiled.

“Enough that Admiral Tal should make a very good decision very soon.”

Outside, the Coalition fleet began withdrawing from Hammer Range.

Inside Kestrel, the fighting continued.

And beyond the gate, another House transit signal appeared.

## 12. Take the Station

For seventeen seconds, the Vardos Coalition fleet hesitated.

House frigates were cutting into its rear.

Two Oath cruisers were forming near the gate.

More Hjertstedt signatures waited beyond transit.

Kestrel remained ahead—damaged, burning, full of civilians and already crawling with Coalition boarding troops.

Admiral Voren Tal stared at the tactical display aboard the *Resolute Vardos*.

If he withdrew now, the Coalition lost everything.

Kestrel.

The sector.

Its reputation.

And perhaps the entire Vardos frontier.

“Admiral,” his tactical officer said. “Recommend immediate withdrawal before additional Hjertstedt capital units transit.”

Tal looked toward the station.

“No.”

“Sir?”

“We cannot defeat their fleet.”

Silence spread across the bridge.

Tal pointed toward Kestrel.

“So we stop fighting their fleet.”

The tactical officer slowly understood.

“Take the station.”

Tal nodded.

“All transports forward.”

“All boarding reserves.”

“Every marine company.”

“We seize the command core before Hjertstedt can secure the orbit.”

His operations officer looked worried.

“That leaves the fleet with almost no boarding reserve.”

“There will be no second battle.”

Tal opened the fleet channel.

His voice reached every Coalition ship.

“Final assault.”

“Capture Sector Command Kestrel.”

“Command core is primary.”

“Reactor control secondary.”

“Captain Sera Venn is to be taken alive.”

He hesitated.

Then added:

“Joel and Anton von Hjertstedt are priority captures.”

---

Inside Kestrel’s command centre, Anton’s laughter came over the communication channel.

“He wants us alive?”

Joel stood beside Sera’s tactical display.

“Yes.”

Anton laughed harder.

“That is very optimistic.”

Dax shouted somewhere behind him.

“Anton! Stop talking and shoot!”

A heavy handgun cracked.

Someone screamed.

Anton returned to the channel.

“Medical remains secure.”

Sera looked toward Joel.

“Your brother is enjoying himself.”

“He usually does.”

The sensor officer suddenly raised his voice.

“Mass launch!”

The tactical display filled with contacts.

Boarding pods.

Assault cutters.

Troop shuttles.

Entire Coalition transports were moving directly toward Kestrel.

“How many?” Sera asked.

The count climbed.

One thousand.

Fifteen hundred.

Two thousand.

Then higher.

Rook swore.

“They are emptying their ships.”

Joel watched the attack pattern.

“Tal knows he cannot beat the arriving House force.”

Sera looked at him.

“So he wants to seize Kestrel before you control the orbit.”

“Yes.”

“And use the civilians as protection.”

Joel’s crimson eyes sharpened.

“Yes.”

The humour disappeared.

Sera immediately opened the station network.

“All defenders, abandon the outer corridors.”

Rook looked at her.

“Captain?”

“We cannot defend forty kilometres of station.”

Joel nodded.

“Correct.”

Sera marked the protected zones.

“Medical.”

“Central habitation.”

“Reactor control.”

“Life support.”

“Command.”

She looked toward Rook.

“Everything else becomes delay ground.”

Rook understood.

“Pull them in. Divide them.”

Joel pointed at the internal map.

“Cut power behind Coalition groups. Lock elevators. Drop local gravity where possible. Force them through maintenance routes.”

Sera glanced at him.

“You are giving orders again.”

“Recommendations.”

“That sounded like an order.”

Anton’s voice came through the speaker.

“It was.”

Joel ignored him.

The station shook.

Another assault cutter struck the hull.

Then another.

“New breaches!”

“Industrial Five!”

“Ring Six!”

“Habitation Outer!”

“Medical maintenance level!”

Anton stopped laughing.

“Medical maintenance?”

The tactical officer confirmed.

“Twenty-three hostile signatures.”

Anton’s voice became cold.

“Dax.”

“I see them.”

Gunfire erupted over the channel.

Then silence.

Then Anton:

“Twenty.”

Dax shouted:

“NINETEEN!”

Anton answered:

“Thank you.”

Sera closed the channel.

Joel was already moving toward communications.

“What now?” she asked.

“More infantry.”

Rook looked at him.

“We do not have more infantry.”

“No.”

Joel sat at the communications console.

“But I do.”

His hands moved across the encrypted House network.

**CRIMSON PRIORITY ACTIVE.**

**LOCAL HOUSE ASSETS AVAILABLE.**

Joel scanned the list.

Vigil frigates.

Sanctuary support.

Oath cruisers.

Then one contact caught his attention.

A fast troop transport moving with the reinforcement group.

He opened a direct channel.

“House transport, identify.”

The reply came through distorted encryption.

“Fast transport *Hrafn Spear*. House authority authenticated.”

Joel did not recognize the voice.

That did not matter.

“Your troop complement?”

A short pause.

“Two hundred available.”

Joel looked toward Sera.

She heard it.

“Two hundred?”

Joel nodded.

“Fresh troops.”

Sera stepped closer.

“Can they reach the station?”

Joel looked at the tactical map.

Coalition ships surrounded Kestrel.

House frigates were opening holes through the formation, but not enough to guarantee a safe approach.

“Yes.”

Sera stared at him.

“That answer came quickly.”

Joel returned to the channel.

“*Hrafn Spear*, Crimson priority order.”

“Receiving.”

“Break formation.”

“Maximum burn directly for Kestrel.”

“Dock at medical-sector military collar.”

The communications officer beside Joel looked at him.

“That route crosses the Coalition assault screen.”

Joel continued.

“House Vigil Lead will open your corridor.”

The transport answered:

“Understood.”

Joel added:

“Deploy all troops immediately upon docking. Medical and central habitation take priority.”

“Confirmed.”

Joel closed the channel.

Anton immediately appeared on another channel.

“You ordered reinforcements to medical?”

“Two hundred.”

Anton laughed.

“Excellent.”

“Try not to frighten them.”

Anton paused.

Then:

“You know that depends entirely on who they sent.”

Joel frowned.

“What does that mean?”

“No idea.”

Anton sounded far too pleased.

The channel closed.

---

Outside Kestrel, the *Hrafn Spear* left House formation.

It was not built like a warship.

It was built to arrive.

Long.

Narrow.

Overpowered engines.

Heavy forward armour.

Minimal broadside weapons.

A transport designed around one principle:

Cross the killing ground before the enemy could stop it.

Coalition sensors detected it immediately.

“Fast transport breaking formation!” Tal’s tactical officer called.

Tal leaned toward his display.

“Destroy it.”

Three Coalition frigates turned.

Missiles launched.

The nearest Vigil group reacted before the first missile crossed half the distance.

House frigates accelerated.

Point-defence fire filled space.

One Vigil placed itself directly between the transport and the incoming salvo.

Light flashed across its armour.

Missiles died.

Fragments struck the frigate.

It kept flying.

The *Hrafn Spear* burned harder.

Coalition gunfire followed.

A shell struck its forward armour.

Another ripped across its port side.

The transport did not slow.

Then the Oath cruiser fired.

One precise spinal shot crossed the battlefield.

A Coalition frigate lost its drives.

A second Vigil crippled another’s fire-control array.

The third Coalition ship turned away.

A corridor opened.

The *Hrafn Spear* went through it at full thrust.

---

Inside medical, Dax Corven fired until his rifle clicked empty.

“Reloading!”

Anton stepped past him.

His heavy pistol fired.

A Coalition soldier disappeared behind a broken medical bulkhead.

Refugees were being evacuated behind them.

Doctors moved wounded civilians toward central treatment rooms.

The entire level smelled of smoke, antiseptic and burned metal.

Dax loaded another magazine.

“How many?”

Anton looked down the corridor.

“Enough.”

“That is not a number.”

“It is a useful number.”

A Coalition breacher appeared.

Anton fired.

The breacher fell.

Dax looked at him.

“You are enjoying this.”

“Yes.”

The deck shook violently.

Then came another sound.

Deep.

Mechanical.

Docking clamps.

Dax turned.

“What was that?”

Anton smiled.

“Our ride arrived.”

“My ride?”

“Our reinforcements.”

The military docking collar at the end of medical came alive.

Ancient locking arms closed around something outside.

Pressure indicators rose.

A warning appeared.

**HOUSE TRANSPORT DOCKED.**

Dax looked at Anton.

“How many?”

“Joel said two hundred.”

The inner docking doors opened.

Steam rolled through.

Heavy footsteps followed.

Anton’s smile slowly changed.

Not laughter.

Recognition.

The first soldiers entered.

Black armour.

Heavy.

Industrial.

Weapons built for corridor fighting.

**Iron Guard.**

Behind them came troops in darker, more refined armour, almost featureless except for the thin heraldic markings around their shoulders.

**Black Guard.**

Then came crimson.

The final formation wore deep red armour, cleaner and heavier than the others.

Their helmets were shaped differently.

Their weapons were larger.

Their movements perfectly synchronized.

**Crimson Guard.**

Dax stared.

Refugees stopped moving.

Even the old Kestrel soldiers looked toward them.

Two hundred House troops filled the docking concourse.

Iron Guard formed the forward wall.

Black Guard immediately moved toward side passages and security access.

Crimson Guard remained in the centre.

Anton slowly lowered his pistol.

“Oh.”

Dax looked at him.

“Oh?”

Anton started laughing.

Not because of the soldiers.

Because two men were walking between the Crimson Guard.

Neither wore full combat armour.

They did not need to.

One was broad-shouldered, older, his coat reinforced beneath the surface with military plating.

The second moved beside him with the quiet confidence of someone completely at home surrounded by armed elites.

Anton stared at them.

Then laughed harder.

Dax looked from Anton to the newcomers.

“You know them?”

Anton could barely answer.

“Yes.”

The first man stopped in front of him.

His eyes turned crimson.

“Anton.”

Anton straightened.

“Christer.”

The second man looked toward the damaged medical level.

Bodies.

Smoke.

Coalition weapons on the floor.

Refugees behind barricades.

Then he looked back at Anton.

“Interesting holiday.”

Anton grinned.

“Daniel.”

Dax froze.

Christer von Hjertstedt.

Daniel von Hjertstedt.

Even here, in a forgotten frontier sector, the names carried weight.

Anton pointed toward the transport.

“Joel ordered a troop deployment.”

Christer nodded.

“We noticed.”

“He does not know you are here.”

Daniel smiled.

“No.”

Anton started laughing again.

Dax looked between the three Hjertstedt men.

“You are telling me Joel ordered a troop transport…”

“Yes.”

“…without knowing who was aboard?”

“Yes.”

“And neither of you told him?”

Christer looked toward Daniel.

Daniel looked toward Christer.

Then both men smiled.

Anton laughed like a madman.

Dax slowly shook his head.

“I hate your family.”

Anton clapped him on the shoulder.

“You are learning quickly.”

Christer looked toward the medical tactical display.

“How bad?”

Anton became serious immediately.

“Coalition committed everything.”

“Boarders?”

“Thousands across the station.”

“Fleet?”

“Being dismantled.”

Daniel glanced toward the ceiling.

“Joel?”

“Command.”

Christer nodded.

“Sera Venn?”

Anton smiled.

“Worth coming for.”

That earned a look from both men.

Anton continued.

“She took responsibility for the refugees before she knew we were House.”

Daniel’s expression changed.

“She did?”

“Yes.”

“No promise of payment?”

“No.”

“No guarantee of survival?”

“No.”

Christer looked toward the civilians.

Then toward the Coalition contacts approaching medical.

“Good.”

Daniel drew his sidearm.

“Very good.”

The Crimson Guard around them reacted.

Weapons raised.

Iron Guard formed assault groups.

Black Guard disappeared into the side corridors almost without sound.

Dax watched the transformation.

Two hundred soldiers had arrived.

But they did not move like reinforcements waiting for orders.

They moved like a force that already knew exactly what kind of war it had entered.

Christer looked toward Anton.

“Who commands locally?”

Anton pointed at Dax.

“Medical belongs to him.”

Dax stared.

“To me?”

Anton smiled.

“Sera assigned it.”

Christer immediately looked at Dax.

“Then give us sectors.”

Dax froze again.

Christer von Hjertstedt was asking him for orders.

Anton leaned closer.

“This is the part where you answer.”

Dax looked at the map.

Then pointed.

“Coalition pushing from East Medical and Maintenance Three.”

Christer nodded.

“Iron Guard takes East Medical.”

Daniel pointed at another route.

“Black Guard cuts behind Maintenance Three.”

Dax looked toward the Crimson Guard.

“And them?”

Anton’s smile returned.

Christer looked down the central corridor.

“Where is the heaviest fighting?”

Dax understood.

“Habitation Central.”

Christer turned.

“Crimson Guard with us.”

The formation moved.

Two hundred House troops entered Kestrel.

---

Back in command, Joel watched the medical tactical display change.

House identification marks appeared.

Dozens.

Then hundreds.

The Coalition advance stopped almost instantly.

Sera stared at the new symbols.

“That transport worked.”

Joel nodded.

“Apparently.”

The communications officer looked closer.

“Two hundred House troops confirmed.”

Joel smiled.

“Good.”

Then she frowned.

“Sir…”

Joel looked at her.

“There are command-level identification codes inside the troop group.”

Joel’s smile disappeared.

“What codes?”

The console hesitated.

Then displayed them.

**HJERTSTEDT BLOOD AUTHORITY.**

**MULTIPLE SENIOR IDENTITIES.**

Joel stared.

Anton’s channel opened before he could ask.

Laughter flooded the command room.

Joel slowly closed his eyes.

“Anton.”

His brother struggled to speak.

“You really should have checked the passenger manifest.”

Joel became very still.

“Who?”

Anton laughed harder.

Behind him another voice entered the channel.

“Hello, Joel.”

Joel’s eyes opened.

Sera watched his face.

For the first time since she had met him, Joel von Hjertstedt looked genuinely surprised.

“Christer?”

A second voice came through.

“And me.”

Joel stared at the speaker.

“Daniel?”

Sera looked at him.

Then at the tactical display.

Then at the hundreds of House soldiers pouring toward the worst fighting.

“You accidentally ordered more members of your family into my station?”

Joel remained silent.

Anton laughed so hard the transmission distorted.

Sera slowly smiled.

Outside, Admiral Tal continued throwing troops at Kestrel.

Inside, the medical line had just become something entirely different.

The Coalition had wanted to take the station before the House arrived in force.

They had failed to notice that the House was now entering through the airlocks.

## 13. Family Business

Joel stared at the communications console.

“Christer?”

Gunfire cracked through the channel.

Then Christer von Hjertstedt answered.

“Yes.”

Joel’s eyes narrowed.

“Daniel?”

Daniel answered next.

“Also here.”

Silence filled Kestrel’s command centre.

Captain Sera Venn stood beside Joel.

Commander Rook had stopped studying the tactical display.

The communications officer was openly staring.

Joel placed both hands on the console.

“You absolute idiots.”

Sera blinked.

Anton immediately started laughing over the channel.

Christer sounded offended.

“That is not an appropriate greeting.”

“You were not supposed to be here.”

Daniel answered calmly.

“We were already travelling with the transport.”

“That does not improve this.”

“It explains it.”

“No. It explains how two senior members of our family managed to enter an active battle without telling anyone.”

Anton laughed harder.

Joel pointed at the speaker as if Anton could see him.

“And you stop laughing.”

“I cannot.”

“You can.”

“I genuinely cannot.”

Another explosion rolled through Medical.

Weapons fired.

Then Christer’s voice returned.

“One moment.”

The channel filled with shouting.

“IRON GUARD, FORWARD!”

Heavy weapons opened.

Something exploded.

Daniel followed.

“Black Guard, close Maintenance Three. No one escapes toward habitation.”

More gunfire.

Then silence returned.

Christer came back to the channel.

“There. Situation improved.”

Joel stared at the console.

“You brought Crimson Guard.”

“Yes.”

“Iron Guard.”

“Yes.”

“Black Guard.”

Daniel answered.

“Obviously.”

Joel slowly looked toward the ceiling.

Sera watched him.

“You sound unhappy about receiving two hundred elite troops.”

Joel turned toward her.

“I am unhappy about who came with them.”

Christer heard that.

“That is rude.”

“You are both supposed to be doing other things.”

Daniel replied.

“We were.”

“And?”

“We changed plans.”

“You changed plans because Anton sent you something, didn’t you?”

A pause.

Joel’s expression hardened.

“Anton.”

Anton stopped laughing.

Mostly.

“I may have sent a message.”

Joel closed his eyes.

“What message?”

Anton hesitated.

Sera started smiling.

Joel noticed.

“This is not funny.”

“It is becoming funny.”

Anton finally answered.

“I told them we had found an interesting captain.”

Joel slowly opened his eyes.

“That was all?”

Another pause.

“Mostly.”

“Anton.”

“I may have said there was a fortress.”

Joel stared at the speaker.

“And?”

“Refugees.”

“And?”

“Coalition fleet.”

“And?”

“A damaged frigate.”

“And?”

Anton lowered his voice.

“And that you were having fun.”

Christer started laughing.

Daniel joined him.

Sera covered her mouth.

Joel looked betrayed.

“Captain.”

She lowered her hand.

“Sorry.”

“You are not sorry.”

“No.”

Rook looked at Sera.

“I have served under military command for forty years.”

“Yes?”

“I have never heard senior officers speak like this.”

Joel pointed toward the speaker.

“They are family first. Officers second.”

Christer answered immediately.

“That is not what you say at formal dinners.”

“Formal dinners do not involve you arriving uninvited with two hundred Guardsmen.”

Daniel spoke.

“Technically, we were invited.”

Joel frowned.

“By whom?”

“You.”

“I ordered the transport.”

“Exactly.”

“I ordered the transport, not both of you.”

“You should issue clearer orders.”

Joel’s crimson eyes brightened.

Sera moved slightly away from him.

Not from fear.

Mostly.

“Daniel.”

“Yes?”

“When this battle ends, I am assigning you to logistics inspection.”

Daniel went silent.

Anton stopped laughing.

Christer whispered:

“That was excessive.”

Sera stared.

“What is wrong with logistics inspection?”

All three brothers answered at once.

“Everything.”

Sera laughed.

She could not stop herself this time.

Joel looked at her.

Then he started laughing too.

For several seconds, Kestrel’s command centre sounded completely wrong.

Outside, warships exchanged fire.

Inside, thousands of Coalition troops fought through ruined corridors.

And House von Hjertstedt’s senior family members argued about paperwork.

Then the station shook.

Hard.

The laughter stopped instantly.

The tactical officer turned.

“Coalition cruiser firing directly on Command Tower!”

Joel was beside the display before Sera reached it.

“Impact?”

“Outer armour only.”

“Next salvo?”

“Forty seconds.”

Joel studied the fleet pattern.

His entire personality changed again.

“Vigil Lead.”

The House commander answered.

“Listening.”

“Coalition cruiser designation Seven is ranging Command Tower.”

“Confirmed.”

“Drive kill.”

“Understood.”

Sera looked at him.

“You just ordered a warship crippled without asking me.”

Joel paused.

Then looked at her.

“Yes.”

Sera folded her arms.

Joel considered the situation.

“My apologies.”

Rook stared at him.

Anton’s voice came through the channel.

“He is terrible at being subordinate.”

Joel ignored him.

Sera looked toward the tactical display.

“Would you have done anything different if you asked?”

“No.”

“Then continue.”

Joel smiled.

“Thank you, Captain.”

A House Oath cruiser rotated outside Kestrel.

Its forward weapon aligned.

One shot crossed the battlefield.

The Coalition cruiser’s drive section erupted in debris.

Its engines died.

The vessel began drifting.

Sera watched.

“No reactor hit.”

Joel nodded.

“We want ships afterward.”

“Of course you do.”

“War is expensive.”

Rook looked at Joel.

“That may be the most mercenary thing you have said.”

Joel smiled.

“Good.”

The medical channel opened again.

Christer’s voice was serious now.

“Joel.”

“Yes.”

“We have stabilized Medical.”

Daniel continued.

“Coalition force retreating toward Central Transit.”

Anton interrupted.

“They are not retreating.”

Gunfire sounded.

“They are regrouping.”

Joel expanded the internal station map.

He saw it.

Hundreds of hostile markers were moving away from Medical.

Not toward the docks.

Toward Command.

Sera leaned over the display.

“They still want the command core.”

Rook nodded.

“Admiral Tal’s last chance.”

Joel marked three routes.

“Christer.”

“Yes.”

“Take Iron Guard through Transit West.”

“Understood.”

“Daniel. Black Guard through maintenance and cut behind them.”

“Already moving.”

Joel frowned.

“I had not finished.”

“I know what you were going to say.”

Anton laughed.

Joel sighed.

“Anton.”

“Yes?”

“You take Crimson Guard.”

Anton went quiet.

Sera noticed.

Even Rook noticed.

Anton’s voice returned differently.

No joking.

No laughter.

“Objective?”

Joel looked toward Sera.

Her station.

Her decision.

Sera understood.

She opened the channel.

“Protect Central Habitation.”

Anton answered immediately.

“Confirmed.”

“Do not chase fleeing Coalition troops into refugee sections.”

“Confirmed.”

“Any surrender is accepted.”

A pause.

Then Anton:

“Understood.”

Sera looked at Joel.

He nodded approvingly.

Christer spoke next.

“Captain Venn.”

Sera hesitated.

“Yes?”

“Your orders are clear.”

Daniel added:

“We will follow them.”

That felt different.

Sera looked toward Joel.

Joel smiled.

“You have command.”

She looked around Kestrel’s command centre.

Rook.

Her exhausted crew.

House officers.

An entire noble-war family slowly taking positions around her station.

“You really came here to recruit me?”

Joel nodded.

“Originally.”

“Originally?”

Anton answered from the field.

“Now we are defending your fortress.”

Joel smiled.

“Plans evolve.”

The tactical officer suddenly called out.

“New Coalition movement!”

The display changed.

Enemy warships were moving closer again.

Several transports accelerated toward Kestrel despite House fire.

Admiral Tal appeared on open communications.

His face was pale.

His fleet was breaking.

But he had not surrendered.

“All boarding forces,” Tal ordered.

“Forget withdrawal.”

“Forget casualties.”

“Take Command.”

“Take Venn.”

“Take the Hjertstedts.”

Anton laughed over the House channel.

Not wildly this time.

Coldly.

Christer joined him.

Then Daniel.

Joel stared at the tactical display.

His eyes turned crimson.

Sera slowly looked at him.

“What?”

Joel smiled.

“He keeps asking for us.”

Anton answered from somewhere deep inside Kestrel.

“Then perhaps we should stop being rude.”

Christer laughed.

Daniel added:

“Family meeting?”

Joel drew his weapon.

Sera pointed at him.

“You stay here.”

Joel looked disappointed.

“You are not going anywhere.”

“Captain—”

“I need someone who can control those lunatics.”

Anton protested.

“We can hear you.”

“That was intentional.”

For the first time, Daniel laughed loudly.

Joel looked toward Sera.

Then toward the tactical map.

Then back at her.

“Very well.”

He holstered his weapon.

Sera sat in the sector command chair.

“Now tell your family to stop the Coalition.”

Joel opened the House command channel.

His expression became almost cheerful.

“Christer. Daniel. Anton.”

Three voices answered.

“Listening.”

Joel smiled.

“The captain says stop them.”

Anton started laughing.

“Now that sounds like an order.”

## 14. Hunt the Admiral

The Coalition’s final assault began with every alarm in Kestrel sounding at once.

Red contacts flooded the internal tactical map.

Central Transit.

Habitation Spine.

Command Lift West.

Maintenance Core.

Hundreds of boarders were converging on the command tower.

Sera Venn watched the numbers climb.

“They are throwing everything at us.”

Joel stood beside her.

“No.”

Sera looked at him.

Joel pointed toward a new signal appearing near Dock Ring One.

A heavy assault shuttle had detached from the crippled Coalition flagship.

Its transponder carried command-level encryption.

Joel smiled.

“They are throwing everyone at us.”

Rook leaned toward the sensor console.

“Whose shuttle?”

The station computer broke the encryption.

**COALITION COMMAND AUTHORITY.**

**ADMIRAL VOREN TAL.**

Anton’s voice exploded across the House channel.

“He came personally?”

Joel slowly started laughing.

Christer joined him.

Then Daniel.

Sera stared at the communications console.

“Why is that funny?”

Anton answered.

“Because now we do not have to go looking for him.”

Joel shook his head.

“He is making this very convenient.”

Rook stared at them.

“That man has thousands of soldiers aboard this station.”

“Yes.”

“He came with elite troops.”

“Yes.”

“And you are pleased?”

Joel’s crimson eyes brightened.

“Very.”

Sera pointed toward the tactical display.

“No killing Tal.”

The laughter stopped.

Anton sounded disappointed.

“Captain.”

“I want him alive.”

Silence.

Then Joel smiled.

“There is the mercenary.”

Sera crossed her arms.

“He owes me a station.”

---

Admiral Tal’s shuttle slammed into Dock Ring One.

The outer doors had already been damaged by bombardment.

Coalition engineers blew the inner seals.

Armoured troops poured through.

Not ordinary boarders.

Tal’s command guard wore heavy black-and-grey armour with old Vardos military markings.

Forty veterans.

Behind them came Admiral Tal.

He wore combat armour beneath his command coat.

A pistol hung at his side.

His officers surrounded him.

Tal looked down the ruined docking corridor.

“Command tower.”

One of his colonels hesitated.

“Sir, the Hjertstedt troops are counterattacking across three sectors.”

“Then move faster.”

“We could return to the fleet.”

Tal turned toward him.

“The fleet is finished if we leave without Kestrel.”

Outside, House frigates were taking apart the Coalition formation one ship at a time.

Drive kills.

Sensor kills.

Weapon mounts crippled.

Transports disabled.

The Coalition still possessed more hulls.

It no longer possessed control of the battle.

Tal knew it.

Everyone around him knew it.

“Take Venn,” Tal ordered.

“Take one Hjertstedt alive.”

“And secure the command core.”

The guard moved.

---

At Central Habitation, Anton met the main Coalition assault.

The Crimson Guard formed behind him.

Deep-red armour filled the corridor.

Two lines.

Heavy shields forward.

Rifles behind.

Anton stood in front of everyone.

Dax stood beside him.

“You are supposed to be behind the shield line,” Dax said.

“So are you.”

“I am the local commander.”

Anton looked at him.

“That excuse works?”

“No.”

“Excellent.”

The Coalition appeared at the far end.

Anton’s eyes burned crimson.

“Last chance!” he shouted.

The enemy hesitated.

Anton raised his heavy pistol.

“Drop weapons and you receive prisoner protection!”

Dax glanced at him.

“That was surprisingly reasonable.”

“Joel says I should practice.”

Someone in the Coalition line fired.

The round struck the Crimson Guard shield.

Anton sighed.

“Practice finished.”

The Crimson Guard advanced.

The corridor erupted.

House rifles fired in controlled bursts.

Coalition troops returned fire.

Anton moved ahead of the shields.

Dax swore and followed.

The first enemy barricade lasted eleven seconds.

---

On Transit West, Christer led the Iron Guard.

There was nothing elegant about them.

The Iron Guard moved like a bulkhead.

Heavy armour.

Heavy weapons.

Slow, disciplined pressure.

Coalition troops fired down the corridor.

Christer raised one hand.

“Shields.”

Iron Guard locked together.

Rounds struck reinforced plates.

“Advance.”

They moved.

Ten metres.

Twenty.

Thirty.

A Coalition machine gun opened from a side position.

Christer pointed.

“Three.”

Three Iron Guards broke formation.

One suppressed.

One flanked.

One smashed through an old maintenance wall and appeared beside the enemy position.

The machine gun stopped.

Christer did not look back.

“Continue.”

---

Daniel’s Black Guard fought differently.

The Coalition never saw most of them.

Maintenance passages went dark.

Communication repeaters died.

Squad leaders disappeared from tactical networks.

Doors opened behind Coalition units.

Weapons were removed from unattended supply points.

Escape routes closed.

One Coalition company reached Junction Thirty-One expecting reinforcements.

Instead, the lights went out.

When emergency power returned, Black Guard stood behind them.

Daniel raised his pistol.

“Weapons down.”

The Coalition captain stared at the blocked corridor.

“You are outnumbered.”

Daniel looked toward the ceiling.

“No.”

Behind the Coalition formation, another door opened.

More Black Guard emerged.

“You are surrounded.”

Weapons hit the floor.

---

Sera watched the internal battle reverse.

Red Coalition markers had been pushing toward the centre.

Now groups were disappearing.

Some turned yellow.

Captured.

Others stopped moving.

Some fled.

The House troops were not merely defending Kestrel.

They were dismantling the boarding force.

Then Rook pointed at the screen.

“Command Lift East.”

A dense group was approaching.

Fifty contacts.

Military-grade identification.

Sera looked closer.

At the centre:

**ADMIRAL TAL.**

Joel laughed.

Sera immediately pointed at him.

“No.”

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

“I was going to suggest a tactical response.”

“You were going to say something about a bet.”

Joel paused.

“That was my second thought.”

Sera opened the House channel.

“Anton.”

Gunfire.

“Yes?”

“Tal is heading for Command.”

Anton went silent.

Then:

“Alive?”

“Alive.”

Another pause.

“That reduces the fun significantly.”

Dax shouted behind him.

“STOP COMPLAINING!”

Sera continued.

“Christer. Daniel.”

Two acknowledgements came immediately.

“Admiral Tal is entering the command spine.”

Joel leaned toward the microphone.

“Do not converge directly.”

Sera looked at him.

Joel was already marking the map.

“He will detect a mass movement.”

Sera understood.

“Herd him.”

Joel smiled.

“Yes.”

Sera looked at the tactical routes.

Then the station became a hunting ground.

“Daniel, close his eastern route.”

“Confirmed.”

“Christer, pressure from Transit West.”

“Moving.”

“Anton…”

Anton laughed.

Sera looked at the map.

“You take the direct route.”

“Finally.”

---

Tal reached Junction Command-Seven.

The door ahead closed.

“Override it!”

His engineer connected a bypass.

The lights went out.

Tal looked behind him.

“Movement!”

Black Guard fired from the darkness.

Not at the admiral.

At the rear troops.

Two Coalition soldiers fell wounded.

Others returned fire.

Then the Black Guard disappeared.

“Keep moving!” Tal shouted.

They turned west.

Heavy impacts came from ahead.

Iron Guard.

Tal’s colonel looked at him.

“They are driving us.”

“I know!”

“Toward what?”

Tal looked at the map.

Then he understood.

Command Central.

They were being allowed closer.

His enemy wanted him alive.

Tal’s expression hardened.

“Then we give them what they want.”

He drew his pistol.

“All guard units forward.”

---

Anton waited at the final junction.

Dax beside him.

Twelve Crimson Guards behind.

Anton checked his pistol.

Dax looked toward him.

“No killing the admiral.”

“I heard her.”

“No accidental killing.”

Anton frowned.

“That sounds insulting.”

“I have watched you fight.”

“That is fair.”

Footsteps approached.

Tal’s guard appeared.

Anton smiled.

“Admiral!”

Tal stopped.

The scarlet-eyed man standing in front of him looked nothing like the passenger shown in the original Coalition intelligence report.

Anton opened his arms.

“We have been waiting.”

Tal raised his pistol.

“Take him!”

The command guard charged.

Anton laughed.

The Crimson Guard moved.

The collision filled the corridor.

Armour struck armour.

Rifles fired at metres of distance.

Dax slammed his metal fist into a Coalition guard’s helmet.

Anton caught another soldier, turned him and threw him into two others.

Crimson Guards moved around their lord with frightening precision.

Tal fired twice.

Anton moved.

The shots missed.

Anton looked offended.

“You shot at me.”

Tal fired again.

Anton’s smile widened.

“Oh, good.”

“Anton!” Dax shouted.

“Alive!”

“Yes!”

Anton surged forward.

Tal’s final guards formed around him.

The first went down under Dax’s rifle.

The second lost his weapon to a Crimson Guard.

The third tried to tackle Anton.

Anton barely moved.

He grabbed the man and placed him against the wall.

Hard.

Then he looked directly at Admiral Tal.

Tal raised his pistol.

Anton’s crimson eyes burned.

“Captain Venn wants you alive.”

Tal fired.

Anton turned his head.

The round passed his cheek.

Anton slowly looked back.

His smile disappeared.

“That was rude.”

Tal fired again.

Dax shot the pistol from his hand.

Anton crossed the remaining distance.

One hand closed around Tal’s armour.

The admiral tried to draw a blade.

Anton caught his wrist.

For one dangerous second, everyone stopped.

Anton could have broken him.

Tal knew it.

Dax knew it.

The Crimson Guard knew it.

Anton stared into the admiral’s eyes.

Then he laughed.

“Lucky man.”

He released the wrist.

Turned Tal around.

And shoved him toward Dax.

“Captain’s property.”

Dax caught the admiral.

Tal struggled.

Dax’s metal hand closed around the back of his armour.

“Try it.”

Tal stopped.

Anton opened the channel.

“Sera.”

Her voice answered instantly.

“Yes?”

“We found your admiral.”

Sera stood from the command chair.

“Alive?”

Anton looked at Tal.

The admiral’s armour was damaged.

His pride considerably more so.

Anton smiled.

“Mostly.”

Dax shook his head.

“He is fine.”

Sera let out a long breath.

“Bring him to Command.”

Tal looked toward Anton.

“You think taking me ends this?”

Anton leaned closer.

“No.”

His crimson eyes burned in the smoky corridor.

“The fleet outside is what ends this.”

---

Minutes later, the command doors opened.

Dax entered first.

Admiral Tal walked beside him under guard.

Anton came behind.

Christer and Daniel arrived from separate entrances moments later.

Sera remained seated in the old sector command chair.

Joel stood beside her.

Tal looked around.

Four Hjertstedts.

Crimson Guard.

Iron Guard.

Black Guard.

Kestrel officers.

The mercenary captain he had dismissed.

Sera looked at him.

“Admiral.”

Tal straightened.

“Captain.”

“Sector Commander.”

Tal’s face tightened.

Sera smiled.

“Small correction.”

Joel laughed quietly.

Anton immediately joined him.

Tal looked toward Joel.

“You called your family because you could not hold the station yourselves.”

Joel shook his head.

“No.”

He pointed toward Sera.

“She held the station.”

Then toward Rook.

“He held the people together.”

Toward Dax.

“They held medical.”

Then toward the Guard formations.

“And they answered a reinforcement order.”

Joel stepped closer.

“We are simply making sure you understand the result.”

The tactical officer suddenly spoke.

“Captain.”

Everyone looked toward him.

“More gate activity.”

Tal’s face changed.

Sera looked at Joel.

Joel smiled.

Anton started laughing again.

Christer looked toward the display.

Daniel folded his arms.

Sera leaned back in her chair.

“Admiral Tal.”

Tal looked at her.

She pointed toward the growing gate bloom.

“I believe the rest of your problem is arriving.”

## 15. Surrender

The gate bloom grew brighter.

Admiral Voren Tal watched it from Kestrel’s command centre.

House signatures continued building outside.

His own fleet was breaking apart.

Some ships had lost drives. Others had lost weapons. Several transports were drifting under House guard.

His boarders were isolated across the station.

And he was standing inside the command centre as a prisoner.

Sera Venn sat in the old sector command chair.

Joel stood beside her.

Anton leaned against the tactical table.

Christer and Daniel remained near the rear of the chamber while Crimson Guard secured the doors.

Nobody rushed Tal.

Nobody needed to.

Sera looked toward the main display.

“Admiral.”

Tal did not answer.

Sera waited.

Finally he looked at her.

“What?”

“Order your forces to surrender.”

Tal laughed once.

A tired sound.

“You expect me to hand you the sector.”

“You already did.”

His expression hardened.

Sera pointed toward the tactical display.

“Your fleet cannot reach the station without crossing Hjertstedt fire.”

She selected the internal map.

“Your boarding force is cut into isolated groups.”

Another command.

“Your command shuttle is docked here.”

She looked directly at him.

“And you are standing there.”

Tal said nothing.

Joel folded his arms.

“You still have a choice.”

Tal looked toward him.

“Do I?”

“Yes.”

Joel’s voice remained calm.

“You can end this while most of your people are alive.”

Anton nodded.

“Good option.”

Joel glanced toward him.

“Helpful.”

“I am trying.”

Tal ignored them.

“If I surrender, the Coalition collapses.”

Sera leaned forward.

“The Coalition abandoned these people.”

“That was a military necessity.”

“You removed ships.”

“Yes.”

“Weapons.”

“Yes.”

“Food.”

Tal hesitated.

Sera saw it.

“You removed food.”

“We could not support the station.”

“So you took the supplies and left nearly five thousand people behind.”

Tal’s jaw tightened.

“The sector was collapsing.”

Rook stepped forward.

His old uniform was still black with smoke.

“We asked for evacuation.”

Tal looked toward him.

“Commander Rook.”

“Do not call me commander as if we are still on the same side.”

Tal’s expression hardened.

“You disobeyed evacuation orders.”

“You gave me room for two hundred officers.”

“We could not take everyone.”

“So I stayed.”

Rook pointed toward the habitation display.

“So did they.”

Silence.

Joel looked at Tal.

“This is the part where commanders decide whether pride is worth more dead soldiers.”

Tal stared at him.

“You speak as though your House has never sacrificed people.”

Joel’s crimson eyes brightened.

“We have.”

The answer came without hesitation.

“That is why I know what it costs.”

The room went quiet.

Anton stopped smiling.

Joel continued.

“Sometimes you lose people because there is no better option.”

He pointed toward the fleet display.

“This is not one of those times.”

Tal looked at his ships.

Coalition markers were scattered around Kestrel.

House frigates moved between them with disciplined precision.

The Oath cruisers remained at Hammer Range.

They were not firing continuously.

They did not need to.

Every time a Coalition ship tried to regain attack position, a House vessel crippled something important.

Drive.

Sensor mast.

Missile launcher.

Fire-control array.

Then it moved on.

The battle had become systematic.

Tal knew what he was looking at.

Defeat.

Not destruction.

Defeat was worse.

Destroyed ships became martyrs.

Captured ships became evidence.

Sera opened the communication controls.

“Give him fleet-wide transmission authority.”

The communications officer looked toward her.

“Captain?”

“Do it.”

A channel opened.

Tal stared at it.

Sera stood.

“You give the surrender order.”

“And if I refuse?”

Anton smiled.

“We go captain by captain.”

Joel nodded.

“And every captain who surrenders keeps prisoner protections.”

Daniel spoke from the rear.

“Every captain who continues attacking civilians gets documented separately.”

Christer added:

“That will matter later.”

Tal looked between them.

“You have already decided the legal outcome.”

Joel shook his head.

“No.”

“Then why the threats?”

“They are not threats.”

Joel pointed toward the communication console.

“They are consequences.”

Tal stared at him.

The gate flashed.

A new House cruiser emerged.

Then two more escorts.

The tactical officer whispered:

“Additional Hjertstedt ships entering.”

Anton looked toward Tal.

“This is becoming less complicated.”

Sera stepped away from the command chair.

She stopped directly in front of Tal.

“You wanted Kestrel because you could not control it.”

Tal said nothing.

“You abandoned the people because they were inconvenient.”

Still nothing.

“You came back when the station had value again.”

Sera pointed toward the channel.

“Now you can do one useful thing.”

Tal looked toward the station map.

Coalition troops were still fighting in four sectors.

Some were surrounded.

Some were wounded.

Some probably did not even know the admiral had been captured.

He slowly approached the console.

Dax released his armour but remained close.

Tal placed his hand against the communications surface.

“Fleet authentication.”

The station requested identification.

Tal entered his command cipher.

**VARDOS COALITION ADMIRALTY AUTHORITY CONFIRMED.**

A fleet-wide channel opened.

Tal hesitated.

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“Five credits he changes his mind.”

Joel looked at him.

“Not now.”

“Fair.”

Tal took a breath.

“This is Admiral Voren Tal.”

His voice went out to every Coalition vessel.

Every boarding company.

Every surviving command post.

“I have been captured aboard Sector Command Kestrel.”

The tactical display changed as Coalition ships acknowledged receipt.

Tal continued.

“The fleet action is lost.”

Several officers in the command centre glanced toward him.

That was not language admirals enjoyed using.

“All Coalition ships will cease offensive operations.”

One frigate stopped firing.

Then another.

A destroyer cut its targeting beams.

“Drives to safe output.”

“Weapons cold.”

“Boarding forces aboard Kestrel will cease resistance and await prisoner processing.”

Anton looked toward the internal station map.

Red contacts began changing.

One by one.

Some stopped moving.

Others dropped weapons.

A few continued.

Tal saw it too.

“To all local commanders,” he continued.

“This order is authenticated.”

“Further resistance is unauthorized.”

“Do not die for a battle already lost.”

Sera looked at Joel.

Joel gave a small nod.

Good order.

Tal finished.

“Admiral Tal out.”

The channel closed.

For several seconds, nobody moved.

Then reports flooded the command centre.

“Dock Ring Four surrendering!”

“Thirty-two Coalition soldiers disarming at Industrial Five!”

“Maintenance East requesting medical aid!”
