Unknown to everyone else.

Mostly.

The convoy crossed the station defence boundary.

Behind them, Kestrel’s House shield group remained in formation.

Six Vigil frigates.

Two destroyers.

An Oath cruiser.

Sanctuary support.

Safe space ended there.

Mara opened shipwide communications.

“All crew, convoy operations active.”

“Assault team to standby.”

Joel and Anton returned to their bunk room.

The moment the door closed, Anton looked at Joel.

“Soren recognized something.”

“Yes.”

“Us?”

“Maybe.”

“He looked at the armour?”

“He did not see it.”

“True.”

Anton sat on his bunk.

Then looked toward his armour case.

“You think we will need these?”

Joel looked at the tactical clock counting down toward first gate approach.

“Yes.”

Anton smiled.

“Good.”

Joel looked toward him.

“Do not sound happy.”

“I enjoy being prepared.”

“You enjoy wearing armour.”

“That too.”

The ship lights changed.

**GATE TRANSIT PREPARATION.**

Joel opened his armour case.

Not to put it on.

Not yet.

He checked the seals.

Power cells.

Weapons interfaces.

Helmet systems.

Anton did the same.

Across from them, two regular security crew watched in silence.

One finally spoke.

“What exactly were you two doing before this?”

Anton looked at Joel.

Joel looked back.

Then Anton smiled.

“Transport work.”

The security crew stared.

Joel sighed.

“You are getting worse too.”

Outside the hull, the first gate began to glow.

## 7. Full Combat Load

The gate filled the forward screen.

White light ran around the ancient structure.

The convoy held formation.

*Resolute Dawn* sat forward and slightly above Holt’s transport. The *Grey Nomad* remained close on the prisoner ship’s port side. Three other mercenary escorts spread behind them.

Captain Mara Vale watched the tactical display from her command chair.

“Gate weather?”

“Stable,” the navigator answered.

“Arrival envelope?”

“Normal.”

“Contacts beyond the gate?”

The sensor officer shook her head.

“Relay feed shows nothing unusual.”

Mara did not like that answer.

“Nothing unusual does not mean clear.”

Joel stood near the rear bridge wall.

He approved.

Anton stood beside him.

He was watching the gate.

Too closely.

Mara noticed.

“What?”

Anton looked toward her.

“Nothing.”

Joel glanced at him.

“That means something.”

Anton sighed.

“I dislike clean sensor pictures.”

Mara raised an eyebrow.

“You dislike good information?”

“I dislike information that looks too good.”

Joel nodded.

“So do I.”

The sensor officer turned.

“Our feed is authenticated.”

Joel walked closer.

“Authenticated by whom?”

“Local relay.”

“Live relay?”

A pause.

The officer checked.

“Delayed relay packet.”

Joel looked at Mara.

“There.”

Mara’s expression changed.

“How delayed?”

“Eleven minutes.”

Anton smiled without humour.

“Long enough to prepare a welcome.”

Mara immediately keyed shipwide communications.

“All hands, combat stations.”

The bridge changed.

Blue operational lights switched to red.

Weapon systems powered.

Point defence entered automatic standby.

Across the convoy, Holt reacted.

“Vale, explain.”

“Sensor packet is old.”

“We knew that.”

“You accepted it.”

Holt went quiet.

Mara continued.

“I am assuming contact on emergence.”

Holt looked toward someone off-screen.

Then nodded.

“Agreed.”

Joel turned toward Mara.

“Captain.”

“Yes?”

“Assault armour?”

She stared at him.

Anton’s smile returned.

Mara looked from one brother to the other.

Then toward the gate.

“Authorized.”

Anton was gone before she finished the word.

Joel remained still.

Mara looked at him.

“You are not excited?”

“Yes.”

“You hide it better.”

“Yes.”

He left the bridge.

---

The bunk room door sealed.

Anton already had half his armour on.

The reinforced leg sections had locked around his boots.

Dark plates climbed over his thighs.

Power-assist lines connected at the hips.

Joel closed the door behind him.

“You started before authorization.”

Anton looked at him.

“She was obviously going to say yes.”

“That is not the same thing.”

“It saves time.”

Joel opened his armour case.

The room became quiet.

Two regular *Resolute Dawn* security crew sat on the opposite bunks.

Neither had seen the suits assembled.

Not properly.

Joel stepped into the lower frame.

The armour woke.

Servos tightened around his legs.

Chest sections rose from the case and locked over his torso.

Black armour closed across him in overlapping plates.

Thin crimson lines came alive along the seams.

The suit adjusted automatically around his augmented arm.

A command interface slid over the dark metal and connected directly into the armour network.

His left gauntlet closed.

The fingers flexed.

Anton locked his chest plate.

His armour was heavier.

Broader.

Built for impact.

The shoulder sections were thick enough to turn him into something almost inhuman.

Deep crimson reinforcement ran across the black surface.

The regular security officer across from him whispered:

“That is boarding armour?”

Anton looked toward him.

“Yes.”

“For what?”

Anton considered.

“Everything.”

Joel looked at him.

“Not everything.”

“Most things.”

The helmet came last.

Anton lifted it.

The faceplate was black.

No eyes visible when sealed.

Only narrow crimson sensor lines.

He put it on.

The helmet locked.

The suit expanded slightly as internal pressure stabilized.

For one second Anton became completely still.

Then the external speakers activated.

“Comfortable.”

One security officer stood slowly.

He was staring openly now.

“You are wearing powered assault armour inside our ship.”

Joel lifted his own helmet.

“Correct.”

“You could tear through half these bulkheads.”

Anton answered:

“Probably.”

Joel turned toward him.

“You are making this worse.”

“I am being truthful.”

Joel sealed his helmet.

The world changed.

Information appeared across his visor.

Ship layout.

Atmosphere status.

Heat signatures.

Local crew identification.

Weapons locations.

Bulkhead integrity.

He immediately disabled three functions.

Anton noticed through the private link.

“You shut off structural penetration mapping.”

“Mara ordered us not to access systems beyond assault requirements.”

“It is useful.”

“It is unnecessary.”

“You are very obedient today.”

“We are crew.”

Anton laughed over the private channel.

“Ten credits I stop the first boarder.”

“Ten that you complain about where she posts you.”

“That is not a combat wager.”

“It uses the more reliable target.”

Anton checked the restraint packs on his belt. He left the disabled mapping function alone.

Joel locked his long-barrelled sidearm to his thigh.

A compact rifle attached across his back.

A short boarding blade locked against his chest.

Anton carried more.

Heavy pistol.

Compact breaching weapon.

Powered cutter.

Three restraint packs.

The security crew stared.

One finally asked:

“How many boarding actions have you two done?”

Anton looked toward Joel.

Joel opened the bunk-room door.

“Enough.”

---

They entered the assault staging compartment.

Conversation stopped.

Venn Korda stood with eight security personnel.

Everyone stared.

Joel and Anton entered fully armoured.

Black.

Crimson.

Large enough that the narrow military compartment suddenly felt smaller.

Mara arrived from the bridge.

She stopped at the doorway.

For several seconds, she said nothing.

Anton turned toward her.

“Captain.”

The voice coming through his armour speakers was deeper.

Cleaner.

More mechanical.

Mara walked slowly around him.

“This is what you were hiding in those bags.”

“Yes.”

She looked toward Joel.

Joel’s armour was slimmer but somehow more threatening.

Anton looked like a weapon.

Joel looked like command.

His helmet carried extra sensor assemblies around the temples. His augmented arm had become part of the suit. Thin crimson markings ran across one shoulder and disappeared beneath the black plate.

Mara stopped in front of him.

“You said House-pattern.”

Joel nodded.

She pointed toward the armour.

“I have seen House security troops.”

Joel said nothing.

“This is not standard House armour.”

Anton turned his helmet slightly toward Joel.

Joel remained still.

Mara continued.

“This is custom command equipment.”

“Yes.”

Venn looked between them.

“How high command?”

Joel answered:

“Higher than normal assault crew.”

Anton laughed.

Mara closed her eyes.

“Wonderful.”

A warning sounded.

**THROAT LOCK IN SIXTY SECONDS.**

Every joke vanished.

Joel turned toward Venn.

“How do you deploy?”

She pointed toward the deck map.

“Four at prisoner compartment. Four at main boarding corridor. You two stay mobile.”

Joel nodded.

“Good.”

Anton looked toward Mara.

“Rules?”

“Protect the prisoner.”

“Yes.”

“Protect the crew.”

“Yes.”

“No offensive boarding unless I order it.”

Anton hesitated.

Mara pointed at him.

“Say it.”

“No offensive boarding without orders.”

Joel looked toward Anton.

“Progress.”

“Silence.”

The ship shook slightly.

The gate began taking them.

---

The universe vanished.

Then returned badly.

The *Resolute Dawn* emerged under fire.

“CONTACT!”

The bridge transmission hit every helmet.

“MULTIPLE BLOOMS!”

The assault compartment shook.

Something struck the outer armour.

Mara’s voice came over shipwide comms.

“Point defence active! All ships separate!”

Joel immediately saw the tactical feed through authorized assault channels.

Contacts filled the arrival envelope.

Not wreckage.

Ships.

Cold-drifting hulls had been waiting close to the gate.

Their drives ignited only after the convoy emerged.

Anton looked at Joel.

“Good instinct.”

“Later.”

A second impact.

The lights failed.

Emergency red came on.

Venn shouted:

“Boarding alert!”

Joel checked the hull map.

Three objects were approaching.

Too small to be ships.

Too large to be missiles.

“Pods,” he said.

Venn looked at her display.

“They are moving for the prisoner deck.”

Anton raised his heavy weapon.

“Of course they are.”

Mara’s voice came over comms.

“Assault team, prepare for breach!”

Joel heard gunfire through the hull.

Point defence.

Close.

Knife Range almost immediately.

The convoy had emerged directly inside the attack.

Exactly what House gate doctrine warned against: arrival first, separation second, formation beauty last.

Joel looked toward Venn.

“Your team holds prisoner access.”

She nodded.

“And you?”

Joel looked toward Anton.

Anton was already smiling behind his sealed helmet.

“We stay mobile.”

The first boarding pod struck the ship.

The impact shook the compartment.

Another hit forward.

Then a third.

Emergency alarms filled the corridor.

**HULL PENETRATION — DECK FOUR.**

**HULL PENETRATION — DECK SIX.**

Mara’s voice:

“BOARDERS ABOARD!”

Anton started toward the door.

Joel caught his shoulder.

“Wait.”

Anton looked at him.

“For what?”

Joel checked the internal sensors.

Crew signatures.

Hostile shapes.

Unknown numbers.

Then another symbol appeared.

A coded tight-beam transmission from outside.

The *Grey Nomad*.

One line only.

**CONTACT. HOLD YOUR DECK. — C**

Anton laughed.

“Christer.”

Joel shook his head.

“He broke cover enough to message us.”

“Very responsible.”

A fourth impact struck the *Resolute Dawn*.

This one was different.

Much heavier.

The ship rolled.

Crew grabbed the walls.

Venn shouted:

“That was not a pod!”

Joel checked the hull.

Something had attached near the main airlock.

A boarding cutter.

Large.

Multiple life signs inside.

Anton’s crimson visor lines brightened.

“How many?”

Joel counted.

“Twelve.”

Anton raised his weapon.

“Now?”

Joel opened Mara’s channel.

“Captain.”

Gunfire sounded behind her voice.

“Talk.”

“Large cutter attached to primary assault corridor. Twelve hostiles minimum.”

“Can you contain it?”

Joel looked at Anton.

Anton tilted his helmet.

Joel answered:

“Yes.”

Mara did not hesitate.

“Then do it.”

Anton laughed.

“Authorization.”

Joel released his shoulder.

“Move.”

The two men went through the doorway.

Venn watched them disappear.

One of her security crew looked toward her.

“Should we follow?”

Venn listened.

Heavy footsteps faded down the corridor.

Then Anton’s powered breaching weapon activated with a low mechanical growl.

Venn shook her head.

“No.”

A tremendous impact sounded ahead.

Then another.

Gunfire erupted.

Then stopped.

The security officer stared.

“That was fast.”

Venn looked toward the prisoner compartment.

“Guard Soren.”

Another alarm sounded.

The convoy was still under attack.

Outside, ships fought to separate from the gate.

Inside, boarders were moving toward the most valuable prisoner in the sector.

And for the first time, the crew of the *Resolute Dawn* was about to see what Joel and Anton looked like when they stopped pretending to be ordinary mercenaries.

## 8. The Boarding Cutter

The boarding cutter finished burning through the outer hull.

Joel felt the vibration through the soles of his armour.

Anton stood beside him in the narrow corridor.

Twelve hostile life signs waited beyond the sealed bulkhead.

Mara’s voice came through the tactical channel.

“Assault team, status?”

Joel watched the cutter’s pressure rise.

“They are equalizing.”

“How long?”

“Seven seconds.”

Anton rolled his shoulders.

Powered joints answered with a quiet mechanical growl.

“Six.”

Joel looked toward him.

“No chasing.”

Anton sounded offended.

“They are boarding us.”

“No chasing outside the assigned section.”

“Fine.”

“Prisoners where practical.”

Anton looked at the sealed door.

“We will see.”

Joel drew his long-barrelled sidearm.

The weapon locked into his gauntlet interface.

Targeting marks appeared across his visor.

The boarders outside had no idea what waited for them.

They expected ordinary mercenary security.

Nine tired ship guards.

A captain with an old frigate.

A valuable prisoner.

Instead, they had chosen the corridor where Joel and Anton von Hjertstedt were standing in command assault armour.

Anton raised his breaching weapon.

“Door opens in three.”

Joel shifted half a step left.

“Two.”

The cutter blew the lock.

The bulkhead exploded inward.

Smoke and fragments filled the corridor.

The first boarder came through behind a heavy shield.

Joel fired once.

The round punched through the shield’s viewing block.

The man behind it dropped without taking another step.

Anton moved.

The second boarder entered with a rifle raised.

Anton hit him.

Not shot him.

Hit him.

One armoured fist struck the man in the chest.

The boarder left the deck and slammed backward into two of his own troops.

Armour cracked against armour.

The entire entrance clogged.

Anton laughed.

“Bad doorway.”

The enemy opened fire through the smoke.

Rounds struck Anton’s chest.

His armour flashed warnings.

Nothing penetrated.

He continued walking.

Joel fired twice.

Two rifles broke apart in hostile hands.

One boarder screamed as fragments struck his glove.

Another tried to fall back into the cutter.

Joel moved his aim away from the man and fired into the hatch controls.

The cutter door slammed shut behind them.

Anton looked toward Joel.

“You locked them in with us.”

“Yes.”

Anton’s laughter became louder.

The remaining boarders understood at the same moment.

They were not advancing anymore.

They were trapped.

One threw a grenade.

Joel saw the movement before the weapon left the hand.

“Anton.”

Anton caught it.

Mara heard him laughing over the channel.

Then he threw the grenade back into the cutter vestibule.

The explosion hit behind the boarding team.

Smoke and pressure rolled forward.

Three attackers went down.

Anton walked through the blast.

One hostile rose with a combat blade.

Anton caught his wrist.

The attacker stabbed with the other hand.

The blade struck the side of Anton’s helmet.

Anton slowly turned his head.

The knife snapped.

For half a second, both men stared at the broken weapon.

Anton grabbed the boarder by the front of his armour.

“Rude.”

He threw him across the corridor.

The man struck the wall hard enough to dent the panel.

Another boarder fired at Anton from three metres.

Joel shot the weapon out of his hands.

The man looked toward Joel.

Joel pointed his pistol at the floor.

“Down.”

The boarder hesitated.

Anton took one step toward him.

The man immediately dropped to his knees.

“Good decision,” Joel said.

Another attacker rushed from the smoke.

Anton drove a shoulder into him.

The assault armour amplified the impact.

The boarder folded over Anton’s shoulder and hit the deck.

He did not get back up.

Joel checked the tactical count.

Twelve initial.

Five incapacitated.

Two wounded.

One surrendered.

Four still moving.

“Four,” Joel said.

Anton looked toward the smoke.

“Mine?”

“Share.”

“Generous.”

The next two hostiles came together.

One carried a compact machine gun.

The other had a powered shield.

Joel stepped behind Anton.

“Forward.”

Anton lowered his shoulder.

The machine gun opened.

Rounds hammered his armour.

Warning markers appeared across Joel’s visor.

Surface damage.

No penetration.

Anton accelerated.

The shield carrier braced.

Anton hit him like a vehicle crash.

The powered shield failed first.

Then the man behind it.

Both went backward into the bulkhead.

Joel stepped around Anton and fired once.

The machine gun stopped.

The gunner collapsed behind cover, clutching a shattered forearm.

Anton looked toward Joel.

“You took mine.”

“He was shooting at you.”

“I was fine.”

“I know.”

“Then why?”

Joel moved forward.

“Efficiency.”

Anton laughed.

Two hostiles remained.

One ran back toward the cutter door.

The other activated a cutting tool and tried to force open a maintenance hatch.

Joel raised his pistol.

“Stop.”

Neither did.

Anton looked at Joel.

Joel sighed.

“Fine.”

Anton moved.

The fleeing boarder reached the cutter door.

Anton reached him first.

His armoured hand closed around the back of the man’s harness.

Anton lifted him completely off the deck.

The boarder kicked uselessly.

Anton turned.

“Where do you want him?”

Joel pointed toward the wall.

“Alive.”

Anton set the man down.

Then pushed him face-first against the bulkhead and magnetically locked his restraint cuffs to the armour plating.

The final boarder disappeared into the maintenance hatch.

Joel looked at Anton.

Anton looked back.

“No chasing,” Joel reminded him.

Anton pointed toward the open hatch.

“He is technically still in our section.”

Joel stared.

Anton waited.

Joel finally nodded.

“Ten metres.”

Anton vanished into the maintenance tunnel.

The corridor became quiet.

Joel looked around.

Smoke.

Broken weapons.

Damaged armour.

Groaning boarders.

One prisoner kneeling.

One restrained against the wall.

Nobody from the *Resolute Dawn* had been hit.

The entire assault had lasted less than ninety seconds.

Mara’s voice returned.

“Joel?”

“Secure.”

A pause.

“What does secure mean?”

Joel looked at the corridor.

“Boarding team neutralized.”

Another pause.

“All twelve?”

“Yes.”

“You had twelve?”

“Yes.”

“And you are both alive?”

Joel looked toward the maintenance hatch.

“Anton?”

A heavy impact sounded inside.

Then another.

Anton’s voice came through the private channel.

“I found him.”

Joel returned to Mara.

“Yes.”

Venn cut into the channel.

“Casualties?”

“Hostile only.”

“How many dead?”

Joel checked his display.

“Three confirmed. Several critical. Four conscious prisoners.”

Mara went silent.

Not because the numbers were high.

Because they were low compared with what she had just heard.

Twelve armed boarders had entered at Knife Range.

Two men had stopped them.

Without losing anyone.

Joel heard Anton returning.

The maintenance panel opened.

Anton emerged dragging the final boarder by one ankle.

The man was conscious.

Barely.

Anton dropped him beside the others.

“Ten metres.”

Joel looked at him.

“That was more than ten.”

“Eight point seven.”

“You measured?”

“Yes.”

Joel shook his head.

Anton looked around.

“Is that all?”

Joel checked the tactical network.

“No.”

Mara’s voice came through immediately.

“Second hostile team pushing toward prisoner deck.”

Anton picked up his weapon.

His armour was scratched now.

Black plating scarred by bullets.

One crimson shoulder panel had a deep gouge.

His visor remained intact.

“Excellent.”

Joel turned toward the prisoners.

“Venn, send security to collect these.”

“Already moving.”

Mara cut in.

“Joel.”

“Yes, Captain?”

She hesitated.

The bridge background was full of shouting.

Missile warnings.

Point-defence reports.

But when she spoke again, her voice was very controlled.

“What exactly did you two do in your former military careers?”

Anton laughed.

Joel looked toward him.

“Not now.”

Mara answered:

“That is becoming my least favourite answer.”

---

Three *Resolute Dawn* security officers arrived.

They stopped when they saw the corridor.

The first officer slowly lowered his rifle.

“What happened?”

Anton pointed toward the wrecked cutter hatch.

“They came in.”

The officer looked at the bodies.

The broken shield.

The man magnetically cuffed to the wall.

The damaged bulkhead.

“And?”

Anton shrugged.

“We disagreed.”

Joel walked toward the prisoner.

The surrendered boarder looked up at him.

His breathing was fast.

His face was pale.

Joel crouched.

“Who hired you?”

The man looked toward Anton.

Anton was cleaning blood from one gauntlet.

The prisoner looked back at Joel.

“I do not know.”

Joel believed him.

“Who gave you the ship layout?”

The man hesitated.

Joel noticed.

Anton noticed too.

Anton slowly turned.

The prisoner immediately spoke.

“We received it before launch.”

“From whom?”

“Anonymous packet.”

“Where?”

“Kestrel.”

Joel became still.

Anton stopped smiling.

The security officers heard it.

Everyone understood.

Someone had provided the attackers with the *Resolute Dawn’s* internal layout before the convoy left the station.

That meant the ambush had not begun at the gate.

It had begun before departure.

Joel opened the command channel.

“Mara.”

“Yes?”

“We need to change prisoner security.”

“Why?”

“The boarders knew the ship.”

Mara went silent.

Joel continued.

“They had deck plans.”

Venn swore.

Mara asked:

“How complete?”

“Enough to hit the prisoner route directly.”

The tactical display in Joel’s visor updated.

Another hostile group had broken through Deck Six.

They were moving.

Not toward engineering.

Not toward the bridge.

Toward Soren.

Anton raised his weapon.

“Captain?”

Mara answered.

“Yes?”

“Permission?”

Mara already knew what he meant.

Her voice came without hesitation.

“Full assault authority inside the ship.”

Anton’s crimson visor brightened.

“Thank you.”

Joel looked toward him.

“Controlled.”

Anton started walking.

“We have different definitions.”

Joel followed.

Behind them, *Resolute Dawn* security stared at the destroyed boarding team.

Ahead, another group of attackers was moving toward the prisoner.

The first team had expected resistance.

Instead, they had found a wall.

The second team was about to discover that the wall was coming toward them.

## 9. The Loudest Professionals

The second boarding team never reached Soren.

They heard something first.

Not gunfire.

Singing.

Bad singing.

Loud singing.

It came through the emergency channel from outside the *Resolute Dawn*.

Captain Mara Vale stared at the bridge speaker.

“What is that?”

Her communications officer adjusted the signal.

The singing became louder.

Several voices.

Someone was beating metal against metal in rhythm.

Then a man shouted:

“STOP THAT! WE ARE SUPPOSED TO LOOK PROFESSIONAL!”

Another voice answered:

“YOU BROUGHT US!”

Laughter exploded across the transmission.

Mara slowly looked toward the tactical display.

The *Grey Nomad* was coming alongside.

Fast.

Too fast.

Her pilot shouted:

“They are inside collision distance!”

Mara opened the channel.

“*Grey Nomad*, back off!”

Christer’s voice answered.

Calm.

Almost.

“Negative. We have detected hostile boarders inside your vessel.”

Mara stared.

“How?”

A different voice came through.

Daniel.

“Your hull has holes in it.”

Behind him, someone shouted:

“VERY TECHNICAL ANALYSIS, DAN!”

More laughter.

Daniel sighed.

Mara looked at her communications officer.

“Who are these people?”

The officer shook his head.

“No idea.”

---

Down on Deck Six, Joel stopped walking.

Anton stopped beside him.

Both were still wearing full assault armour.

The second hostile boarding team was ahead.

Nine contacts.

Moving toward the prisoner deck.

Then Joel’s helmet received a short encrypted burst.

**GREY NOMAD COMING ALONGSIDE.**

Anton saw it too.

“Oh no.”

Joel looked at him.

“What?”

Anton laughed.

“They brought troops.”

Joel checked the signal.

“How many?”

A second packet appeared.

He stared at it.

Anton waited.

“Well?”

Joel slowly looked toward him.

“Forty-eight.”

Anton started laughing harder.

“What?”

“Boarding team.”

“Yes.”

“That many?”

“Yes.”

Anton sounded delighted.

“Which team?”

Joel opened the attached personnel list.

His silence answered the question.

Anton nearly doubled over inside his armour.

“No.”

“Yes.”

“The loud ones?”

“Yes.”

“All of them?”

“Apparently.”

Anton slammed one gauntlet against the wall while laughing.

Venn came around the corner with four security officers.

“What is wrong with you now?”

Anton pointed toward the hull.

“Reinforcements.”

Venn checked her display.

“From *Grey Nomad*?”

“Yes.”

“How many?”

“Too many.”

Joel corrected him.

“Forty-eight.”

Venn stared.

“For one boarding cutter?”

Anton was still laughing.

“You have not met them.”

---

The *Grey Nomad* came alongside without docking.

A compact assault bridge extended from its lower hull.

Magnetic clamps struck the *Resolute Dawn*.

Mara saw the impact warning.

“Who authorized that?”

Christer answered over communications.

“No time.”

“You cannot simply attach your ship to mine!”

“We already did.”

Daniel’s voice appeared.

“Technically, that makes the debate late.”

Mara closed her eyes.

“These people know Anton.”

Her engineer looked at her.

“How can you tell?”

“Same disease.”

The airlock alarm sounded.

The temporary bridge pressurized.

Then the inner door opened.

Noise entered first.

Boots.

Weapons being checked.

Arguments.

Laughter.

Forty-eight mercenaries poured into the *Resolute Dawn*.

Their armour did not match.

Some wore heavy boarding plate.

Others wore stripped-down assault gear.

One had painted teeth across his helmet.

Another had a small metal bell hanging from his shoulder.

Several carried weapons that had obviously been modified far beyond manufacturer recommendations.

They looked undisciplined.

They sounded worse.

Yet they moved through the airlock in proper assault intervals.

Weapons remained down around friendly crew.

Fields of fire never crossed.

Their chaos had structure.

The first man through shouted:

“WHERE IS THE PARTY?”

Venn arrived just in time to hear it.

She stopped.

“No.”

The mercenary looked at her.

“No what?”

“No whatever this is.”

Another boarder leaned around him.

“We are reinforcements.”

“You are shouting.”

“Helps morale.”

“Whose?”

“Ours.”

Then Christer entered.

Plain armour.

No insignia.

No special markings.

Nothing that identified him as anything except an experienced mercenary captain.

The boarding team reacted immediately.

“Captain!”

“Boss!”

“Heron!”

One man shouted:

“YOU STILL OWE ME MONEY!”

Christer pointed toward him.

“You lost that bet.”

“You changed the rules!”

“You misunderstood them.”

Daniel entered behind him.

The reaction became worse.

Three boarders immediately started complaining.

“Oh, brilliant.”

“Ward is here.”

“I am not fighting him again.”

Daniel stopped.

“What?”

One boarder pointed at him.

“Last contract.”

“You challenged me.”

“You broke my shield.”

“It was a cheap shield.”

“It was new!”

Another mercenary shouted:

“You threw me through a cargo door!”

Daniel frowned.

“The door was open.”

“That DOES NOT HELP!”

Venn looked slowly toward Christer.

Christer looked tired.

“These are my best assault troops.”

Venn stared at the shouting formation.

“Best?”

Christer corrected himself.

“Most effective.”

“That is not the same thing.”

“No.”

---

Then Joel and Anton appeared at the far end of the corridor.

The boarding team went silent.

Completely silent.

Joel noticed.

Anton noticed.

Daniel definitely noticed.

One mercenary slowly raised a hand.

“Oh, come on.”

Another looked toward Christer.

“You did not say Hart was here.”

Joel stopped.

Hart.

Good.

They were maintaining the cover.

Christer shrugged.

“I did not know.”

A third boarder looked directly at Joel.

“I am not doing another breach race against you.”

Joel’s helmet turned toward him.

“You lost fairly.”

“You sealed the wrong door!”

“It became the right door.”

“That is exactly the kind of thing I mean!”

Mara had reached the corridor behind Venn.

She heard everything.

Her eyes narrowed.

Old contracts.

Old jobs.

Old competitions.

That was what it sounded like.

A strange group of veteran mercenaries who had crossed paths many times.

Nothing more.

Then one of the boarders saw Daniel.

He groaned.

“And Ward too.”

Daniel folded his arms.

“What did I do?”

“You know.”

“I genuinely do not.”

“You took our bridge in six minutes.”

Daniel considered it.

“Seven.”

“THAT IS WORSE!”

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“I like them.”

Joel replied:

“You would.”

One mercenary looked at Anton.

Then his armour.

Then his heavy pistol.

His expression changed.

“Oh.”

Anton tilted his head.

“What?”

The mercenary pointed.

“You are Berg.”

“Yes.”

The man looked toward the others.

“Berg is here.”

Several groans followed.

One boarding specialist actually sat down against the bulkhead.

Mara stared.

“What did he do?”

The seated man looked at her.

“Old contract.”

Anton smiled.

The mercenary pointed angrily.

“He boarded our ship through the waste-disposal lock.”

Anton looked proud.

“It was unguarded.”

“BECAUSE IT WAS THE WASTE-DISPOSAL LOCK!”

Joel started laughing.

Daniel joined him.

Christer tried not to.

Failed.

Mara stared at all four men.

She had never seen strangers look so familiar.

That bothered her.

But there was no time to investigate.

The ship shook.

Venn’s tactical slate flashed.

“Second boarding team moving!”

All humour vanished.

Instantly.

The loud mercenaries stood.

Weapons came up.

The transformation was unsettling.

One second they had been shouting about old contracts.

The next, forty-eight people were waiting silently for direction.

Christer looked toward Venn.

“Local command?”

She nodded.

“My deck.”

“Then tell us where.”

Venn pointed to the tactical map.

“Nine hostiles moving from Deck Six toward prisoner security.”

Daniel leaned closer.

“Secondary approach?”

“Maintenance Spine Three.”

He nodded.

“Obvious split.”

Christer pointed toward half his force.

“Twenty with Ward. Maintenance Spine.”

Nobody complained now.

Daniel moved.

Twenty mercenaries followed.

Christer looked at the rest.

“With me.”

Anton raised a hand.

“Can we come?”

Christer looked toward him.

“You are under her contract.”

He pointed toward Mara.

Mara realized Christer had deliberately made the distinction.

Good.

She looked at Joel and Anton.

“You stay assigned to my assault team.”

Anton sounded disappointed.

Joel nodded.

“Understood.”

Mara looked at Christer.

“You take the second boarding team.”

Christer smiled.

“Gladly.”

---

The attackers had no idea what was coming.

They moved down Deck Six in two groups.

Five forward.

Four behind.

Professional.

Disciplined.

Their mistake was expecting ship security.

Instead, the forward group rounded a corner and found twenty-eight shouting mercenaries charging directly toward them.

“THERE THEY ARE!”

“FIRST ONE DOWN OWES DRINKS!”

“STOP SHOOTING THE WALL!”

“I WAS AIMING THERE!”

The hostile team fired.

The mercenary formation broke apart.

Not in panic.

Deliberately.

Two went high.

Four moved behind cover.

Three threw suppression grenades.

Another team went through a side maintenance passage.

The attackers suddenly had fire coming from three angles.

Christer walked behind the first line.

“Alive where possible.”

One boarder shouted:

“BORING!”

Christer raised his rifle.

“That was not optional.”

“FINE!”

The exchange lasted twenty seconds.

Two attackers surrendered.

One tried to retreat.

A mercenary tackled him before he reached the hatch.

Another hostile raised a grenade.

Christer shot the hand holding it.

The grenade fell.

A boarder kicked it back into an empty maintenance compartment.

The door sealed.

Explosion.

Someone cheered.

Christer pointed.

“No cheering during containment.”

“After containment?”

Christer considered.

“Acceptable.”

The entire team cheered.

Venn heard it over communications.

She looked toward Mara.

Mara rubbed her forehead.

“They are effective.”

“Yes.”

“I dislike them.”

“Yes.”

Anton leaned closer.

“They grow on you.”

Mara stared at him.

“That is exactly what I am afraid of.”

---

Daniel’s group never gave the second half of the enemy force a fight.

They gave them a trap.

Maintenance Spine Three had three exits.

Daniel closed two.

Left the third open.

His mercenaries spread through access trunks.

The four attackers entered.

The lights went out.

A voice came from the darkness.

“Drop weapons.”

One attacker fired.

Twenty weapon lights appeared around them.

Another mercenary shouted:

“WARD, CAN I SHOOT HIM?”

“No.”

“He shot first.”

“No.”

“What if he shoots again?”

“Then maybe.”

The hostile soldier slowly lowered his weapon.

Daniel stepped into the emergency light.

“Good decision.”

One of his own boarders muttered:

“That is what Hart always says.”

Daniel heard him.

“So?”

“So last time Hart said that, you still attacked him.”

Daniel looked toward the man.

“That was a different contract.”

“You lost.”

Daniel’s face tightened.

“I know.”

The mercenary grinned.

“Badly.”

Daniel pointed down the corridor.

“Prisoners.”

The man kept smiling.

“Yes, boss.”

---

Back near prisoner security, Mara watched hostile contacts disappear from the internal display.

Nine.

Seven.

Four.

Zero.

She looked toward Joel.

“That was your friends.”

Joel answered carefully.

“People we have worked with.”

Anton nodded.

“Repeatedly.”

Mara looked toward him.

“They complain about losing battles against you.”

Anton shrugged.

“Training jobs.”

Joel added:

“Contract competitions.”

Mara stared.

“And Daniel?”

“Same.”

“And Christer?”

“Same.”

Mara was quiet for a long moment.

Then:

“You four keep ending up on the same contracts.”

Anton smiled.

“Small galaxy.”

Joel looked at him.

“Do not make it worse.”

A transmission arrived from Christer.

“Boarding team secure. Six prisoners alive. Three critical. Our casualties: one bruised ego.”

Someone shouted in the background:

“IT WAS A VERY EXPENSIVE HELMET!”

Christer continued.

“And one damaged helmet.”

Mara closed her eyes.

“Understood.”

Daniel’s voice followed.

“Maintenance Spine secure. Four prisoners.”

Another voice interrupted.

“WARD STILL LOST THAT BOARDING MATCH!”

Daniel terminated the transmission.

Anton started laughing.

Joel joined him.

Mara watched them.

Still suspicious.

But whatever these people were, they fought well.

Very well.

And right now that mattered more.

Outside, the convoy battle was still raging.

Several hostile ships remained near the gate.

Holt’s transport was damaged.

The *Grey Nomad* was attached to Mara’s hull.

Soren remained alive.

And the convoy had just acquired forty-eight of the loudest, least disciplined, most strangely competent boarding mercenaries Mara had ever seen.

Then the tactical officer spoke.

“Captain.”

Mara looked up.

“What now?”

“Hostile cruiser closing.”

Joel moved toward the display.

Christer’s voice came through again.

“Vale.”

“Yes?”

“We should probably detach.”

“Why?”

A new contact appeared.

Large.

Fast.

Heading directly toward the *Resolute Dawn*.

Christer answered:

“Because I think they are about to board you with something larger.”

Anton’s grin returned.

Across the corridor, several of Christer’s mercenaries heard him.

One groaned.

“Berg is smiling.”

Another checked his weapon.

“That means this contract is getting worse.”

A third laughed.

“No.”

He locked his helmet.

“It means it is finally getting good.”

## 10. First to the Bridge

The hostile cruiser came in hard.

Too hard for a ship that intended to remain at Hammer Range.

Captain Mara Vale saw the vector change on the tactical display and immediately understood.

“They are not trying to kill us.”

Joel stood behind her in full assault armour.

“No.”

Anton looked at the closing cruiser.

“They want to dock.”

Mara’s sensor officer shouted:

“Multiple launch doors opening!”

The tactical screen filled with contacts.

Boarding cutters.

Assault pods.

More than twenty.

Then thirty.

Venn Korda stared at the count.

“That is not a boarding team.”

Joel’s visor tracked the incoming craft.

“No.”

Anton sounded almost pleased.

“That is a boarding company.”

The *Grey Nomad* remained attached alongside the *Resolute Dawn*.

Christer’s voice came over the common channel.

“Vale, we count approximately one hundred eighty boarders.”

Daniel corrected him.

“Closer to two hundred.”

Someone behind Daniel shouted:

“THAT MEANS FIFTY EACH!”

Another voice immediately answered:

“NOT HOW THIS WORKS!”

Anton froze.

Then slowly turned his helmet toward Joel.

Joel knew that look.

“No.”

Anton opened the private channel.

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

Across the shared comms, one of Christer’s loud boarding troops shouted:

“TWENTY CREDITS HERON REACHES THEIR COMMAND DECK FIRST!”

Mara stared at the speaker.

Another mercenary answered:

“WARD!”

A third voice:

“HART!”

Then several people shouted at once.

“BERG!”

“NO, BERG BREAKS TOO MANY DOORS!”

“THAT MAKES HIM FASTER!”

Anton began laughing.

Joel closed his eyes behind his helmet.

Mara slowly turned toward him.

“What are they betting on?”

Joel answered:

“Old habit.”

“That did not answer me.”

Christer’s voice suddenly exploded over the channel.

“NOBODY IS BOARDING THAT CRUISER!”

Silence.

Then someone asked:

“Yet?”

Christer sighed.

“No.”

Daniel added:

“We are defending the *Resolute Dawn*.”

One mercenary sounded disappointed.

“That is much less interesting.”

Mara opened the channel.

“This is Captain Vale.”

The entire channel went quiet.

“If any person leaves my ship without authorization, I will personally lock them in the waste compartment for the remainder of the contract.”

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“I like her more every hour.”

Mara heard him.

“Berg.”

“Yes, Captain?”

“That includes you.”

“Understood.”

The first assault pod struck.

The ship jumped under their feet.

Then another.

Then five almost together.

Alarms screamed across the bridge.

**BREACH — DECK TWO.**

**BREACH — DECK FOUR.**

**BREACH — ENGINEERING ACCESS.**

**BREACH — CARGO SPINE.**

Mara pointed at the tactical map.

“Venn, prisoner level.”

“Moving.”

“Joel. Anton.”

Both looked toward her.

“Central transit.”

Joel nodded.

Anton raised his heavy pistol.

“Good.”

Mara switched channels.

“Heron.”

Christer answered immediately.

“Listening.”

“Take your people to engineering and cargo.”

“Understood.”

“Ward.”

Daniel responded.

“Maintenance routes and rear access.”

“Accepted.”

Mara studied the map.

Four groups.

Four directions.

Exactly what the attackers wanted.

Split defenders.

Overwhelm the prisoner deck.

Take Soren alive.

Joel saw the pattern too.

“They will have a fifth group.”

Mara looked at him.

“Where?”

“Not attached yet.”

Then the sensor officer shouted.

“Large cutter coming around our ventral side!”

Anton laughed.

“There.”

Mara pointed at him.

“Do not sound happy.”

“Sorry.”

The large cutter slammed directly into the *Resolute Dawn’s* lower command spine.

The impact threw crew from their chairs.

Lights failed.

Emergency red returned.

Mara grabbed the command rail.

“Damage!”

“Armour breach below bridge access!”

Joel looked at the internal map.

Forty-three new hostile signatures appeared.

Very close.

Anton’s visor brightened.

“They came for Command.”

Mara drew her pistol.

“Then we disappoint them.”

---

Central Transit became a battlefield in less than a minute.

Joel moved through smoke with four of Mara’s security personnel behind him.

Anton walked five metres ahead.

Hostile troops poured from a breached cargo intersection.

Fifteen.

Then more.

Anton raised his weapon.

“Drop weapons!”

They fired.

Anton sighed.

“Why does nobody choose the easy version?”

He returned fire.

The corridor became one-sided almost immediately.

Joel shot weapons, knees and shoulder joints.

Anton broke the line.

Where Joel created openings, Anton entered them.

One boarder fired a burst into Anton’s chest.

His armour took it.

Anton kept moving.

The attacker tried to retreat.

Anton grabbed the rifle and pulled.

The man came with it.

Anton turned and threw him into two others.

Joel fired past Anton’s shoulder.

A hostile machine gun stopped.

“Left.”

Anton turned.

Three boarders entered through a side hatch.

Anton laughed.

“You found more.”

One of Mara’s security officers whispered:

“They talk like they have done this together for years.”

The other answered:

“Apparently old contracts.”

Joel heard them.

So did Anton.

Neither corrected the assumption.

Good.

The cover remained intact.

Mostly.

---

Engineering was louder.

Much louder.

Christer’s boarding team had decided silence was optional.

“LEFT SIDE!”

“I KNOW!”

“YOU ARE SHOOTING THE WRONG LEFT!”

“THERE ARE TWO LEFTS!”

Christer shouted over them.

“There is one left!”

Someone answered:

“FROM YOUR POSITION!”

Despite the chaos, the attack collapsed.

Their formation looked loose.

Their execution was not.

Two teams pinned the enemy.

A third slipped through service access.

A fourth came over the upper catwalk.

The hostile squad found itself surrounded before it understood the manoeuvre.

Then one of Christer’s boarders shouted:

“HERON, HART ALREADY HAS NINE!”

Christer stopped firing for half a second.

“What?”

Another shouted:

“BERG HAS ELEVEN!”

Christer stared toward the ceiling.

“We are not counting!”

A voice answered:

“WE ARE!”

Mara heard the exchange from the bridge.

Her expression became increasingly suspicious.

“Why do they know Joel and Anton’s numbers?”

Her communications officer hesitated.

“Shared tactical channel?”

Mara looked unconvinced.

---

Daniel’s group was worse.

He had taken the maintenance route exactly as ordered.

Then his troops discovered the betting pool.

“Ward, Hart is ahead.”

“I do not care.”

“You are losing.”

“This is not a competition.”

“You said that last time.”

Daniel stopped.

“When?”

“Vesper contract.”

Another voice:

“No, that was Helios.”

“Vesper was the engine room.”

“Helios was the bridge.”

Daniel slowly turned.

Every mercenary suddenly became fascinated with the corridor walls.

Mara listened.

Old contracts.

Again.

Always old contracts.

Yet these men remembered Joel, Anton, Christer and Daniel with the kind of detail soldiers usually reserved for commanders they had served beside for years.

Not strangers.

Not occasional rivals.

Something deeper.

But the fighting gave her no time to ask.

“Ward,” Mara called.

“Yes?”

“Forty hostiles moving behind your position.”

Daniel checked his display.

“I see them.”

“Can you hold?”

Someone behind him shouted:

“FORTY? THAT PUTS WARD BACK IN THE RUNNING!”

Daniel closed his eyes.

“Yes, Captain.”

Mara heard him draw his weapon.

“We can hold.”

---

Then the enemy reached the bridge.

The lower command access exploded inward.

Smoke rolled across the deck.

Mara dropped behind her command chair.

Her pilot pulled a compact carbine from under the console.

The communications officer drew a pistol.

The first hostile soldier appeared.

Then stopped.

Joel stood in the doorway behind him.

His black assault armour was scarred.

Crimson lines glowed beneath the emergency lights.

Anton appeared beside him.

Behind them lay a corridor full of defeated boarders.

Mara stared.

“How did you get here?”

Joel raised his pistol.

“They were coming here.”

Anton looked toward the incoming attackers.

“And apparently we are too.”

The enemy command team entered.

Elite armour.

Heavy shields.

Breach weapons.

Forty soldiers had launched.

Twenty-eight had reached the command spine.

Mara looked toward Joel.

“Can you hold this?”

Anton laughed.

Joel answered calmly:

“Yes.”

Then the enemy charged.

The bridge became Knife Range in its purest form—smoke, impacts, point-blank fire, bulkheads shaking, everyone fighting for metres instead of kilometres.

Joel took the centre.

Anton took everything Joel did not.

A shield carrier rushed forward.

Anton hit the shield with both hands.

The powered frame screamed.

Then folded backward.

Joel stepped through the opening and fired twice.

Two enemy weapons died.

Mara fired from behind the command chair.

Her shot hit a boarder’s leg.

Venn arrived from prisoner security with six troops and struck the attackers from the side.

The enemy formation broke.

That should have ended it.

Then the channel erupted.

“HERON HAS TWENTY-THREE!”

“WARD HAS TWENTY!”

“HART?”

Someone checked.

“TWENTY-FOUR!”

Anton shouted over open communications:

“COUNT MINE PROPERLY!”

Joel turned his helmet toward him.

“Anton.”

“What?”

“You are using your real—”

Joel stopped.

Too late?

No.

The channel was chaos.

Mara was firing.

Nobody seemed to notice.

Anton immediately corrected:

“BERG. I MEANT BERG.”

There was a pause.

Then Christer’s entire boarding team burst into laughter.

Daniel joined them.

Joel actually lowered his pistol for half a second.

Mara stared at all four channels.

“You people are impossible.”

A surviving attacker tried to use the distraction.

Anton shot the weapon from his hands.

“No cheating.”

The man surrendered.

---

Nine minutes later, the last hostile squad aboard the *Resolute Dawn* put down its weapons.

Two hundred boarders had attacked.

Most were alive.

Many were wounded.

Dozens were prisoners.

None had reached Soren.

Mara stood on the damaged bridge looking at the internal tactical map.

Green again.

Mostly.

Joel removed his helmet.

Anton did the same.

Christer and Daniel arrived minutes later with their loud boarding teams.

The mercenaries immediately started arguing.

“Hart won.”

“No, Heron had more confirmed captures.”

“Captures count half.”

“Since when?”

“Since now.”

“Ward took an entire maintenance squad.”

“Without fighting!”

“That should count double!”

Anton pointed at everyone.

“I breached the command spine.”

“You did not breach it,” Mara said.

“The enemy did.”

Anton paused.

“That is technically true.”

One of the older Grey Nomad boarders held up a restraint pack.

“Six prisoners. No ammunition spent.”

Anton looked at him.

“They surrendered to you?”

“Told them they could come quietly or listen to the four of you argue.”

Christer nodded.

“Sound use of available weapons.”

Venn sent the veteran to help the wounded. He went at once, still grinning.

Joel folded his arms.

Mara watched them.

Christer.

Daniel.

Joel.

Anton.

Four supposed mercenaries from different backgrounds.

Four men whose movements fit together too well.

Whose troops argued about old battles against them.

Whose habits seemed less like random contract history and more like family rivalry hidden under new names.

Soren’s voice came through the prisoner intercom.

Calm.

Amused.

“Captain Vale.”

Mara looked toward the speaker.

“What?”

“You should ask them who normally wins.”

Everyone stopped.

Joel looked toward the ceiling.

Anton smiled.

Christer frowned.

Daniel became completely still.

Mara’s eyes narrowed.

Soren continued.

“Because men who merely shared old contracts do not remember one another like that.”

Silence.

Then one of Christer’s loud boarders whispered:

“Oh.”

Another answered:

“We may have talked too much.”

Christer slowly turned toward them.

“You think?”

Mara looked directly at Joel.

Joel smiled faintly.

“Captain.”

“Do not.”

Anton started laughing.

Mara pointed at him.

“Especially you.”

Outside, the hostile cruiser was still alive.

Damaged.

Close.

And now retreating.

One of Christer’s mercenaries looked at the tactical display.

Then at Joel.

Then at Daniel.

Then at Anton.

His grin returned.

“So…”

Christer immediately said:

“No.”

The mercenary pointed toward the fleeing cruiser.

“First to the bridge?”

Daniel looked away.

Anton started laughing again.

Joel sighed.

Mara stared at all of them.

Then she looked toward the enemy cruiser.

Her own expression slowly changed.

“Actually…”

Everyone went quiet.

Mara smiled.

“You wanted assault work.”

Anton’s crimson eyes brightened.

Joel looked at her carefully.

“Captain?”

She pointed toward the hostile cruiser.

“Prepare to board.”

The entire loud mercenary company erupted into cheers.

## 11. Race to the Bridge

The hostile cruiser was trying to escape.

Its engines burned hard across the tactical display, but the ship had taken too much damage during the ambush. One drive section was unstable. Two manoeuvring clusters were gone. Its point-defence network fired in broken patterns.

Captain Mara Vale watched it pull away from the *Resolute Dawn*.

“Range?”

“Hammer Range, opening slowly.”

“Can we catch it?”

Her pilot shook his head.

“Not alone.”

The *Grey Nomad* moved across the tactical display.

Christer’s voice came through.

“We can.”

Mara stared at the speaker.

Of course they could.

She looked toward Joel, Anton, Christer and Daniel.

All four were now standing on her damaged bridge.

Behind them waited nearly fifty loud mercenary boarders.

Several were still arguing about who had won the previous fight.

Mara pointed toward the cruiser.

“You wanted assault work.”

Anton smiled.

“Yes.”

“Take it.”

The room exploded with noise.

“YES!”

“BRIDGE RACE!”

“I HAVE TWENTY ON WARD!”

“HERON CHEATS!”

Christer turned around.

“Nobody is racing.”

The shouting stopped.

Then someone quietly asked:

“Can we race without calling it a race?”

“No.”

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“I think we should race.”

Joel looked at him.

“We are under contract.”

“So?”

“That means the captain decides.”

Mara looked directly at Anton.

“No racing.”

Anton nodded.

“Understood.”

Joel narrowed his eyes.

“You are still going to race.”

“I accepted the order.”

“That was not what I said.”

Anton lowered his voice.

“Cleanest capture, then. My sealed eastern reserve against your cellar choice. On her objectives.”

“After the logs are checked,” Joel said. “No changed routes.”

“Agreed.”

Christer held out his slate.

“Witnessed. I would also like the record to show neither of you asked what Ward is doing.”

Daniel looked up.

“I am in. Same stake.”

“You have not even seen the door,” Anton said.

“I can live with that.”

Before Anton could object, another voice came through the bridge.

Marek Soren.

“Captain Vale.”

Mara looked toward the prisoner channel.

“What?”

“You should speak with me before sending those men aboard another ship.”

Everything became quieter.

Soren continued.

“Because I know where I have seen them.”

Joel stopped smiling.

Daniel looked toward the speaker.

Christer’s expression became flat.

Anton looked interested.

Mara noticed all four reactions.

That was enough.

“Venn.”

Her security chief answered.

“Yes?”

“Bring Soren to briefing.”

---

Soren entered under restraints five minutes later.

Holt was not present.

Two security officers stood behind the prisoner.

Joel, Anton, Christer and Daniel remained against the far wall.

Mara sat at the head of the tactical table.

“Talk.”

Soren looked toward the four men.

Then smiled.

“Different names.”

Nobody answered.

Mara frowned.

“What does that mean?”

Soren looked at Joel.

“Joel Hart.”

Then Anton.

“Anton Berg.”

Christer.

“Chris Heron.”

Daniel.

“Dan Ward.”

Soren slowly shook his head.

“Very poor choices.”

Anton looked offended.

“Berg is believable.”

Joel looked at him.

“Not helping.”

Soren smiled wider.

Mara leaned forward.

“Where have you seen them?”

“Coalition intelligence archives.”

The room became silent.

Christer crossed his arms.

“Old contracts.”

Soren looked toward him.

“No.”

The single word changed the air.

“Classified military recordings.”

Mara looked sharply toward the four men.

Soren continued.

“Boarding actions. Fleet engagements. Security footage from captured stations.”

Daniel said quietly:

“You had poor intelligence.”

“We had expensive intelligence.”

“Same thing sometimes.”

Soren ignored him.

“I remember a station assault twelve years ago.”

His eyes moved toward Joel.

“One man directed three boarding groups through separate access levels without using local communications.”

Then toward Anton.

“Another entered engineering from the outside hull.”

Anton smiled.

“That sounds sensible.”

“It was rotating.”

“That makes it interesting.”

Mara looked toward Joel.

“You told me these were old contract competitions.”

Joel answered:

“They were contracts.”

Soren laughed softly.

“Technically true.”

Mara's eyes narrowed.

Soren looked toward Christer.

“And you.”

Christer said nothing.

“You were aboard a cruiser that lost its bridge.”

One of Christer’s loud boarders whispered:

“That sounds familiar.”

Christer turned his head.

The man immediately looked away.

Soren continued.

“Then Daniel.”

Daniel sighed.

“What about me?”

“You appeared on three different Coalition threat reports under three different identities.”

Daniel looked toward Joel.

“That seems excessive.”

Mara slapped one hand against the table.

“Enough.”

Everyone stopped.

She pointed toward Soren.

“Do you know who they actually are?”

Soren smiled.

“I have theories.”

Joel looked directly at him.

“Keep them.”

Soren’s expression changed slightly.

For the first time, the prisoner looked uncertain.

Not frightened.

Careful.

Mara noticed.

Interesting.

“You are afraid of him,” she said.

Soren looked at Joel.

“No.”

Then:

“I respect consequences.”

Anton began laughing.

Joel ignored him.

Soren continued.

“I do not know exactly who they serve.”

That was a lie.

Joel could hear it.

So could Daniel.

But Soren was choosing not to expose them.

For now.

“However,” Soren said, “I know one thing.”

Mara waited.

“These four men do not randomly meet on mercenary contracts.”

Anton looked toward Christer.

“Small galaxy?”

Christer shook his head.

“Stop.”

Mara stared at all four.

“We discuss this after the mission.”

Joel nodded.

“Fair.”

Soren smiled.

“If you return.”

Anton laughed.

“Now I like him.”

---

The assault began eleven minutes later.

The *Grey Nomad* accelerated first.

The *Resolute Dawn* followed.

The hostile cruiser fired its remaining weapons.

Missiles crossed Hammer Range.

Point defence answered.

The space between the three ships filled with light.

One missile struck the *Grey Nomad’s* forward armour.

Christer barely reacted.

“Damage?”

Daniel checked.

“Cosmetic.”

One of their mercenaries shouted:

“THAT WAS MY BUNK SECTION!”

Daniel corrected himself.

“Moderate emotional damage.”

The *Resolute Dawn* moved closer.

Knife Range.

Mara’s pilot fought the cruiser’s remaining guns while boarding cutters prepared.

Joel stood inside Cutter One.

Anton beside him.

Mara’s security team filled the rear.

Across from them, Venn checked her rifle.

“Objective?”

Joel answered automatically.

“Bridge.”

Venn stared at him.

“I was asking Anton.”

Anton looked disappointed.

“Also bridge.”

Mara’s voice came over the channel.

“Primary objective is command capture.”

Then more firmly:

“Engineering is secondary.”

“Nav-core third.”

“No reactor damage.”

“No unnecessary killing.”

Joel answered:

“Understood.”

Anton:

“Understood.”

Then Christer’s voice came through.

“*Grey Nomad* Cutter One launching.”

Immediately one of his mercenaries shouted:

“BRIDGE RACE STARTS NOW!”

Christer shouted:

“IT DOES NOT!”

Anton started laughing.

The cutter launched.

---

Their boarding craft crossed the last kilometres under point-defence fire.

Rounds flashed past.

The cruiser filled the forward viewport.

Armour.

Damage.

Burning thrusters.

Then impact.

The cutter slammed into the command-side hull.

Magnetic clamps locked.

Breaching arms extended.

Joel stood.

“Ready.”

Anton raised his heavy weapon.

The cutter cut through.

The inner armour broke.

Atmosphere blasted outward.

Then pressure seals engaged.

The door opened.

Enemy defenders waited.

Anton charged.

The first line lasted seconds.

Joel followed directly behind him, firing precise shots into weapons and armour joints.

Venn’s people entered after.

“LEFT!”

“CLEAR!”

“SECOND CORRIDOR!”

The cruiser’s interior became red emergency light and smoke.

Joel checked the map.

Bridge: four levels above.

Engineering: six below.

Another boarding signal appeared.

Christer’s team.

Then another.

Daniel.

Mara had authorized three breach points.

Joel smiled.

Efficient.

Anton looked at the map.

“Daniel is closer.”

“To engineering.”

“He can redirect.”

“No.”

“Christer?”

“Farther.”

Anton laughed.

“We are winning.”

Joel stared at him.

“There is no race.”

“Of course.”

They reached the first security junction.

Twenty defenders.

Joel stopped.

“Terms.”

Anton sighed.

Then opened his external speaker.

“Drop weapons!”

Nobody moved.

“We are taking your command deck.”

Still nothing.

“Anyone surrendering receives medical treatment.”

A hostile officer fired.

Anton looked toward Joel.

“I tried.”

Joel nodded.

“You did.”

The fight began.

It was brutal.

Fast.

One-sided.

Anton broke their centre.

Joel cut down resistance around him.

Venn’s team secured prisoners.

Three minutes later, the junction belonged to Mara.

They continued.

---

On another deck, Christer’s crew entered through a cargo access.

Immediately:

“HERON, HART IS TWO LEVELS AHEAD!”

Christer shouted:

“STOP TRACKING THEM!”

“They are nearly at Command!”

“WE HAVE OUR OWN OBJECTIVE!”

“What is it?”

“COMMAND!”

The entire team cheered.

Christer stopped.

He looked toward Daniel over the tactical link.

Daniel was laughing.

“This is your fault.”

“How?”

“You brought them.”

“They are your troops too.”

“Not today.”

---

Daniel had entered through maintenance.

He took one look at the route map.

Then quietly changed direction.

One mercenary noticed.

“Ward.”

“Yes?”

“Bridge is the other way.”

“I know.”

“Then where are we going?”

Daniel pointed toward the tactical map.

“Security control.”

The mercenary frowned.

“That is not the race.”

Daniel stopped walking.

“We secure our assigned route first. Then security control.”

“Right.”

“And nobody leaves a prisoner unsecured to improve my time.”

The mercenary checked the restraints being applied behind them.

“Understood.”

Daniel smiled.

“Besides, whoever controls security controls every bridge door.”

The mercenary stared.

Then grinned.

“Oh.”

Daniel continued walking.

“Now you understand.”

---

Joel and Anton reached the final bridge access.

The door was sealed.

Heavy.

Military.

Anton examined it.

“Finally.”

Joel looked at him.

“Do not.”

Anton placed the breaching tool against the centre.

“You know what I am good at.”

The charge fired.

The bridge door folded inward.

Joel entered through smoke.

Enemy command personnel stood behind consoles.

Several security troops raised weapons.

The hostile captain stood beside the command chair.

Joel raised his pistol.

“Drop them.”

The captain looked at Anton.

At the destroyed door.

At Venn’s troops behind them.

Then at Joel.

“No.”

His hand moved toward a command control.

Joel fired.

The console shattered.

Anton charged the security team.

The fight lasted nineteen seconds.

The hostile captain found himself on his knees.

Venn secured him.

Joel walked toward the command chair.

Then stopped.

A voice came through shipwide communications.

Daniel.

“Security network captured.”

Every bridge door behind them unlocked.

Anton looked toward Joel.

Joel looked toward the ceiling.

Daniel continued.

“Also, technically, I gained command-level access before you entered the bridge.”

Anton shouted:

“That does not count!”

Christer’s voice interrupted.

“We physically reached Command Two seconds after Hart.”

Someone behind him screamed:

“TWO SECONDS!”

Then another:

“WARD WON BY SYSTEM CONTROL!”

“NO!”

“HART WON PHYSICAL BRIDGE!”

“BERG BREACHED THE DOOR!”

Anton pointed at the ceiling.

“Exactly!”

Venn stared at them.

The captured enemy captain stared at them.

Joel closed his eyes.

“There was no race.”

Every mercenary channel answered together:

“Yes there was.”

---

Back aboard the *Resolute Dawn*, Mara listened to the argument.

Soren sat restrained across from her.

He smiled.

“You see?”

Mara looked toward him.

“See what?”

“They have done this before.”

The comms continued.

Christer complaining.

Daniel arguing technical victory.

Anton claiming breach credit.

Joel insisting nobody had been competing.

Their troops shouting over all of them.

Soren leaned back.

“Those are not men who occasionally shared contracts.”

Mara watched Joel’s helmet feed as he secured the enemy bridge.

“What are they?”

Soren looked at the screen.

Then at Mara.

“People your contract was never supposed to involve.”

Mara frowned.

“That is not an answer.”

“No.”

“Do you know?”

Soren hesitated.

This time Mara saw genuine calculation.

“Yes.”

Her hand moved closer to her pistol.

“Tell me.”

Soren smiled faintly.

“Not yet.”

Mara stared.

“Why?”

“Because if I say it now, your next decision will change.”

“What decision?”

Soren looked toward the captured cruiser.

“The one where you decide whether you still want them aboard your ship.”

Mara looked back at the tactical display.

Joel, Anton, Christer and Daniel had captured a cruiser in less than twelve minutes.

Most of its crew were alive.

Engineering remained intact.

The reactor was stable.

The nav-core was secured.

Exactly the kind of controlled capture experienced military forces practiced when ships were more valuable intact than destroyed.

Mara leaned back.

“They saved my ship.”

“Yes.”

“They protected Soren.”

Soren smiled.

“Unfortunately.”

“They followed my orders.”

“Mostly.”

Mara looked at him.

Soren laughed.

Then Joel’s voice came through.

“Captain Vale.”

Mara opened the channel.

“Report.”

“Hostile cruiser secure.”

“Casualties?”

“Manageable. Medical teams requested.”

“Prisoners?”

“Many.”

“Ship?”

Joel looked across the captured bridge.

“Worth keeping.”

Anton shouted behind him:

“I WON THE DOOR!”

Daniel answered on the private link.

“Keep the door. I will have the eastern bottle.”

“The logs are not checked.”

“I have the logs.”

Christer added, “That was the part you should have worried about.”

Joel sighed.

Mara almost smiled.

“Return when the prize crew arrives.”

“Understood.”

Soren watched her.

Mara ended the channel.

Then looked directly at him.

“You are right about one thing.”

“What?”

“They are lying.”

Soren nodded.

“But?”

Mara looked at the captured cruiser.

“But I am starting to think I want to know why.”

## 12. The Deal and the Hunter

The captured cruiser stayed alongside the convoy under prize control.

Its drives were dead.

Its weapons were safed.

Its surviving crew had been separated into guarded compartments while Mara’s medics treated wounded from both sides.

Joel and Anton returned to the *Resolute Dawn* first.

Christer and Daniel stayed aboard the captured ship with the temporary prize crew.

For the moment, the four men were still Hart, Berg, Heron and Ward.

Barely.

Mara Vale waited inside the briefing room.

Marek Soren sat across from her.

His wrists were still restrained.

Two guards stood behind him.

Soren looked far too relaxed for a prisoner whose transport mission had nearly turned into a massacre.

Mara remained standing.

“You said you know who they are.”

Soren smiled.

“Yes.”

“You said you would tell me.”

“I said I could.”

Mara’s eyes narrowed.

“That is not the same thing.”

“No.”

Soren leaned back.

“You are learning.”

Mara put both hands on the table.

“What do you want?”

Soren’s smile disappeared.

“A guarantee.”

“You already have prisoner protections.”

“From Holt.”

“You are his prisoner.”

“That is exactly the problem.”

Mara stared at him.

Soren continued.

“Garran Holt has a legal warrant to deliver me alive.”

“Yes.”

“He does not have a legal obligation to ensure I survive long after delivery.”

Mara said nothing.

“That relay is full of people I helped expose, betray or arrest.”

“You sound surprised.”

“I am not.”

“Then why should I care?”

Soren looked toward the observation window.

Outside, the captured cruiser drifted beside the convoy.

“Because I know things.”

“You have mentioned that repeatedly.”

“I know which Coalition intelligence cells survived Vardos.”

Mara’s expression did not change.

“I know who sold the convoy route.”

That did.

The room became colder.

Mara slowly sat.

“You know who leaked it?”

“I know enough to identify the channel.”

“Name.”

Soren shook his head.

“Guarantee.”

Mara stared at him.

“What guarantee?”

“That I reach Ardent alive.”

“You already will.”

“No.”

He leaned forward.

“I reach Ardent alive, I receive an open hearing, and nobody moves me into private custody before trial.”

Mara laughed once.

“You want me to guarantee judicial procedure at a station I do not control.”

“I want you to witness the agreement.”

“That has no legal force.”

Soren’s eyes moved toward the door.

“It may have more force than you think.”

Mara understood.

Hart.

Berg.

Heron.

Ward.

Whoever they really were.

Soren believed their presence changed everything.

“You think they can protect you.”

“I think people like them dislike hidden executions.”

Mara studied him.

“You are very sure about people you supposedly only know from intelligence files.”

Soren smiled again.

“Captain, I used to make a living understanding dangerous men.”

Mara leaned closer.

“Then help me understand them.”

Soren lowered his voice.

“They do not belong to four different mercenary careers.”

“I know.”

“They do not simply meet by accident.”

“I know.”

“Their troops do not complain about losing training games.”

Mara waited.

Soren looked directly at her.

“They complain like soldiers talking about commanders they have fought beside, fought against, and spent years trying to outperform.”

Mara slowly leaned back.

“Who are they?”

Soren smiled faintly.

“Guarantee first.”

The briefing-room door opened.

Joel entered.

Anton followed.

Both had changed out of full armour but still wore black under-suits beneath civilian coats.

Mara looked toward them.

Soren did not.

Joel stopped.

“You wanted us?”

Mara pointed toward the seats.

“Sit.”

Anton looked toward Soren.

“That sounds official.”

“Sit.”

Anton sat.

Joel remained standing for half a second.

Then sat beside him.

Mara looked between the three men.

“Soren wants protection.”

Anton raised an eyebrow.

“From us?”

“From whoever controls his trial.”

Joel looked toward Soren.

“Why?”

Soren answered:

“Because I prefer breathing.”

Anton nodded.

“Reasonable.”

Mara continued.

“In exchange, he claims he can identify the convoy leak.”

Joel became very still.

“Can he?”

Soren smiled.

“Yes.”

Joel looked toward Mara.

“What is he asking?”

“Open hearing at Ardent. No private transfer before trial.”

Joel considered it.

Soren watched him.

Anton watched Joel.

Mara noticed both.

Joel finally spoke.

“If Ardent accepts custody under published warrant and recognized judicial procedure, I will witness that transfer.”

Mara frowned.

“That is all?”

“That is what he asked for.”

Soren smiled.

“No.”

Joel looked at him.

“You asked for survival.”

“I asked for protection.”

“Different thing.”

Soren studied Joel.

Then nodded slowly.

“Fair.”

Anton leaned back.

“You are negotiating badly for an intelligence director.”

“I am restrained.”

“Excuse.”

Soren ignored him.

Then the ship-wide communication tone sounded.

Sharp.

Priority docking request.

Mara’s officer came over the channel.

“Captain, Holt’s transport is moving alongside.”

Mara closed her eyes.

“Of course it is.”

Joel looked toward Anton.

Anton smiled.

“This should be entertaining.”

Mara pointed at him.

“No.”

---

Garran Holt entered the *Resolute Dawn* like a storm.

Two of his security officers followed him.

The bounty hunter crossed the boarding collar without waiting for formal reception.

Mara met him outside the briefing room.

Holt stopped directly in front of her.

“You boarded an enemy cruiser.”

Mara folded her arms.

“Yes.”

“This was an escort contract.”

“Yes.”

“You launched an offensive capture.”

“They boarded my ship.”

“That does not authorize taking their cruiser!”

Mara stared at him.

“They tried to take my prisoner.”

“My prisoner.”

“Currently aboard my ship.”

Holt’s artificial eye shifted.

Then he saw Joel and Anton standing behind her.

His expression worsened.

“And you two.”

Anton looked innocent.

“Yes?”

Holt pointed toward him.

“I specifically said this was not a hero contract.”

Anton smiled.

“We were not heroic.”

Joel nodded.

“Mostly efficient.”

Mara looked at Joel.

“Do not encourage him.”

Holt walked past them into the briefing room.

Soren smiled when he entered.

“Holt.”

The bounty hunter stopped.

“You are enjoying this.”

“A little.”

“You nearly got an entire convoy killed.”

Soren tilted his head.

“I was restrained.”

Holt looked toward Mara.

“Why is he in a briefing room?”

Mara answered:

“Because he knows who leaked our route.”

Holt’s anger disappeared.

Completely.

“What?”

Soren looked pleased.

Joel noticed the shift.

So did Mara.

Holt stepped toward the prisoner.

“Who?”

Soren held up his restrained hands.

“Terms.”

Holt’s expression hardened again.

“No.”

Mara cut in.

“He wants guaranteed public transfer at Ardent. No private custody before hearing.”

Holt stared at her.

“You agreed?”

“I am considering witnessing it.”

Holt looked toward Joel.

“And him?”

Joel answered:

“I will witness lawful transfer.”

Holt’s artificial eye focused on him.

“You keep using that word.”

Joel raised an eyebrow.

“Lawful?”

“Yes.”

“You object?”

“No.”

Holt looked uncomfortable.

That told Mara something.

Soren saw it too.

“Interesting,” the prisoner said.

Holt turned sharply.

“Do not.”

Soren smiled.

Mara stepped between them.

“Enough.”

She pointed toward Soren.

“Name the leak.”

Soren hesitated.

Then looked at Holt.

“Your contract packet was clean.”

Holt remained silent.

“Kestrel traffic control was clean.”

Joel’s expression tightened.

Soren continued.

“The leak happened after convoy formation was finalized.”

Mara frowned.

“Where?”

“Someone copied the internal transfer amendment.”

Holt looked toward her.

“The prisoner transfer.”

Mara understood.

The change placing Soren aboard the *Resolute Dawn*.

That information had existed for less than an hour before departure.

Very few people had access.

Soren continued.

“The attackers knew my exact deck route.”

Joel nodded.

“We already confirmed that.”

“Yes.”

“But the captured cruiser carried updated layout data.”

Everyone looked at him.

Soren smiled.

“How do you know?”

Joel said nothing.

Mara narrowed her eyes.

“You already checked.”

Anton leaned toward Joel.

“Technically Daniel checked.”

Holt turned.

“Daniel?”

Anton froze.

Joel stared at him.

Mara slowly looked toward Anton.

Anton cleared his throat.

“Dan.”

“Dan Ward.”

Mara’s expression became flat.

“Right.”

Anton smiled weakly.

Holt looked between them.

“You people are terrible undercover operators.”

Joel sighed.

“Today has been difficult.”

Soren laughed.

Then the briefing-room door opened again.

Christer entered.

Daniel followed.

Both had returned from the captured cruiser.

Daniel carried a data slate.

Mara pointed at them.

“Perfect.”

Christer stopped.

“That tone is worrying.”

Daniel looked toward Soren.

Then Holt.

Then Mara.

“What happened?”

Anton answered:

“I almost used your real name.”

Daniel stared at him.

“You what?”

Joel cut in.

“Later.”

Daniel handed the slate to Mara.

“We found the attack orders.”

Mara opened the file.

Encrypted route maps appeared.

Convoy formation.

Ship capabilities.

The *Resolute Dawn’s* internal prisoner path.

And one attached source marker.

Daniel pointed.

“That is where it gets interesting.”

Holt leaned closer.

Mara read the identifier.

Then looked at Soren.

Soren’s smile had vanished.

“What?” Holt asked.

Mara turned the slate around.

The source packet carried a valid judicial security signature.

Not Kestrel.

Not Coalition.

Ardent.

The destination.

Silence filled the room.

Anton slowly smiled.

“So the people waiting to receive the prisoner…”

Joel finished:

“…may be the same people who tried to take him.”

Holt stared at the data.

“No.”

Soren leaned back.

“Now you understand why I wanted guarantees.”

Mara looked toward him.

“You knew.”

“I suspected.”

“You let us fly toward them.”

“I had limited alternatives.”

Holt grabbed the edge of the table.

“My warrant came from Ardent.”

Soren looked at him.

“Yes.”

The bounty hunter stared at the prisoner.

Then at the captured attack orders.

His anger became something else.

Cold.

Professional.

Dangerous.

Joel watched him carefully.

Holt finally spoke.

“We are not delivering him there.”

Soren smiled faintly.

“Now we agree.”

Mara looked around the room.

A bounty hunter whose employer might be compromised.

A prisoner who knew too much.

Four fake mercenaries whose shared history was collapsing by the minute.

A captured cruiser.

A convoy still damaged from battle.

And somewhere ahead, Ardent Judicial Relay waited for a prisoner its own security network may have tried to steal.

Anton broke the silence.

“So.”

Joel looked at him.

“No.”

“I have not said anything.”

“You are about to suggest something aggressive.”

Anton smiled.

“We have a cruiser now.”

Christer nodded slowly.

“That is true.”

Daniel looked toward the captured vessel on the tactical display.

Mara stared at all four.

“You are all thinking the same thing.”

Joel answered:

“Probably.”

Holt looked suspicious.

“What?”

Joel enlarged the route map.

“Ardent expects a damaged convoy.”

Anton stepped beside him.

“A frightened bounty hunter.”

Christer joined them.

“A valuable prisoner.”

Daniel completed the circle.

“And no reason to believe we know they are compromised.”

Soren slowly smiled.

Mara looked at the map.

Then at Joel.

“You want to continue.”

Joel nodded.

“Yes.”

Holt stared.

“Into a trap.”

Joel looked toward him.

“No.”

His eyes hardened.

“Into their trap.”

## 13. The Trojan Horse

No one spoke for several seconds.

Ardent Judicial Relay glowed on the tactical map.

Officially, it was a neutral legal station.

A place where prisoners were transferred.

Warrants were authenticated.

Trials were held.

Mercenaries were paid.

Convoys entered under protection.

But the captured attack files had changed everything.

Ardent security codes had been used to target the *Resolute Dawn*.

The people waiting to receive Marek Soren had helped prepare the ambush.

Captain Mara Vale looked at Joel.

“You still want to continue toward Ardent.”

“Yes.”

“Knowing they are corrupt.”

“Yes.”

Garran Holt folded his arms.

“That station has nearly forty thousand people aboard.”

Joel looked toward him.

“I know.”

“Security personnel. Judges. Dock workers. Families. Traders.”

“I know.”

“You cannot treat the entire station as hostile.”

Joel’s expression became colder.

“I did not say I would.”

Anton smiled.

That smile worried Mara.

Holt continued.

“Then what exactly are you planning?”

Joel looked toward the tactical map.

“First, we stop pretending.”

Anton started laughing.

Christer smiled.

Daniel quietly removed the false identification badge from his coat.

Mara stared at them.

Joel reached beneath his civilian jacket.

He removed the temporary identification chip marked:

**JOEL HART — ASSAULT SPECIALIST.**

He placed it on the table.

Anton did the same.

**ANTON BERG — BREACH SPECIALIST.**

Christer dropped:

**CHRIS HERON.**

Daniel added:

**DAN WARD.**

Four false identities sat beside one another.

Soren looked amused.

Mara did not.

Joel straightened.

“My name is Joel von Hjertstedt.”

Anton’s eyes slowly turned crimson.

“Anton von Hjertstedt.”

Christer nodded.

“Christer von Hjertstedt.”

Daniel finished:

“Daniel von Hjertstedt.”

Holt stared.

Mara did not move.

She had suspected something.

Not this.

Joel looked directly at her.

“I am High Marshal of House von Hjertstedt.”

Mara slowly sat down.

Anton raised one hand.

“Breach Commander.”

Christer and Daniel identified their own senior House authority.

Holt looked around the room.

“You put four senior Hjertstedt commanders inside my prisoner convoy.”

Joel corrected him.

“You hired mercenaries.”

“I hired a disaster.”

Anton nodded.

“More accurate.”

Mara looked toward Joel.

“You joined my assault team.”

“Yes.”

“You slept in my crew bunks.”

“Yes.”

“You let me order you around.”

Anton laughed.

“That part was excellent.”

Joel looked at him.

“Not helping.”

Mara stared at all four.

Then Soren began laughing.

Quietly.

“I knew it.”

Anton looked at him.

“How much?”

“Enough.”

“You could have said something.”

Soren looked toward Joel.

“I enjoy remaining alive.”

Reasonable.

Then Joel’s expression changed.

The humour disappeared.

“So does everyone aboard Ardent who had nothing to do with this.”

That silenced the room.

Joel enlarged the station.

“The corrupt people inside Ardent used legal authority to arrange an illegal seizure.”

Anton’s crimson eyes burned brighter.

House von Hjertstedt could tolerate enemies.

Pirates.

Raiders.

Rival mercenaries.

Open war.

Corruption was different.

Corruption hid predation behind law.

The House built its legitimacy on the opposite idea: violence had to be witnessed, bounded, and accountable.

Joel looked toward Holt.

“That warrant of yours was used as bait.”

Holt’s jaw tightened.

“Yes.”

“That means someone inside Ardent believes law belongs to whoever controls the paperwork.”

Anton laughed once.

Coldly.

“We dislike that.”

Daniel nodded.

“Strongly.”

Christer looked toward the station marker.

“Very strongly.”

Mara studied them.

“You are going to take the station.”

Joel shook his head.

“No.”

“Then what?”

“We are going to give the honest people aboard Ardent the opportunity to keep it.”

---

Joel opened an encrypted House channel.

The civilian communication system vanished.

A dark command interface replaced it.

Mara watched biometric layers unlock one after another.

**HOUSE COMMAND AUTHORITY VERIFIED.**

**HIGH MARSHAL PRIORITY.**

Holt stared.

“That is considerably more serious than your passenger identification.”

Joel ignored him.

“Nearest battle group.”

The network searched.

A response appeared.

**BANNER GROUP VARGSKOLD.**

Distance:

One short lane deviation.

Joel opened the channel.

“Vargskold Actual.”

A woman answered immediately.

“High Marshal.”

Mara looked toward Joel.

No hesitation.

No verification question.

No uncertainty.

The voice knew exactly who he was.

“Status?”

“One Oath cruiser. Four Vigil frigates. Two destroyers. Sanctuary support. One assault carrier.”

Joel looked toward Anton.

Anton smiled.

“Useful.”

Joel continued.

“Rendezvous coordinates transmitted. Full emission control.”

“Threat?”
