Primarch Lars von Hjertstedt appeared.

Old.

Grey.

Bald.

Still powerful enough that every bridge officer near the family command display straightened without being ordered.

Lars had been watching the same tactical feeds.

The Husk Guard.

The Seraph fleet.

The gate queue.

The open invasion route.

His expression was completely calm.

That frightened Joel more than anger would have.

“Family command,” Lars said.

“Report.”

Annette answered.

“Full Excision status. Central secured and claimed. Bastion nearing resolution. Gatehold under active Seraph transit assault. Multiple major human fleets now engaged.”

Lars looked toward the gate.

Then at the red enemy markers multiplying across Ardent.

“Understood.”

He opened another House channel.

The authorization code that appeared was so old that several bridge officers did not recognize it.

Joel did.

Anton did.

Christer did.

Daniel did.

Annette certainly did.

Joel stared at Lars.

“Father.”

“Yes.”

“You are authorizing that?”

“Yes.”

Lars looked directly into the command feed.

“Remove the limiters.”

Silence.

Then Kira confirmed:

“Primarch-level authorization accepted.”

Lucy followed:

“House combat inhibition protocols released for all authorized bloodline personnel and compatible specialist forces.”

Across the three fronts, House systems changed.

Medical governors disengaged.

Neuromuscular safeties opened.

Reaction filters dropped.

Combat implants moved from protected operating limits into unrestricted war mode.

Pain suppression increased.

Reflex assistance accelerated.

Adrenal control systems released levels normally forbidden outside existential combat.

For ordinary House operations, limiters existed for a reason.

Without them, the body could damage itself.

Muscles could tear.

Joints could fail.

The mind could move faster than fear.

But for a short period—

House warriors became something else.

Joel felt it first.

The world slowed.

Not truly.

His mind simply accelerated.

Every tactical icon became easier to track.

Every officer movement became obvious.

Every hesitation around him became painfully slow.

His eyes burned.

Mara Vale looked at him.

Then took one step backward.

Joel’s eyes were no longer simply crimson.

They were screaming red.

Bright enough to look almost illuminated.

Mara whispered:

“What the hell?”

Across Bastion, Anton’s eyes did the same.

He stopped laughing.

That was worse.

He walked through the captured capital’s corridor with terrifying speed, placing shots before defenders seemed to understand he had moved.

Christer stood on his command deck and began issuing orders faster than his bridge crew could repeat them.

“Cruiser Three turn seven degrees.”

“Destroyer group break left.”

“Boarding teams advance now.”

“Fire spinal.”

Every order landed exactly where it needed to.

At Gatehold, Daniel suddenly stopped breathing heavily.

Dennis looked toward him.

Daniel’s eyes burned red through the smoke.

Dennis nodded once.

“There you are.”

Daniel grinned.

“Try to keep up.”

Dennis actually laughed.

At Bastion, the effect was immediate.

Anton’s boarding teams smashed through the remaining Seraph-held command section.

Christer’s fleet tightened its formation and hit the Seraph screen with near-perfect timing.

A Seraph cruiser tried to withdraw.

Three House ships fired almost simultaneously.

Its drives vanished.

Another turned toward Bastion.

Anton’s assault craft were already moving before its turn completed.

Christer opened the family channel.

“Bastion resolution estimate: eleven minutes.”

Joel answered.

“Make it eight.”

Anton replied:

“Seven.”

Annette looked between them.

“You are all going to medical afterward.”

Nobody answered.

“That was not optional.”

Joel turned toward his own fleet.

Central was secured.

House banners now hung across the station.

Only the necessary garrison would remain.

The rest was already forming around his capital ship.

Heavy engines lit.

Mara looked through the command windows as ships turned toward Gatehold.

“You are leaving Central.”

“I am reinforcing Gatehold.”

“With this much force?”

Joel looked at the tactical display.

Imperium ships were already under Seraph fire.

Zenith formations were taking losses.

Daniel was holding the gate from inside.

Five hostile transits were still queued.

“Yes.”

Mara looked at his burning red eyes again.

Joel noticed her staring.

“The eyes?”

“Yes.”

“They stop.”

“When?”

“When the limiters come back.”

“That was not comforting.”

Joel almost smiled.

Then Lars gave the final order across every House combat channel.

“Ardent Excision Forces.”

Thousands listened.

“Gatehold is the road.”

“If the road falls, the enemy enters human space freely.”

“Bastion forces will finish their task and move.”

“Central forces will reinforce.”

“Gatehold will hold.”

Lars’s own eyes had begun glowing crimson.

“Full efficiency authorized.”

“Do not waste it.”

The channel closed.

Joel pointed toward Gatehold.

“Fleet.”

His officers looked toward him.

Several already had red eyes of their own.

“Move.”

The House formation accelerated.

Behind them, Central remained under House protection.

Ahead, Gatehold burned.

And across Ardent, people unfamiliar with House von Hjertstedt were about to discover why its warriors normally fought with limiters.

---

## 6. Bastion Falls in Seven Minutes

The timer began when Joel said it.

SEVEN MINUTES.

Anton saw the number on his visor.

Christer saw it on his fleet display.

Neither questioned it.

Bastion was still fighting.

Barely.

The limiter release had changed the rhythm of the battle. House crews were no longer merely faster. They were synchronized in a way that frightened even experienced allied observers.

Orders became movement almost instantly.

Movement became fire.

Fire became results.

At Bastion, Christer stood over the tactical table with eyes burning red.

“Cruiser group two, cut their retreat.”

“Destroyer four, thirty degrees down-plane.”

“Spinal battery, fire on my mark.”

He did not pause between commands.

His bridge crew almost struggled to keep up.

The Seraph cruiser ahead began turning.

Christer spoke before its maneuver completed.

“Now.”

The spinal gun fired.

The shot struck directly through the Seraph ship’s drive section.

Its engines died.

Three House destroyers were already converging before the wreck began to drift.

“Board it,” Christer ordered.

“Preserve command core.”

“Next.”

Inside Bastion’s captured capital, Anton moved through the final command decks like a weapon with a human face.

His crimson eyes shone through smoke.

A Husk Guard came around a bulkhead.

Anton fired into the knee.

Pivoted.

Second shot through the exposed neck seam.

Moved before it hit the deck.

Two experimental soldiers tried to flank him.

The first lost its weapon arm.

The second took a shot through the support frame and collapsed alive.

Anton did not even look back.

“Prisoner.”

A marine dragged it away.

“Still counting?”

Anton asked.

“Always!”

“Good.”

The next blast door opened.

Behind it stood Bastion’s surviving Seraph command group.

One true Seraph commander.

Three converted officers.

Six Husk Guard.

Anton smiled.

“Finally.”

The commander spoke through a human translator grafted into its throat.

“You are burning yourselves.”

Anton’s smile widened.

“Temporary problem.”

Then he attacked.

FIVE MINUTES.

Christer’s fleet broke Bastion’s outer screen.

House cruisers pushed through wreckage and missile fire.

One Ardent cruiser surrendered.

Another tried to flee.

Christer let it.

His tactical officer looked surprised.

“Sir?”

“It is broadcasting surrender codes.”

“But it is leaving the battlespace.”

“Then it can leave.”

The Seraph ships received no such mercy.

A black-gold boarding carrier turned toward the station.

Christer immediately marked it.

“Kill engines.”

Four batteries fired.

Its drives vanished.

“Hunter teams board.”

The order was already moving before he finished speaking.

In Sol, the war was watched from Luna.

The emergency diplomatic chamber had gone strangely quiet.

Delegates from the major human powers stood around the central tactical projection while Bastion telemetry streamed across the room.

Imperium representatives.

Terran officials.

Hiigaran officers.

Zenith observers.

Others from smaller human states.

All had seen House von Hjertstedt fight before.

Not like this.

At the center stood Lars.

His hands rested behind his back.

His eyes burned crimson.

Not metaphorically.

Not reflected light.

Deep red, almost luminous.

The Terran chairwoman stared for several seconds before she caught herself.

The Hiigaran admiral did not hide his discomfort.

A Zenith delegate whispered something to an aide.

The Imperium representative finally spoke.

“Primarch.”

Lars looked toward him.

The man paused.

Those eyes were worse when they focused directly on someone.

“What precisely did you authorize?”

“Removal of combat limiters.”

The representative looked back toward the battle feed.

House reaction times were visible even in fleet-scale telemetry.

Orders and maneuvers were occurring with brutal precision.

“Your people fight like this normally?”

“No.”

That answer did not help.

The Terran chairwoman asked:

“And the physiological effect?”

Lars’s eyes seemed to flare brighter as another Bastion objective turned green.

“It passes.”

The Hiigaran admiral frowned.

“At what cost?”

Lars answered without emotion.

“Medical recovery.”

No one spoke for a moment.

A smaller-state delegate finally said what several were thinking.

“That is terrifying.”

Lars looked back toward Bastion.

“Yes.”

He offered no apology.

THREE MINUTES.

Anton reached the Seraph commander.

The creature moved fast.

Anton moved faster.

Its blade cut across his shoulder plate.

He stepped inside the strike and drove his pistol into the armor beneath its ribs.

One shot.

The Seraph staggered.

Anton grabbed its throat and slammed it against the command console.

“Where are the transit reserves?”

The creature hissed.

Anton’s eyes burned brighter.

“Wrong answer.”

He fired again.

The commander collapsed.

Behind him, the last converted officer surrendered.

Anton looked around.

“Command deck?”

A Grey Nomad marine checked.

“Clear!”

“Labs?”

“Hunter teams moving.”

“Reactor?”

“Secure.”

“Station command?”

“Breaking.”

Anton opened the family channel.

“Joel.”

Joel answered from his moving fleet.

“Yes?”

“Bastion is almost boring now.”

Christer cut in.

“You have not finished.”

Anton checked the timer.

TWO MINUTES.

“Fine.”

Christer ended the fleet battle with one coordinated strike.

The last organized Seraph formation tried to retreat behind Bastion.

House destroyers blocked the lower vector.

Cruisers sealed the upper.

Anton’s captured capital, now under partial House control, brought surviving batteries online.

Christer saw the alignment.

“Fire.”

The surviving House line erupted.

Three Seraph ships lost drives within seconds.

One surrendered Ardent escort went dark and drifted away.

The final hostile cruiser tried to accelerate through the gap.

A Vigil frigate cut across its bow and forced it back into House guns.

Its weapons stopped firing.

Christer checked the tactical display.

No organized hostile fleet formation remained.

Only isolated contacts.

Boarding actions.

Station compartments.

Cleanup.

He opened Bastion-wide command.

“Ardent Bastion military command is terminated.”

“Remaining Ardent personnel may surrender.”

“Seraph forces receive no passage.”

“House forces hold.”

The timer continued.

00:41

Anton’s voice came through.

“Show-off.”

Christer smiled.

“Station?”

Anton checked.

The final Bastion command bunker had just surrendered.

“Secured.”

“Labs?”

“Hunter teams have them.”

“Seraph commander?”

“Dead.”

Christer looked at the clock.

00:23

He opened the family channel.

“Joel.”

Joel’s fleet was already accelerating toward Gatehold.

“Report.”

Christer looked across the ruined battlespace.

“Bastion secured.”

Anton added:

“With twenty-three seconds remaining.”

Joel smiled.

“Good.”

Anton sounded offended.

“That is all?”

“You wanted praise?”

“Yes.”

“You get Gatehold.”

Anton laughed.

“Better.”

In Sol, Bastion turned green on the diplomatic display.

One major military station.

One hidden warfleet.

Seraph infiltration.

Broken in under seven minutes after limiter authorization.

The delegates looked again at Lars.

His eyes were still burning red.

The Zenith observer finally asked:

“How many more forces like that does your House possess?”

Lars turned toward him.

The room became very quiet.

“Enough.”

No one asked again.

At Bastion, House ships began immediate redeployment.

Damaged vessels remained with tenders and garrison forces.

Captured Ardent hulls were secured.

Hunter teams stayed behind to purge the last Seraph sections.

Everything combat-capable turned toward Gatehold.

Anton transferred onto his assault flagship.

Christer returned to fleet command.

Their formations accelerated.

Ahead of them, Joel’s Central force was already moving.

Behind them, Bastion flew House colors.

Three House commanders.

Three separate fleets.

All converging on one place.

Gatehold.

And the gate was still open.

---

## 7. The Second Ship

The first Seraph capital had still not cleared the gate.

More than half its hull was already in normal space. Its aft section remained trapped inside the transit field, black-gold armor twisting under the strange light of the ancient structure.

Then Kira spoke.

“Second handshake advancing.”

Daniel looked at the gate-control display.

“That should be impossible.”

Lucy answered:

“It is not waiting for the first vessel to clear.”

The next mass signature moved forward.

Fast.

Too fast.

Dennis looked over Daniel’s shoulder.

“What happens if two capital-scale hulls overlap the transition?”

Daniel frowned.

“Nothing good.”

Then the gate screamed.

Not audibly.

Through the station.

Through every deck.

Through the floor beneath their feet.

The transit aperture brightened violently.

Something struck the first Seraph capital from behind.

The ship lurched forward.

Daniel stared.

“They are pushing it through.”

Dennis raised his incineration cannon.

“Efficient.”

Daniel looked at him.

“Do not admire them.”

Outside Gatehold, the Imperium of Man fleet was already being torn apart.

They had arrived in aggressive formation and immediately tried to force a corridor toward the gate.

The Seraph ambushers punished them for it.

An Imperium battleship drove forward under continuous macro-battery fire, its guns hammering one Seraph cruiser until the alien ship lost half its outer armor.

Then three Seraph vessels hit the battleship at once.

Lances punched through its upper decks.

A missile swarm struck the aft quarter.

The battleship’s engine section erupted.

It did not explode.

It simply lost propulsion and began drifting while internal fires spread through kilometer-long compartments.

Another Imperium cruiser tried to shield it.

That ship lasted twenty seconds.

A Seraph spinal beam crossed the void and ripped through its forward third.

The cruiser broke formation.

Escape pods began launching.

Joel watched the casualty numbers rise while his fleet accelerated toward Gatehold.

“Imperium losses?”

His tactical officer answered.

“Severe. Five escorts destroyed. Two cruisers crippled. One battleship combat ineffective.”

“And still advancing?”

“Yes.”

Joel almost smiled.

“Of course they are.”

The Zenith fleet was suffering differently.

They had entered clean.

Disciplined.

Layered formation.

Precise long-range fire.

For the first minute, it worked.

Zenith ships cut apart several Seraph strike craft and disabled one light cruiser with near-perfect coordination.

Then the Seraph changed tactics.

They went after command nodes.

The lead Zenith command cruiser took a concentrated missile strike through its sensor lattice.

Its tactical network died.

The formation hesitated.

Only for seconds.

That was enough.

Seraph boarding carriers rushed the opening.

One Zenith destroyer disappeared beneath a mass of black-gold assault craft.

Another was hit broadside by a heavy lance and vented half its crew compartments.

The Zenith fleet fragmented into local defense groups.

Annette watched the telemetry.

“They are losing synchronization.”

Kira agreed.

“Zenith formation efficiency reduced thirty-seven percent.”

Lucy added:

“Casualties increasing rapidly.”

Joel looked toward the tactical projection.

The Seraph had attacked exactly where each human faction was strongest.

The Imperium had been punished for aggression.

The Zenith for dependence on coordination.

And the House?

The Seraph had not yet decided how to break them.

At Gatehold, Daniel saw the second ship.

Only its bow.

But enough.

Another Seraph capital.

Smaller than the first.

Faster.

Its forward weapons were already firing before it fully crossed.

A lance struck Gatehold’s outer defense ring.

The entire station shook.

Warning lights erupted.

“Structural damage!”

“Outer Ring Three depressurizing!”

“Civilian sectors?”

“Not hit!”

Daniel exhaled once.

Then another warning appeared.

HUSK GUARD CONTACTS — CORE LEVEL.

Hidden compartments opened again.

Dennis turned before anyone else did.

A wall panel exploded outward.

Two Husk Guard emerged.

Dennis fired once.

The incineration cannon filled the passage with fire.

One creature collapsed immediately.

The other kept coming.

Daniel shot its damaged leg.

A Grey Nomad marine finished it with concentrated fire.

Dennis looked toward the gate display.

“We need to stop the second ship.”

Daniel nodded.

“How?”

Dennis pointed toward the first capital.

“Use the first one.”

Daniel stared.

Then understood.

“Jam the aperture.”

“Yes.”

“You want to deliberately cripple it inside the gate.”

“Yes.”

Daniel smiled.

“That is horrible.”

Dennis tilted his helmet.

“Thank you.”

Joel entered the Gatehold battlespace.

His fleet came in hard.

House Vigils surged ahead.

Destroyers spread.

Oath cruisers aligned behind them.

The capital ship at the center of Joel’s formation opened fire almost immediately.

Not at the first Seraph capital.

At the escorts killing the Zenith fleet.

Three heavy volleys crossed the void.

One Seraph cruiser lost its weapons spine.

A second had its drive section shattered.

A third broke away.

The Zenith commander appeared over tactical comms.

His face was bloodied.

“House command.”

Joel answered.

“High Marshal Joel von Hjertstedt.”

“We are taking extreme losses.”

“I can see that.”

“We require support.”

Joel looked toward the Imperium line.

They were in worse condition.

The Imperium flagship was still firing even with half its starboard batteries gone.

Joel opened a broad allied channel.

“Imperium. Zenith.”

Both answered through interference.

“Fall behind my line.”

The Imperium commander snapped back:

“We do not retreat.”

Joel’s eyes burned crimson.

“Then call it repositioning.”

Silence.

Then the Imperium commander laughed.

“Acceptable.”

Joel continued.

“House ships take center pressure. Zenith, rebuild your command net behind us. Imperium, pull crippled hulls and cover evacuation.”

The Zenith commander hesitated.

“You are assuming overall command.”

“Yes.”

Another pause.

Then:

“Accepted.”

The Imperium commander answered next.

“For now.”

Joel smiled.

“Good enough.”

The first Seraph capital finally cleared another hundred meters.

The second slammed into the transit sequence behind it.

Kira spoke across all House channels.

“Gate stress increasing.”

Lucy followed:

“Additional queued handshakes remain active.”

Daniel stared at the numbers.

“They are going to keep coming.”

Dennis stood beside him.

“Not if we make the first ship stay where it is.”

Daniel opened direct fleet comms to Joel.

“We have a plan.”

Joel’s answer came immediately.

“That sentence worries me.”

“It was Dennis’s idea.”

Joel closed his eyes.

“Of course it was.”

Dennis spoke calmly.

“Cripple the first capital’s aft structure while it is still inside transition. Lock it into the aperture.”

Joel looked toward the gate.

A capital ship jammed sideways inside the transit corridor.

A physical blockade.

A catastrophic one.

Dangerous.

Ugly.

Effective.

Annette entered.

“Risk to the gate?”

Daniel answered.

“High.”

Dennis added:

“Lower than letting six Seraph ships through.”

Annette considered for only a moment.

“Authorized.”

Daniel smiled.

“Joel?”

His brother looked toward the Seraph capital.

Then toward the brutal losses being taken by the Imperium and Zenith fleets.

Then back to the gate.

“Do it.”

Outside, House heavy guns began turning.

The target was not the ship’s reactor.

Not its command deck.

Not its weapons.

The target was its aft structure—

the part still trapped inside the gate.

And behind it, the second Seraph capital kept coming.

---

## 8. The Wall at Gatehold

The plan failed.

Daniel saw it happen in real time.

The first Seraph capital took the House fire exactly where Dennis had predicted, its aft structure tearing open under repeated heavy strikes while still caught in the transit aperture. For a moment, it looked as though the ship might twist, lock sideways, and jam the gate.

Then the Seraph adapted.

Hidden drive vanes flared inside the transition field. The wounded capital lurched forward instead of locking in place, shedding burning armor and crippled sections as it forced its own mass clear. The gate screamed through every deck of Gatehold.

And behind it, the second capital came through clean.

Then the third signal resolved into a cruiser.

Then another.

Then a boarding carrier.

Daniel stared at the unfolding queue.

“We failed.”

Dennis stood beside him, incineration cannon still smoking.

“Yes.”

No excuse.

No long explanation.

Just yes.

The tactical display updated again and again as Seraph ships entered battlespace.

Gatehold was no longer preventing the invasion.

It was now receiving it.

Outside, the Imperium of Man and Zenith fleets paid the price first.

The Imperium line held its position too long and got butchered for it. One crippled battleship took two more lance strikes across the same ruined quarter and finally split open, its stern breaking away in fire. Destroyers died trying to screen evacuation craft. An entire escort cluster vanished under a storm of Seraph missiles before it could even turn.

The Zenith fared no better. Their recovered command net lasted less than three minutes before the newly arrived Seraph cruisers hit it again. One command ship lost its sensor spine. Another was boarded. Their beautiful formation dissolved into isolated knots of ships fighting to survive.

Joel watched the losses climb from his bridge and made the decision instantly.

He opened the command line to Gatehold.

“Daniel.”

“Here.”

“You and Dennis stop trying to block the transit.”

Daniel looked back toward the core chamber, toward the gate screaming through the station walls.

“Understood.”

Joel’s voice became harder.

“Take full control of Gatehold.”

Daniel’s expression changed at once.

“The whole station?”

“Yes.”

“Command?”

“Yes.”

“Platform control?”

“Yes.”

“Defense rings?”

“All of it.”

Dennis turned slightly toward Daniel.

“That is a better plan.”

Daniel ignored him.

Joel continued.

“I need every defensive platform online, every surviving station gun awake, and every control room under House authority.”

Outside the viewscreen, a Seraph cruiser vaporized a drifting Zenith escort.

Joel’s eyes burned screaming red.

“My fleet is the wall. You make me a fortress.”

Daniel nodded once.

“Done.”

Joel cut the line and opened the fleet-wide channel.

“House ships, form on me.”

Across Gatehold space, the House answer was immediate. Vigils surged inward. Destroyers tightened the screen. Oath cruisers adjusted into overlapping lines of fire. Joel’s capital ship moved to the center and did not yield a meter.

Behind that forming wall, battered Zenith and Imperium vessels began falling back.

Not routed.

Not broken completely.

But bloodied enough to understand what Joel was offering them.

Cover.

The first full Seraph line pushed forward.

Joel met it head-on.

“Forward batteries,” he said. “Target the lead cruiser. Drive section.”

His capital ship fired first. Two Oath cruisers followed. The Seraph lead ship lost engines and drifted straight into the path of its own trailing formation. The House destroyers cut through the confusion, placing precise fire into escorts already turning to avoid collision.

The Imperium commander came over the allied channel, voice ragged and furious.

“You are holding the center.”

“Yes,” Joel answered.

“We are repositioning behind you.”

“I noticed.”

The man almost laughed despite everything.

“We owe you.”

Joel looked toward the Seraph line.

“Survive first.”

Then the Zenith commander answered, quieter but no less strained.

“House command, our wounded are crossing behind your screen now.”

“Keep them moving,” Joel said. “Any ship still able to fire joins the second line.”

That was the wall.

House in front.

Wounded allies behind.

Anything still dangerous layered where it could still matter.

The Seraph hit it with everything.

Lances.

Missiles.

Boarding craft.

Knife-range pressure.

The House line bent.

It did not break.

Inside Gatehold, Daniel moved just as fast.

He left a Black Guard team to secure the authentication core and dragged Commandant Oren Vale back to his feet.

“Station command access,” Daniel said.

Vale laughed weakly through the pain.

“You think you can run Gatehold while it burns?”

Dennis stepped closer.

The laugh stopped.

Vale swallowed.

“I can provide access.”

“Good,” Daniel said. “Because if you do not, Dennis starts solving walls again.”

One Grey Nomad marine muttered, “He is very good at that.”

The commandant’s authorization opened the first control layer. Daniel immediately began taking the station apart and rebuilding it under House command.

Platform control.

Defense-ring access.

Missile authorization.

Internal security overrides.

Compartment locks.

He sent teams everywhere.

Grey Nomad maniacs to seize the nearest defense control hubs.

Black Guard to communications and platform-link centers.

Hunters with Dennis to purge the hidden compartments still producing Husk Guard.

The station fought back.

More walls opened.

More horror came out.

Dennis answered with flame.

At one junction he fired straight through a sealed support column, and when the structure split open it revealed another hidden chamber packed with Husk Guards and a second Seraph officer sheltering behind them.

Daniel stared at the burning remains after the shot.

“You do realize this is why people fear you.”

Dennis reloaded the incineration cannon.

“It saves time.”

“It also destroys architecture.”

“It was hostile architecture.”

Daniel hated that this made sense.

Minutes later the first Gatehold defense ring came online.

Then the second.

Joel saw it on his tactical board.

A slow smile touched his face.

“Good.”

Station platforms woke and joined the battle.

Missile towers that had been silent moments before turned under House control and opened fire into the advancing Seraph line. A defense lance stitched across a Seraph boarding carrier and broke it open before it could release its cargo.

The wall in front of Gatehold thickened.

Fleet.

Station.

Platforms.

Command.

All House now.

Joel opened the command line one more time.

“Daniel.”

The reply came over gunfire, alarms, and somebody in the background shouting about points.

“Gatehold belongs to us.”

Joel looked at the wall his fleet had become and the fortress Daniel was building behind it.

“Good. Hold it.”

---

## 9. The Counteroffensive

Joel saw Anton and Christer before Gatehold control identified them.

The force released from Bastion arrived in two groups: Anton’s assault ships and Christer’s heavier formation. Both were burning hard at the far tactical edge.

Not scattered survivors.

Not relief ships.

War fleets.

Anton’s assault group came in low, fast, and aggressive.

Christer’s heavier formation approached above the gate plane, capital hulls at the center and cruisers spread around them in disciplined firing arcs.

Daniel’s voice came over the family channel.

“About time.”

Anton answered immediately.

“We had a station to finish.”

Christer added:

“Which we did.”

Joel looked across Gatehold.

His own fleet was still holding the center.

The Seraph had hammered the wall repeatedly. House armor was scorched. Several destroyers had fallen out of formation. One Oath cruiser was fighting with only half its main batteries.

Behind them, the remnants of the Zenith and Imperium fleets were reorganizing.

Ahead—

the Seraph invasion force kept growing.

Joel smiled.

“Good.”

He opened the full House command channel.

“Anton. Take the lower flank.”

“Gladly.”

“Christer. Upper vector.”

“Already moving.”

“Daniel.”

“Gatehold platforms ready.”

Joel’s crimson eyes burned.

“Then we stop defending.”

The tactical map changed.

House symbols began moving forward.

“Counteroffensive begins now.”

Anton’s fleet accelerated first.

Of course it did.

His assault carriers pushed ahead behind a screen of Vigil frigates. Oath cruisers rolled their broadsides toward a Seraph carrier formation that had been trying to flank Joel’s wall.

Anton pointed at the largest carrier.

“That one.”

His tactical officer asked:

“Disable or destroy?”

Anton considered for half a second.

“Disable if practical.”

A pause.

“Destroy if it becomes annoying.”

The officer nodded.

“Understood.”

House spinal guns fired.

The first volley tore through the carrier’s escort screen.

A Seraph cruiser lost its forward weapons.

A second had its drive cluster shattered.

Anton’s assault ships immediately poured through the opening.

The Seraph line began turning toward him.

That was exactly what Joel wanted.

Christer attacked from above.

Less dramatic.

More dangerous.

He waited until the Seraph formation shifted toward Anton, then ordered his entire heavy group down across their exposed upper quarter.

“Mark command vessels.”

Three contacts flashed.

“Concentrate.”

His capital ship fired.

Two cruisers followed less than a second later.

The first Seraph command ship disappeared inside overlapping impacts.

The second tried to turn.

A House destroyer group cut across its escape vector.

The third immediately transmitted new command traffic.

Christer watched the change.

“There.”

His tactical officer looked over.

“Sir?”

“The command traffic shifted to that ship.”

“New fleet command?”

“Likely. Watch which ships answer it.”

Christer pointed.

“Kill its communications.”

Not the reactor.

Not the crew sections.

Communications.

The next House volley ripped away the Seraph ship’s command spines and external sensor architecture.

Across the hostile formation, coordination degraded almost immediately.

Joel saw it.

“Excellent.”

Then Gatehold joined the counterattack.

Daniel stood inside the station’s restored defense command center, blood on one side of his armor, crimson eyes bright enough to unsettle even House personnel.

Dennis stood behind him.

Still holding the incineration cannon.

Daniel pointed toward the external tactical projection.

“Platform groups One and Four. Target Seraph boarding carriers.”

One Grey Nomad veteran operating a weapons station shouted:

“DO PLATFORM KILLS COUNT?”

Daniel stared at him.

“No.”

“That seems unfair.”

“Fire the weapons.”

“Yes, sir!”

Gatehold erupted.

Heavy station lances crossed space.

Missile silos opened.

Old defense cannons that had not fired under legitimate command in years began hammering Seraph ships at close Hammer Range.

One boarding carrier took a station lance through its center and split.

Another tried to withdraw and ran directly into Anton’s advancing cruisers.

Dennis watched.

“Better.”

Daniel looked at him.

“You sound disappointed that nothing exploded inside the station.”

“I am capable of enjoying external explosions.”

“That is somehow worse.”

Joel’s wall became a spear.

His capital ship advanced at the center.

Not quickly.

Relentlessly.

The surviving House line moved with him.

The Imperium commander saw it first.

“You are advancing.”

Joel answered over allied comms.

“Yes.”

The man looked toward his damaged battleships.

“Then so are we.”

Joel frowned.

“You are supposed to recover.”

“We recovered enough.”

The Zenith commander entered next.

“Our surviving command groups can support your right flank.”

Joel studied their status.

They had taken brutal losses.

Both factions had.

But they were still there.

“Stay behind House screening elements.”

The Imperium commander laughed.

“You really enjoy giving orders.”

Joel’s eyes burned red across the feed.

“At the moment, yes.”

The man stopped laughing.

“Understood.”

Across the allied command feeds, red-eyed House officers issued calm, precise orders. The foreign crews followed them; those ships were still their shield.

The three House fleets converged.

Joel center.

Anton lower flank.

Christer upper flank.

Gatehold behind.

The Seraph invasion force suddenly had nowhere clean to move.

Joel watched the geometry close.

“Daniel.”

“Ready.”

“Fire corridor Alpha.”

Gatehold’s platforms opened.

Seraph ships moved away from the station fire.

Straight toward Anton.

“Anton.”

“I see them.”

His cruisers fired at near-broadside range.

Two Seraph vessels died.

A third spun upward.

Christer was waiting.

“Thank you.”

His heavy batteries smashed it back down.

Joel’s fleet drove forward through the center.

One Seraph capital ship tried to anchor the retreat.

Joel marked it.

“All central batteries.”

His tactical officer hesitated.

“Full concentration?”

“Yes.”

Every available House gun in Joel’s formation fired into the capital’s weapons and drive sections.

The massive vessel staggered.

Its forward lances went dark.

Its engines weakened.

Joel opened the command channel.

“Now.”

House destroyers surged past him.

Boarding carriers followed.

The Seraph formation began breaking apart.

Not defeated.

Not yet.

But no longer advancing.

For the first time since the gate opened—

the Seraph were being pushed backward.

Daniel saw it from Gatehold.

“They are falling back toward the gate.”

Dennis looked at the display.

“Good.”

Daniel immediately pointed at him.

“No destroying the gate.”

Dennis sighed.

“I said nothing.”

“You were thinking loudly.”

Joel stood at the center of the tactical display.

Central was secured.

Bastion was secured.

Gatehold was under House control.

The converging House groups now fought as one battle group, supported by Daniel’s Gatehold defences.

He opened every allied channel.

“Imperium. Zenith. House.”

The other commanders listened.

“The defensive phase is over.”

“Gatehold remains open.”

“The Seraph remain in our battlespace.”

His eyes burned red.

“So we remove them.”

Ahead, Anton laughed.

Christer ordered another coordinated strike.

Daniel brought more platforms online.

And the combined House formation pushed forward.

The battle for Gatehold had become a counteroffensive.

---

## 10. Elder Bjorn’s Bottle

The gate closed.

Not slowly.

Not cleanly.

The ancient aperture collapsed in on itself with a violent white flash that washed across the entire battlespace.

Every Seraph ship was already through.

No more transit signatures.

No more queued handshakes.

No escape route.

Daniel watched the gate status change.

TRANSIT FIELD: INACTIVE

AUTHENTICATION LOCK: HOUSE CONTROL

He looked at Dennis.

“They are trapped.”

Dennis stared at the tactical projection.

“Yes.”

That single word sounded almost pleased.

Joel saw the same thing from the center of the combined House formation.

The remaining Seraph ships were inside Gatehold space, with the transit aperture closed behind them.

House formations surrounded them from three directions.

Gatehold’s platforms covered the fourth.

The Imperium and Zenith remnants were regrouping behind the House line, both still badly mauled.

Joel’s eyes burned bright red.

“Annette.”

“Here.”

“Confirm no civilian vessels inside the hostile formation.”

Kira answered first.

“No authenticated civilian transponders within primary Seraph combat group.”

Lucy followed.

“Multiple captured human hulls present, but all are operating under Seraph military command.”

Joel nodded.

Then opened the full House combat channel.

“All commands.”

Anton stopped joking.

Christer looked toward the main tactical plot.

Daniel listened from Gatehold.

Dennis stood beside him.

Joel spoke calmly.

“The gate is closed.”

“The enemy cannot withdraw.”

He marked one black-gold contact near the center of the Seraph formation.

Large.

Armored.

Surrounded by escorts.

Kira had already identified its traffic pattern.

Command ship.

Joel highlighted it.

“That vessel stays alive.”

Then every other hostile contact turned blood-red.

“Everything else…”

He paused.

“…annihilate.”

Anton smiled.

Christer’s crimson eyes narrowed.

Daniel looked toward the platform controls.

Dennis simply checked the charge on his incineration cannon.

Joel continued.

“No general boarding competition.”

Anton immediately objected.

“Joel—”

“Not yet.”

Anton sighed.

“The command ship is the prize.”

That got his attention.

Christer’s too.

Joel closed the open channel.

Then sent one private message.

Only one recipient.

DENNIS RASK.

Dennis’s helmet chimed.

He opened it.

Joel’s face appeared alone.

No Annette.

No Daniel.

No Anton.

No Christer.

Joel looked completely serious.

“The first one to take the enemy command ship wins a bottle from Vintergard.”

Dennis remained still.

Joel added:

“Made by Elder Bjorn.”

Silence.

Then Dennis slowly turned his helmet toward Daniel.

Daniel immediately knew something was wrong.

“What?”

Dennis said nothing.

Daniel narrowed his eyes.

“What did Joel say?”

Dennis began walking toward the launch access.

Daniel stepped in front of him.

“Dennis.”

The Hunter commander stopped.

Daniel pointed.

“No.”

“I have not said anything.”

“You are walking with purpose.”

“I usually do.”

“That is not normal purpose.”

Dennis opened the boarding preparation channel.

“Hunter Lance Duskbringer. Prepare for capital assault.”

Daniel stared.

“You bastard.”

Dennis continued walking.

Daniel opened the family channel immediately.

“JOEL!”

Joel answered.

“Yes?”

“What did you promise him?”

Nothing.

Anton joined.

“Oh, this is interesting.”

Christer entered next.

“What did Joel do?”

Daniel shouted:

“Dennis is preparing to board the command ship!”

Anton laughed.

“That sounds reasonable.”

Daniel snapped:

“He started moving before Joel even announced boarding authority!”

Annette appeared.

“Joel.”

Joel looked at her.

She stared back.

“What did you offer Dennis?”

Joel hesitated.

That was enough.

Anton’s expression changed.

“Oh.”

Christer leaned toward his display.

“It is alcohol.”

Daniel pointed furiously.

“OF COURSE IT IS ALCOHOL.”

Annette’s eyes narrowed.

“What bottle?”

Joel answered quietly.

“Elder Bjorn.”

The channel went completely silent.

Anton stopped smiling.

Christer’s mouth opened slightly.

Daniel stared.

Even Annette looked surprised.

Anton spoke first.

“You offered him a Bjorn bottle?”

“Yes.”

“For first capture?”

“Yes.”

Christer immediately opened his assault command channel.

“All boarding formations prepare.”

Daniel shouted:

“NO!”

Anton was already moving.

“Assault carriers forward.”

Annette looked at Joel.

“You have caused a problem.”

Joel glanced toward the Seraph command ship.

“No.”

A faint smile appeared.

“I created motivation.”

The counteroffensive erupted.

Christer’s heavy fleet struck first.

His capital guns hammered Seraph escorts around the command ship, not destroying the central vessel, but tearing apart everything protecting it.

“Escort cruiser two,” Christer ordered.

“Erase it.”

The House line fired.

The Seraph cruiser disappeared inside overlapping heavy impacts.

Anton attacked lower.

His fleet tore into the boarding carriers and destroyers trying to reinforce the Seraph center.

“Command ship stays alive!” Anton shouted.

His gunner replied:

“Everything else?”

Anton’s red eyes burned.

“Joel said annihilate.”

The batteries opened.

Gatehold joined them.

Daniel pointed across the platform-control grid.

“Defense rings, coordinated fire.”

Missile towers erupted.

Ancient station lances cut through the dark.

One Seraph carrier broke apart under direct platform fire.

Another lost its engines and was finished by Anton’s cruisers.

Daniel watched the hostile numbers collapse.

“Dennis, where are you?”

No answer.

He checked.

Dennis was already aboard an insertion craft.

Daniel stared at the icon.

“That lunatic.”

Behind the House line, the surviving Imperium and Zenith commanders watched.

The Imperium admiral saw Seraph ships disappear one after another.

Not crippled.

Not driven away.

Destroyed.

“This is extermination.”

The Zenith commander answered quietly.

“They cannot retreat.”

The Imperium admiral looked toward Joel’s capital.

“And he knows it.”

House formations advanced with terrifying limiter-released precision.

Red eyes filled bridge cameras.

Orders came faster than normal crews could follow.

Targets vanished almost immediately after designation.

For the first time since arriving, neither allied commander felt pity that House von Hjertstedt had taken command.

They felt relief.

The Seraph command ship realized what was happening.

Its escorts were dying.

Its formation collapsing.

Its route closed.

It turned.

Not toward the gate.

Toward Gatehold.

Daniel saw the vector.

“It is coming at the station.”

Joel answered immediately.

“Break its engines.”

“Not the reactor.”

“Not the bridge.”

“Leave it alive.”

Three House fleets adjusted.

Christer targeted its maneuvering vanes.

Anton targeted weapons.

Joel targeted drives.

The first volley struck.

The command ship staggered.

Its forward lance died.

Second volley.

One engine went dark.

Third.

The massive vessel began drifting.

Dennis’s insertion craft accelerated toward it.

Anton saw the same icon.

“No.”

Christer saw it too.

“He is ahead.”

Anton shouted:

“FULL BURN!”

The carrier captain answered on their secure link.

“To the cleared limit, Breach Commander. I am not taking you through our own fire.”

Anton checked the escort lanes.

“Cleared limit. Confirmed.”

His boarding carriers accelerated.

Daniel watched the absurd race beginning around the crippled command ship.

“Joel.”

“Yes?”

“You have turned the capture of the most important Seraph vessel in the campaign into a drinking competition.”

Joel watched Dennis close on the target.

“Yes.”

Daniel sighed.

“I hate this family.”

Dennis’s craft struck the Seraph hull first.

Magnetic clamps engaged.

Breach systems extended.

His voice entered the private House channel.

Very calm.

“Tell Elder Bjorn I am coming for his bottle.”

Then the cutting charges fired.

---

## 11. All of Them Board

Dennis breached first.

His charges punched through the Seraph command ship’s outer hull, and Lance Duskbringer went in behind fire and smoke. For three heartbeats it looked clean.

Then the ship woke up.

Not engines.

Not guns.

Inside the hull, sealed compartments opened all at once.

Kira’s voice cut through every House boarder channel.

“Multiple internal vaults opening.”

Lucy followed.

“Husk Guard reserves confirmed. Additional experimental warforms present.”

Dennis looked down the first corridor just as red emergency lights came alive.

The walls split.

Armored coffins unfolded into standing shapes.

Husk Guard.

Dozens.

Behind them came taller things.

Experimental command warforms shaped from human and Seraph stock together, stretched into elegant nightmare forms with plated limbs, weapon grafts, and too-human faces trapped beneath clear masks.

Dennis raised his incineration cannon.

“Contact.”

Then the corridor disappeared into white-orange fire.

Anton’s assault carriers struck a moment later.

Three cutters attached along the lower command hull, and the former Grey Nomad maniacs poured in cheering like criminals arriving at a festival.

“BRIDGE RACE!”

“PRISONERS STILL COUNT!”

“NOT IF IT’S ALL MONSTERS!”

“Check before you decide,” Anton snapped. “Living prisoners come out.”

Anton entered behind them, crimson eyes screaming red, heavy pistol in one hand and blade in the other.

He opened the family channel.

“Dennis, do not take the bridge before I arrive.”

Dennis answered through flame and gunfire.

“No promises.”

Anton laughed.

“That counts as respect.”

Christer came in from above.

He chose the harder route on purpose: armored dorsal access, closer to the command spines and farther from obvious corridors. Black Guard and disciplined boarding teams followed him in.

He did not shout.

He did not laugh.

He simply moved with frightening efficiency, reading the ship’s structure as if it were already surrendering.

“Mark junctions.”

“Seal rear passages.”

“No one wanders.”

A Black Guard officer checked the internal plot.

“Hostiles converging from six vectors.”

Christer nodded.

“Good. That means we are close.”

Daniel left Gatehold only after handing full station control to trusted command teams. Then he boarded with a smaller force through a captured docking aperture blasted open by House guns.

He entered already irritated.

That helped.

He found the first corridor stacked with burned Husk Guards, a cut-open bulkhead, and a Seraph warform still twitching on the floor.

Daniel looked at the destruction.

“Dennis was here.”

One Grey Nomad veteran beside him nodded.

“How can you tell?”

Daniel stepped over half a wall.

“He solved architecture.”

Annette did not stay behind.

That surprised several foreign observers more than Joel’s decision.

She boarded aboard a Justicar insertion cutter with Black Guard, evidence teams, and a compact command section built around Kira and Lucy’s live tactical feeds. Her peach hair was braided tight for war. The pink rose pin remained visible.

Inside the House, that mattered.

The Head Justicar was boarding personally.

Meaning this was no longer only a battle.

It was judgment.

“Preserve command data,” she ordered.

“Capture living Seraph command personnel where possible.”

One Black Guard officer looked at the feed.

“Possible appears unlikely.”

Annette touched the rose.

“Try anyway.”

Then Joel did something even by House standards completely unreasonable.

He did not take a cutter.

He did not wait for a corridor.

He used a self-drilling assault drop.

The device detached from beneath his capital ship, a one-man armored spike with breaching drills, shock insulation, and just enough guidance to hit a moving capital hull under battle conditions.

The allied reaction was immediate.

On the Imperium flagship, the battered admiral stared at the feed.

“He is doing what?”

The Zenith commander answered in disbelief.

“He launched himself.”

“Inside a torpedo?”

“More or less.”

The Imperium admiral watched the House High Marshal slam bodily through a Seraph command vessel.

Then shook his head.

“These people are lunatics.”

Behind them, bridge crews watched the red-eyed House feeds with a mixture of horror and admiration.

They had already seen House ships become a wall.

Now they were watching the family itself become boarding weapons.

Joel’s drop struck the Seraph command ship near the inner command well.

The drill screamed through armor, two decks, and a sealed support cavity before finally blowing inward.

Joel came out alone.

Smoke.

Red lights.

Alarms.

Then shapes.

He was surrounded immediately.

Husk Guard from three sides.

Two experimental warforms dropping from the ceiling rigging.

A Seraph command escort at the far end raising a long weapon.

Joel’s eyes burned.

He drew and fired in the same motion.

First shot killed the escort.

Second broke a Husk Guard knee.

Third hit a warform’s mask.

Then they were on him.

A Husk Guard smashed into his shield and drove him back against the ruined drop shell. Another came from the left. Joel dropped low, cut the first behind its leg joint, rose, and shot the second through a seam under its jaw plating.

The warforms hit next.

Fast.

One nearly took his head off.

Joel blocked, pivoted, and rammed it into the wall hard enough to crack both armor and bulkhead. Another struck his shoulder and drove him to one knee.

House command channels erupted.

Anton saw Joel’s telemetry spike.

“Where is he?”

Kira answered.

“High Marshal inserted alone near command well.”

Christer actually stopped for half a second.

“He did what?”

Daniel groaned.

“Of course he did.”

Annette did not even sound surprised.

“Push toward him.”

On the allied command net, the Imperium admiral looked at Joel’s isolated icon deep inside the Seraph ship.

“He is alone?”

The Zenith commander watched Joel’s biometrics remain violently active while surrounded by hostiles.

“For the moment.”

The Imperium admiral barked a laugh.

“I revise my earlier statement. They are not lunatics.”

“What are they?”

He looked at the boarding feed, where Joel kept fighting inside a circle of monsters.

“They are worse.”

Inside the ship, every von Hjertstedt was now aboard.

Joel alone at the center.

Anton carving upward through chaos.

Christer closing from above.

Daniel driving inward through the wreckage Dennis had left behind.

Annette advancing with the law itself at her back.

And Dennis, somewhere ahead, still burning a road straight toward command.

The Seraph command ship had become a trap.

For anyone else, perhaps.

For House von Hjertstedt, it had become a race.

---

## 12. The Living Seraph

Annette found the prisoner by accident.

Or rather, Lucy found it.

“Stop.”

Annette raised one fist.

The Black Guard froze around her.

Ahead, the corridor looked empty.

Broken lights.

Blood.

Burned wall panels.

One dead experimental soldier folded against a bulkhead.

Nothing else.

Kira added:

“Biological signature behind the command partition.”

Annette looked toward the wall.

“Seraph?”

“Probability seventy-four percent.”

One Black Guard trooper raised a breaching weapon.

Annette shook her head.

“Alive.”

The trooper lowered it.

They cut the partition carefully.

Behind it was a narrow command alcove.

And something was hiding inside.

Not a Husk Guard.

Not one of the distorted experimental soldiers.

A true Seraph.

Smaller than the battlefield commanders they had seen.

Thin.

Almost delicate.

Its black-gold armor was built around a narrow torso, with fine neural tendrils running from its neck into a command harness.

Its face was wrong in a quieter way than the warforms.

Almost human.

Almost beautiful.

Too symmetrical.

Too still.

One side of its body had been crushed by the collapsing compartment.

It was alive.

It looked directly at Annette.

Then smiled.

A Black Guard trooper raised his rifle.

Annette caught the barrel with one hand.

“No.”

The Seraph spoke through a translator implant.

“You came personally.”

Annette’s pink rose pin caught the emergency light.

“Yes.”

The creature looked at it.

Something changed in its expression.

Recognition.

“Justicar.”

Annette’s eyes narrowed.

“You know what that means?”

“Yes.”

That answer made the entire team quieter.

Annette stepped closer.

“Name.”

The Seraph hesitated.

Then:

“Sael-Vareth.”

“Function?”

“Interpretation.”

Lucy immediately flagged the term.

“Possible command-intelligence caste.”

Annette crouched beside it.

“What does interpretation mean?”

Sael-Vareth looked toward the ceiling as distant gunfire shook the hull.

“We make your people understandable.”

Annette’s expression went cold.

“Spies.”

“No.”

“Interrogators?”

“No.”

“Then explain.”

The Seraph smiled again.

“We study what remains after conversion.”

Nobody moved.

Annette understood.

Memory.

Personality.

Speech.

Habits.

How much of a human could survive inside a controlled body while still being useful.

Project Shepherd.

The returned assets.

The experiments below Central.

This creature knew exactly what they had been doing.

Annette opened a secure channel.

“Joel.”

Static answered first.

Then gunfire.

Joel’s breathing remained controlled despite the combat around him.

“Yes?”

“I have a living Seraph.”

The family channel went silent.

Anton answered first.

“You what?”

Christer immediately cut in.

“Alive?”

“Yes.”

Daniel:

“True Seraph or host?”

“True Seraph.”

Dennis’s voice followed.

“Location?”

Annette paused.

“No.”

Dennis sounded almost offended.

“I only asked.”

“You are carrying an incineration cannon.”

“That is unrelated.”

Daniel laughed.

For half a second.

Then Joel spoke.

“Can it talk?”

Annette looked at Sael-Vareth.

“Yes.”

The Seraph looked back at her.

“It can.”

Joel went quiet.

“Secure it.”

Annette nodded.

“That was already the plan.”

Across the ship, Joel was still alone.

For the moment.

His drop point had turned into a graveyard of Husk Guard.

Two bodies blocked the corridor.

A third was still moving.

Joel shot it through the head seam and continued toward the command well.

His armor showed damage warnings.

Shoulder actuator degraded.

Shield at forty-three percent.

Pulse elevated far beyond normal human limits.

Limiter release still active.

His eyes burned red.

Then Kira patched Annette’s prisoner feed into his helmet.

Joel saw Sael-Vareth.

And stopped.

The Seraph saw him too.

Its smile disappeared.

Interesting.

Annette noticed.

“You recognize him.”

Sael-Vareth said nothing.

Joel stepped over another corpse.

“Ask again.”

Annette did.

“Do you recognize High Marshal Joel von Hjertstedt?”

The Seraph looked away.

That was enough.

Anton entered the channel.

“Oh, I like that.”

Christer sounded colder.

“They know him personally.”

“No,” Annette said.

“They know something about him.”

Sael-Vareth looked back toward her.

“You should not exist.”

Annette became very still.

Joel did too.

Daniel spoke softly.

“That sounds familiar.”

House blood.

The failed conversions.

The elders retained for study.

Project Shepherd’s bloodline stock.

Annette leaned closer.

“Why should he not exist?”

Sael-Vareth smiled weakly.

“Because your blood kills us.”

Silence.

Absolute silence.

Even Dennis stopped moving.

Annette’s face changed.

Not visibly to outsiders.

But Joel knew her.

That sentence had crossed a line.

One of the House’s deepest secrets had just been spoken aloud by the enemy.

Annette cut every non-family channel immediately.

“Secure compartment.”

The Black Guard closed formation around the prisoner.

Kira encrypted the feed.

Lucy wiped local relay buffers.

Joel’s voice came through.

“How much do they know?”

Sael-Vareth answered before Annette could ask.

“Enough.”

Then it laughed.

Quietly.

Painfully.

“We spent decades asking why your family rejects perfection.”

Daniel muttered:

“Perfection.”

Dennis answered:

“I dislike it already.”

Annette ignored them.

“How many House subjects?”

The Seraph did not answer.

Annette touched the rose pin.

“You misunderstand your situation.”

Sael-Vareth looked at the Justicar Shield.

“No.”

“I understand it very well.”

A distant explosion shook the compartment.

Then the Seraph added:

“You are not here to interrogate us.”

Annette frowned.

“What do you think we are here to do?”

Sael-Vareth looked toward the direction of the bridge.

“To take the Shepherd.”

Annette’s blood went cold.

“What Shepherd?”

The creature smiled.

Not Project Shepherd.

Something else.

Something aboard the command ship.

Before Annette could continue, Lucy interrupted.

“New internal command burst detected.”

Kira followed.

“Origin: central command vault.”

Then:

“Multiple Seraph units redirecting toward this compartment.”

Annette stood.

“They want the prisoner back.”

The Black Guard formed immediately.

Sael-Vareth corrected her.

“No.”

Its smile vanished.

“They want me dead.”

That was worse.

Very much worse.

Annette opened the family channel.

“Everyone listen.”

Joel stopped advancing.

Anton halted mid-breach.

Christer redirected his tactical overlay.

Daniel looked up.

Dennis turned toward the nearest route.

Annette spoke carefully.

“The prisoner knows about House blood.”

Nobody joked.

“And it just referenced something aboard this ship called ‘the Shepherd.’”

Joel’s crimson eyes flared brighter.

“Keep it alive.”

Annette looked toward the corridor as the first hostile markers appeared.

“That is becoming difficult.”

Dennis answered instantly.

“I am coming.”

Daniel shouted:

“No incinerating the prisoner!”

Dennis replied:

“I know the difference.”

Anton laughed.

“That is debatable.”

Then the corridor lights went out.

In the darkness ahead, dozens of Seraph signatures began closing on Annette’s position.

---

## 13. Family Converges

Anton heard Annette’s warning first.

Christer understood it first.

Daniel reacted hardest.

Annette was trapped inside a Seraph command ship with a living enemy prisoner who knew one of House von Hjertstedt’s deepest secrets. For Christer and Daniel, she was their sister. For Anton, she was his brother’s wife. None of them needed a second call.

That changed everything.

Christer stopped in the middle of a command corridor.

“Bridge objective suspended.”

His Black Guard officer turned.

“Sir?”

“Annette.”

That was enough.

Christer changed the tactical overlay.

His route to the Seraph bridge disappeared.

A new route appeared instead.

Straight toward his sister.

“Move.”

Daniel received the same coordinates.

He had been pushing inward from the lower docking levels, following the destruction Dennis had left behind.

Then Annette’s marker turned red.

HOSTILE CONVERGENCE — HEAVY

Daniel’s crimson eyes flared.

His expression changed immediately.

The Grey Nomad veterans around him noticed.

One whispered:

“Oh.”

Another looked over.

“What?”

“That is his sister.”

Daniel drew his second weapon.

“Yes.”

He opened a direct channel.

“Annette.”

Gunfire answered before her voice did.

“I am busy.”

“How many?”

Kira answered for her.

“Forty-three confirmed hostile contacts approaching. Additional signatures masked.”

Daniel stared at the map.

“Christer?”

His brother answered immediately.

“Moving.”

“Distance?”

“Four hundred metres.”

“I am closer.”

Christer replied dryly:

“Do not turn rescuing our sister into a competition.”

Daniel started running.

“You are only saying that because I am closer.”

Annette heard them.

Despite everything, she almost smiled.

Almost.

The compartment around her was becoming a fortress.

Black Guard had overturned command consoles into firing positions. Two narrow approaches had been mined with directional charges. Sael-Vareth remained restrained behind the inner shield line.

The living Seraph watched Annette carefully.

“You mobilized them.”

“My brothers?”

“Yes.”

Annette checked her weapon.

“You threatened their sister.”

Sael-Vareth tilted its head.

“Humans attach strategic value to blood.”

Annette looked at it.

“You experimented on my family for decades and still needed that explained?”

The Seraph said nothing.

Then the first attack came.

Husk Guard hit the outer barricade.

Heavy.

Fast.

One crashed through the first defensive shield and drove a Black Guard trooper backward.

Annette fired into the creature’s exposed joint.

Another Guard finished it.

Then experimental soldiers appeared behind the warforms, firing through gaps with frightening discipline.

“Left!” Annette shouted.

The Black Guard shifted.

The corridor filled with controlled fire.

One Husk Guard fell.

Another reached the barricade.

Then the lights died again.

Kira switched Annette’s vision to tactical overlay.

Red shapes kept coming.

Too many.

Sael-Vareth spoke softly behind her.

“They will not allow me to leave.”

Annette fired twice.

“They do not get to decide.”

Christer hit the enemy rear first.

Not Daniel.

Daniel would complain about that later.

Christer’s teams emerged through a service corridor above the attacking formation.

He saw Husk Guard below.

Experimental soldiers.

Two Seraph command escorts directing them.

His eyes burned red.

“Mark officers.”

The Black Guard beside him fired.

One Seraph escort collapsed.

Christer took the other himself.

Then his entire team dropped into the corridor.

The Seraph assault suddenly had a second front.

Annette heard the gunfire change.

“Christer?”

“Yes.”

“You took your time.”

“I was securing your escape route.”

“That sounds like an excuse.”

“It is a professional explanation.”

For one second, Annette smiled.

Then another Husk Guard charged.

Daniel arrived from the opposite side seconds later.

He did not use the door.

Dennis had apparently influenced him.

A breaching charge blew through the side wall.

Daniel came through the smoke with Grey Nomad veterans behind him.

“ANNETTE!”

She looked over.

“I am standing right here.”

“I can see that.”

“Then stop shouting.”

The Grey Nomad troops immediately opened fire.

One yelled:

“PROTECT THE JUSTICAR!”

Another:

“PROTECT CHRISTER’S SISTER!”

Daniel snapped:

“She is also my sister!”

“THAT TOO!”

Christer heard it over comms.

“This is why I did not want them.”

Daniel fired into a Husk Guard.

“You helped bring them!”

“That was your idea!”

Annette shouted:

“Both of you stop arguing and kill the Seraph!”

Both brothers answered simultaneously.

“Yes, Annette.”

The Black Guard around her exchanged looks.

Sael-Vareth watched the entire exchange with visible confusion.

“You obey her.”

Daniel glanced toward the prisoner while reloading.

“She is our sister.”

Christer added:

“And Head Justicar.”

Daniel nodded.

“That part is significantly more dangerous.”

Annette looked at both of them.

“Keep talking.”

Neither did.

Then Anton arrived.

He came around the northern corridor expecting to find a battle.

Instead he found Christer, Daniel, Annette, and a growing pile of dead Seraph warforms.

He stopped.

“You started without me.”

Daniel shouted:

“This is not a competition!”

Anton looked toward Annette.

“You all right?”

“Yes.”

“The prisoner?”

“Alive.”

Anton looked at Sael-Vareth.

The Seraph looked back.

Anton’s smile disappeared completely.

“So that is one of them.”

Annette nodded.

“It knows about our blood.”

Anton became very still.

Then his red eyes brightened.

“Ah.”

That single sound frightened Sael-Vareth more than shouting would have.

Annette pointed toward the corridor.

“Anton.”

“Yes?”

“Do not kill my prisoner.”

“I was not planning to.”

Daniel muttered:

“That answer runs in the family now.”

Then another voice entered the channel.

Dennis.

“Twenty seconds out.”

Daniel immediately shouted:

“No incinerator near the prisoner!”

Dennis replied:

“I know.”

Christer looked toward Daniel.

“You really do not trust him.”

“He once solved a hostage situation with anti-ship charges.”

Anton laughed.

Dennis answered:

“It worked.”

Annette closed her eyes for half a second.

Then Kira interrupted.

“New hostile concentration detected.”

Lucy followed.

“Primary source: bridge sector.”

The tactical map changed.

The Seraph were no longer sending small groups.

A full command-security formation was moving toward them.

Christer looked at Daniel.

Daniel looked at Anton.

Anton looked at Annette.

Annette stood in front of the living Seraph prisoner.

“Fine,” Annette said.

Her pink rose glinted under the red emergency lights.

“Family position.”

Weapons came up.

Christer, Daniel, and Anton formed around her.

And somewhere down the corridor, Dennis Rask was approaching with an incineration cannon.

The Seraph had chosen the wrong prisoner to retrieve.

---

## 14. The Commander Comes

The Seraph attack changed.

Christer noticed first.

“They stopped pushing.”

Daniel checked the tactical overlay.

The red contacts that had been flooding toward Annette’s position were pulling back.

Not retreating.

Opening a corridor.

Anton looked at the map.

“That is worse.”

Annette turned toward Sael-Vareth.

The prisoner had gone completely still.

For the first time since capture, it looked afraid.

Annette saw it.

“Who is coming?”

Sael-Vareth did not answer.

Then Kira spoke.

“High-priority biological signature leaving bridge sector.”

Lucy followed.

“Multiple elite escorts accompanying. Command traffic restructuring around moving entity.”

Christer understood.

“The fleet commander.”

Sael-Vareth finally whispered:

“Yes.”

Daniel looked down the corridor.

“It is coming personally?”

The Seraph prisoner stared at Annette.

“To kill me.”

Anton smiled without humor.

“And us?”

Sael-Vareth looked at the four Hjertstedts.

“You are the greater concern.”

The first bodyguards appeared thirty seconds later.

They did not resemble Husk Guard.

They were cleaner.

Taller.

Purpose-built.

Black-gold armor followed humanoid shapes, but each warrior moved with impossible precision. Their weapons were narrow, elegant things that fired bursts of white energy capable of cutting deep into heavy armor.

One Black Guard trooper took a hit across the shield and was thrown backward.

Christer fired immediately.

“Command elites!”

The corridor erupted.

Anton moved left.

Daniel right.

Christer held the center.

Annette remained behind them with Sael-Vareth and the evidence team.

Her brothers and Anton formed a killing line in front of her.

Then Dennis arrived.

He came around the corner, saw the Seraph elites, and raised the incineration cannon.

Daniel immediately shouted:

“CONTROLLED FIRE!”

Dennis fired.

The entire far corridor vanished behind flame.

Daniel stared.

“That was your controlled fire?”

“Yes.”

Three Seraph elites came through the flames anyway.

Dennis lowered the cannon.

“Impressive.”

Anton laughed.

“I want one.”

“No,” Annette said.

Anton looked back.

“I meant the weapon.”

Annette stared at him.

“I know.”

Then the Seraph commander appeared.

The escorts parted.

It was enormous.

Not Husk Guard enormous.

Controlled.

Deliberate.

Almost regal.

Black-gold armor wrapped around a tall biological frame. Layered plates curved from the shoulders like folded wings. A long command blade rested in one hand. Neural filaments moved behind its head like living hair.

Its face was partly human.

Not because it had once been human.

Because someone had designed it that way.

To communicate.

To manipulate.

To frighten.

The creature looked directly at Sael-Vareth.

“Interpreter.”

Sael-Vareth lowered its head.

The commander's voice emerged through several overlapping tones.

“You failed.”

Annette stepped forward.

“No.”

The creature's gaze shifted to her.

“She was captured.”

Annette touched the pink rose.

“She surrendered to lawful custody.”

Sael-Vareth looked at Annette sharply.

The commander did not understand the distinction.

Or did not care.

“You preserve defective things.”

Annette's expression hardened.

“We preserve witnesses.”

The commander looked beyond her.

At Christer.

Daniel.

Anton.

Then Dennis.

Its gaze stopped on the red eyes.

“You opened yourselves.”

Daniel frowned.

“You know about the limiters?”

The Seraph commander tilted its head.

“We know your blood.”

That wiped the last trace of humor from the corridor.

Anton stepped forward.

“Say that again.”

Annette raised one hand.

He stopped.

Barely.

The Seraph commander continued.

“You reject union.”

Christer answered coldly:

“We reject parasites.”

The creature ignored him.

Its attention returned to Annette.

“Your lineage was studied before you were born.”

Daniel’s weapon rose.

Annette said quietly:

“Daniel.”

He did not lower it.

The commander looked almost amused.

“Brothers.”

That made Daniel even angrier.

It knew.

Not merely House blood.

Their relationships.

Their family.

Their history.

The commander pointed its blade toward Sael-Vareth.

“Return the Interpreter.”

Annette answered immediately.

“No.”

“Then it dies.”

“No.”

“You will die with it.”

Anton smiled.

“Now we are negotiating.”

The commander moved.

Fast.

Far faster than its size suggested.

Its first strike hit Christer’s weapon and threw him into the wall.

Daniel fired from the flank.

The commander twisted, taking the rounds across shoulder armor.

Anton charged directly.

Of course he did.

Their blades hit with enough force to crack the deck beneath them.

Dennis fired past both of them and burned two approaching escorts apart.

Annette pulled Sael-Vareth back behind cover.

“Keep it alive!”

A Black Guard trooper shouted:

“Trying!”

Christer recovered and attacked from the commander's blind side.

Daniel joined him.

Three Hjertstedts hit the Seraph commander at once.

The creature still held.

Its blade struck Anton across the chest and sent him sliding backward.

Then it punched Daniel hard enough to deform his shoulder plate.

Christer fired into the joint under its arm.

The commander finally staggered.

Anton came back laughing.

“You should have done that first.”

Christer snapped:

“I just did.”

Sael-Vareth watched.

Not the battle.

The family.

Something about its expression changed.

“You fight differently.”

Annette kept her weapon trained toward the corridor.

“Different from what?”

“From the records.”

“What records?”

Sael-Vareth looked toward the commander.

Then back to Annette.

“The Shepherd archive.”

Annette froze.

“What is it?”

The prisoner spoke quickly now.

Afraid the commander might reach them.

“A living strategic archive.”

“Where?”

“Inside this ship.”

The commander heard.

Its head turned.

Sael-Vareth continued.

“It contains captured memories.”

“Admirals.”

“Scientists.”

“Gatewrights.”

“Nobles.”

“Your elders.”

Annette's blood went cold.

Daniel heard every word.

So did Christer.

Anton stopped laughing.

The commander suddenly abandoned the fight.

Not retreating.

Charging toward Sael-Vareth.

Dennis stepped directly into its path.

For the first time, the Seraph commander met something almost as large and considerably less reasonable.

Dennis raised his blade.

“You are not getting past me.”

The Seraph commander struck.

Dennis blocked.

The impact shook the corridor.

Then he answered with a headbutt powerful enough to crack the creature’s faceplate.

Daniel stared.

“Did you just—”

“Yes.”

Anton laughed again.

The commander's helmet fractured.

Beneath it—

a human face stared back.

Not grafted decoration.

A real face.

Older.

Male.

Terrified.

And still conscious.

Annette stopped breathing.

The human mouth moved beneath Seraph control.

One word came out.

“Help.”

---

## 15. Red Corridor

The human face inside the Seraph commander whispered again.

“Help.”

Then the parasite seized control.

The commander moved with terrifying speed.

Its blade came down on Dennis hard enough to crack his shield from top to bottom.

Dennis staggered.

The second strike split the shield completely.

The third punched through his shoulder plate and drove him against the bulkhead.

“Dennis!” Daniel shouted.

The commander lifted him bodily by the damaged armor.

Then threw him across the corridor.

Dennis hit the deck and did not immediately rise.

That frightened everyone.

Because Dennis always rose.

The Seraph assault hit at the same moment.

Command elites flooded the corridor.

Husk Guard came behind them.

Experimental soldiers crawled from hidden wall compartments, some still wearing pieces of human uniforms beneath surgical armor.

The House position collapsed into close combat.

A Black Guard trooper took a Seraph blade through the chest.

Another dragged him backward while firing one-handed.

“MEDIC!”

“He’s gone!”

The dead man was left where he fell.

There was no time.

A Husk Guard crashed through the barricade and caught two Grey Nomad veterans.

One disappeared beneath it.

The other was thrown against the wall hard enough to break his leg.

He screamed once, then raised his rifle from the deck.

“KEEP MOVING!”

Daniel shot the Husk Guard through the knee.

Christer destroyed the exposed neck joint.

The creature fell almost on top of the wounded marine.

Daniel dragged him clear.

“Still alive?”

“Unfortunately.”

“Good.”

“Does this count toward wounded recovery?”

Daniel stared at him.

“Of course you ask that now.”

Annette remained with Sael-Vareth.

The prisoner had become the center of the battle.

Seraph troops were not trying to rescue it.

They were trying to erase it.

Annette fired twice into an approaching command elite.

The first shot broke armor.

The second stopped it.

Another reached her line.

Christer intercepted it.

Its weapon cut across his side and opened his armor.

Blood ran immediately.

Christer ignored it.

He drove his weapon beneath the creature’s ribs and forced it backward.

Daniel saw him.

“You’re hit.”

“So are you.”

Daniel had blood running beneath his own side plate.

Neither brother stopped.

Anton came through the center with his pistol overheating in his hand.

He fired.

Click.

Empty.

“Of course.”

He threw the weapon into a Seraph soldier’s face and drew his blade instead.

The corridor became a melee.

Bodies.

Armor.

Blood.

Burning insulation.

The limiter release made the Hjertstedts horribly efficient, but it did not make them invulnerable.

House casualties climbed.

Seven dead.

Then nine.

Twelve wounded.

One Seraph Hunter went down when two Husk Guard pinned him and tore into a damaged hip joint.

Another Hunter dragged him free but lost an arm at the elbow doing it.

The wounded Hunter kept fighting with the other hand.

Dennis got back up.

Slowly.

His left arm hung badly.

His shield was gone.

The Seraph commander saw him rise.

It charged.

Dennis met it with his great blade.

Steel hit black-gold alloy.

The impact shook the corridor.

Dennis was strong.

The commander was stronger.

The Seraph blade slipped beneath Dennis’s guard and punched deep into his abdomen armor.

Dennis froze.

Daniel saw the penetration.

“No!”

The commander twisted.

Dennis dropped to one knee.

Blood spilled through the armor seam.

Then the creature raised its weapon for the finishing strike.

That was when the command-well door exploded inward.

Joel came through.

Alone.

Broken.

His armor looked as though someone had tried to tear him apart piece by piece.

One shoulder plate was missing.

His shield was gone.

Blood ran from his scalp and mouth.

One arm was barely responding.

His crimson eyes were screaming red.

He raised his pistol.

Shot.

One Seraph elite dropped.

Shot.

Another.

Shot.

A Husk Guard lost its knee.

Shot.

The next target fell.

Then—

Click.

Empty.

Joel looked at the pistol.

Dropped it.

A command elite rushed him.

Joel pulled a House boarding axe from the magnetic lock at the back of his armor.

Short haft.

Heavy wedge blade.

Designed for opening emergency bulkheads when breaching tools failed.

Or people when necessary.

The Seraph elite struck.

Joel caught the weapon on the axe head, stepped inside, and buried the wedge into the creature’s chest seam.

He ripped it free.

Turned.

A Husk Guard came from his right.

Joel ducked beneath the claw and brought the axe into the back of its knee.

The leg folded.

He climbed the falling body and drove the axe into the neck joint.

Black fluid sprayed across his armor.

Another experimental soldier attacked.

Joel smashed the axe haft across its jaw, broke its support frame with the backswing, then buried the blade beneath the collar.

No wasted movement.

No shouting.

He was running on damage, limiter release, and pure decision.

The remaining Seraph troops tried to overwhelm him.

Joel erased them.

One by one.

A strike to a wrist.

A broken knee.

A blade through a seam.

An axe buried into a helmet.

He moved through the corridor like an execution machine that happened to be bleeding to death.

Anton watched him.

“Now he’s showing off.”

Annette snapped:

“He is barely standing.”

Joel heard her.

“I’m fine.”

“You are not.”

“I know.”

That silenced her for half a second.

Then Joel saw Dennis.

On one knee.

The Seraph commander standing over him.

The blade raised.

The human face beneath the broken Seraph armor was crying.

Joel moved.

The commander brought the weapon down.

Joel caught it with the boarding axe.

The impact nearly drove him to his knees.

Dennis rolled away.

“Joel.”

“Stay down.”

Dennis looked offended.

Then obeyed.

The Seraph commander turned fully toward Joel.

For the first time, the corridor seemed to empty around them.

The family understood.

This was no longer crossfire.

It was a duel.

The commander attacked first.

Joel blocked once.

Twice.

The third strike tore through his damaged shoulder plate and opened flesh beneath.

Joel grunted but did not step back.

He slammed the axe head into the commander’s faceplate.

The human face inside screamed.

The Seraph control responded with a knee strike that threw Joel against the wall.

He hit hard.

Nearly fell.

The commander came for him.

Joel stepped aside at the last possible second and buried the axe into its thigh joint.

The creature roared.

Joel ripped the weapon free.

The command blade came across horizontally.

Joel ducked.

Too slow.

The edge caught his back armor and spun him.

Anton moved forward.

Annette shouted:

“No!”

Joel recovered before anyone could intervene.

The commander lunged.

Joel let it.

He stepped inside the blade.

Took the strike across his already ruined side.

Then trapped the Seraph weapon beneath his arm.

The commander realized too late.

Joel headbutted the broken faceplate.

Once.

Twice.

The human face disappeared beneath blood and shattered material.

The Seraph tried to pull free.

Joel hooked the boarding axe behind its knee and dragged it down.

Both hit the deck.

The commander reached for Joel’s throat.

Joel caught the wrist.

Their faces were centimeters apart.

The human host surfaced again.

“Please.”

Joel froze.

Only for an instant.

Then the Seraph seized the body and tried to drive a hidden blade into Joel’s chest.

Joel trapped the arm.

His expression changed.

“I’m sorry.”

He raised the boarding axe.

The Seraph screamed.

Joel brought it down into the command core at the base of the neck.

Once.

The commander convulsed.

Still moved.

Joel struck again.

The black-gold neural housing split.

The Seraph control died.

The human body went still.

Joel remained kneeling over it.

Breathing hard.

Blood dripping from his armor.

Dennis lay nearby, still alive.

Annette stared at the dead commander.

The prisoner had been lost.

But Dennis had not.

Joel looked toward him.

“You owe me.”

Dennis managed:

“For what?”

Joel glanced at the dead Seraph commander.

“I killed it before you did.”

Dennis almost laughed despite the wound.

“That was not the competition.”

Anton immediately cut in.

“The competition was command-ship capture.”

Christer nodded.

“Correct.”

Daniel, bleeding against the wall, added:

“So technically none of you has won the bottle yet.”

Joel looked at all of them.

Then at the corridor full of dead Seraph.

“You people are impossible.”

Annette stepped toward him.

“And you are going to medical.”

Joel tried to stand.

His legs failed.

Annette caught him.

This time he did not argue.

---

## 16. House Victory

Annette held Joel upright as the limiter release collected its debt.

His vision narrowed.

The boarding axe slipped from his hand.

She kept him from falling as his remaining strength gave way.

“Joel.”

He tried to push himself upright.

“Bridge.”

“No.”

“Still contested.”
